They turn on the pin at the original's pace, two minutes of the clock a frame at thirty frames a second (an hour a second), the camera going to the face first and to the fountain that drains. A fountain is a pool in a stone kerb whose water falls by its own depth over two seconds.
- **Punching bags and stacks of tyres** (Veilstone) stand in the way like people and are drawn as cards, as the field's obstacles are: a bag of red leather `206,62,58` banded in white at top and foot and hanging from a chain, 24 by 44 texels; three tyres `58,58,72` stacked, 30 by 30. A kicked bag draws back half a tile for a second, then runs along its track at the original's speed (3.75 tiles a second) with the camera after it when it runs out of the picture; one that can't go only swings. A stack it runs into topples and is gone.
- **The dark rooms** (Hearthome) are a black fog over the whole picture, the dark of "Caves" (`8,6,12`) but never quite opaque: the original's 109 of 127 in the first room (86%), 119 in the second (94%), 91 in either once the Relic Badge is won (72%). It lifts round the player and round every trainer, each a light of their own: clear for 1.5 tiles, soft over 1.1 more, the circles lying on the ground and joining where they meet. Flash does nothing there.
- **The doors** (Hearthome) are arched doors of dark wood in a gilt frame, 28 by 50 texels, on the back wall at each way on, each with its sign in gilt over the middle: a circle, a square, a triangle, a sun, a ring, a moon, a star, a heart. The sign of the door that leads on stands a little above the floor where the original puts its clue, gilt on a dark disc 22 texels across, and like everything else it is seen only when a light comes near it. A pad between the entrance and Fantina's room is a ring of pale violet light on the floor. A bollard is dark polished stone `84,70,108` with a gilt cap, 16 by 28.
- **The shaft and its lifts** (Canalave): the room's floor is the bottom of a mine shaft, the hall's quarry stone where the lowest floor can be walked and nothing drawn where it can't (the dark of the room shows through, `20,18,24`). Each floor above is ten tiles over the last, a deck of steel grating over every tile its map lets one walk: plates `150,156,166` with a grid of slots `96,100,112` every four texels and a light edge `196,202,212` at the top and left, six texels thick, with its edge faces shown wherever the deck ends. A floor shows only once the viewer has risen a tile toward it, as the original's props do: from the ground the shaft is open to the dark above. A lift between floors (and the two shafts from the bottom to the top) is a red plate `196,58,50` with a band of yellow `236,196,60` and black hazard stripes at its edge; a cart that runs along a floor is a yellow plate with two dark arrows pointing the ways it runs, on a pair of dark rails `62,64,74` along its whole track. A platform's top stands two texels over its floor, six deep, and it moves with the player on it at the original's speed (fifteen tiles a second); where it has gone from a floor, its place there is a hole.
- **The ice and its snowballs** (Snowpoint): the floor is the field's ice ("Ground": `150,200,236`, its glints) laid over the original's bowl as relief, ring below ring, and it is the ground under everything there, as a cave's floor is under its rock: no lawn shows where rounded corners meet. Each slope of ice is streaked down its fall, five light lines `206,232,250` a texel wide and six to twelve long, each with a line of blue shade `124,176,222` beside it, so a ramp reads as a ramp. Where the original's ground stops a slide (a patch here and there, the steps down the middle of each slope) it is the field's snow, and Candice's dais and the way in are the hall's tiles. A snowball stands in the way like a person and is drawn as a card 30 by 30 of the scenery, not a sprite: lit as the ice it stands on, it comes out about as bright as the snow beside it, where a sprite's warmer light greyed it to a rock. It is a smooth ball of packed snow lit from the upper left (`204,230,255`, then `186,210,244`), shaded blue toward the lower right (`148,182,232`) and deepest where it sits on the ice (`110,148,218`), with a few sparkles on its lit side and a soft blue outline, below white. A slide with speed bursts it ("Life": obstacles giving way) and goes on through where it stood. The slide keeps a run's pace on flat ice and quickens as it gathers speed down the slopes, by the original's frames a tile (four, three, then two); a slip back down a slope goes at a quarter of the run's pace, the player still facing up it and not walking.
- **The gears and their walkways** (Sunyshore): the room is a power station's floor over the dark. Its floors are the hall's steel plate laid as relief at the original's heights (one, four and seven tiles), and they stand on faces of their own: a pale steel edge `206,212,226`, a band of hazard stripes in yellow `236,196,60` and black `40,40,48`, then panels of dark steel `74,82,104` with rivets `140,148,168`, sixteen rows to a panel. Under the gears there is nothing (the dark of the room, `20,18,24`), the floors' faces running down into it to two tiles under the room's lowest floor, the same depth wherever a walkway meets a floor, so every floor reads as a deck. A flat gear is a toothed wheel lying on its hub, 86 texels across, gold `228,184,58` (light `250,220,118`, shade `164,122,36`) or, for the original's "alt" gears, steel blue `110,150,214` (light `164,198,244`, shade `66,96,160`), with a dark steel boss `60,66,86` on its hub; its arms are walkways 26 texels wide reaching two tiles past the hub, decks of pale steel `186,194,210` in plates eight texels long with a yellow line along each side, six texels thick, their tops level with the floor. A gear on edge is a bar of the same deck five tiles long and a tile thick, its two ends in hazard stripes, turning on a dark axle with a toothed drum of the gear's colour at each end; it is a bridge while it lies flat and a striped post standing up out of the dark while it doesn't. All of a room's gears turn together at the original's pace (5.625 degrees a frame at thirty frames a second: a quarter turn in about half a second), each its own way, the field waiting. A button on a hub is a steel rim `176,184,196` with a cap of yellow `248,214,72` and a black arrow round it showing the way the gear under it turns (counter-clockwise, clockwise, or two arrows for a half turn), standing two texels over the hub and never turning with it. A doorway on to the next room is a steel door `120,128,150` in a yellow frame on the back wall.
- **Don't**: bake a puzzle's moving part into the floor, light a dark room fully, or let a bag or a hand move by itself while the player can act: the field waits until each has stopped.

### The League's rooms

The rooms behind the League's door (plan 01 · M9, part 2d) are halls as the Gyms are ("Gyms", above: the original's plan, a floor baked from its tiles, hall tiles where they are plain floor, what moves drawn apart by `GymPieces`), in one family: gold on every door, lift and rail, and a look to each room.

- **The four rooms** take theirs from the type of their master (`arenaType`), each a place more than a hall. *Aaron's garden*: lawn with flowers and a path of earth from door to door, a hedge and trees in tubs where the original's room is closed; hall tiles of mossy stone `150,176,120` / `138,164,110`; walls leaf green `196,222,170` over a wainscot of bark `126,98,64`. *Bertha's canyon*: a floor of sand between shelves of rock two tiles high, drawn as the field's relief with its rock faces, boulders on the shelves and along the front row, where the shelves stop so the front wall's kerb stands on the floor; tiles of sandstone `214,186,140` / `202,172,126`; walls clay `222,196,150` over terracotta `170,110,70`. *Flint's causeway*: a floor of basalt `92,74,78` / `80,64,70` seven tiles wide over a bed of lava a tile below (the room's tiles there are void, as under the Sunyshore Gym's gears); walls deep red `122,50,46` with a motif and a rail of brass `226,150,70` over charcoal `70,58,64`. *Lucian's study*: tiles of violet `170,140,206` / `156,126,194` with four columns; walls lilac `206,186,232` over plum `110,76,150`.
- **The rest have no type** and take a look of their own (`hall` in the map file). *The League's own stone* (the passages): tiles of slate blue `128,140,172` / `116,128,160`; walls navy `58,70,112` with a motif and a rail of gold `226,190,96` over a wainscot `84,96,138`. *The Champion's room*: tiles of marble `228,226,216` / `212,210,200`, never whiter, so the daylight doesn't bloom them blank; walls ivory `236,230,212` with gold `222,190,104` over a wainscot of pale gold `206,178,110`. *The Hall of Fame* and the hallway to it: tiles of dark polished stone `58,60,84` / `50,52,76` that mirror whoever stands on them, as a puddle does ("Puddles"); walls midnight `40,44,74` with gold `214,180,92` over deep violet `70,60,96`.
- **The faces the floors stand on** (a shaft's wall, the causeway's sides, the wall under the Champion's gallery) are courses of dressed stone twelve rows high, their joints half a block along from course to course, each block lit along its top and left and shaded at its foot and right, under a cap with a pale edge and a band of gold `226,190,96`: slate `96,104,134` in the passages, basalt `70,56,62` over the lava, sandstone `196,160,112` under the walls of Bertha's room, marble `196,190,174` in the Champion's room.
- **Lava** is a sheet over the whole bed, 0.45 of a tile under the causeway: molten orange `226,96,36` with pools of a deeper red `200,70,30`, veins of `250,170,60` that brighten and fade and a few sparks of `255,214,110`, and plates of dark crust `120,40,30` (edge `84,28,26`), each an irregular slab lit along its upper edge by the glow under it, that rock a texel or two to and fro: four frames at three a second, in a pattern two tiles across so no plate is seen twice in a row. Nothing of it is white: the glow is the bloom's.
- **Doors.** A way on is a door in the back wall, 30 by 56 texels in a frame of gold `226,190,96` (light `250,226,150`, shade `170,130,60`) with a round head: open, the dark of the room beyond `20,18,28` with two steps of shade at its jambs; shut (a room of the Four until its master is beaten), two leaves of dark bronze `70,78,116` in sunken panels, studded with gold, with a bar across them. The door behind the challenger is in the front wall, which is cut away to its kerb like every room's: shut, it is a gate of the same bronze as high as two kerbs, capped in gold, across the gap it closed; it comes down with the original's clank and is not there before. The door from the Pokémon Center is in a block of the room's own wall that stands two tiles out from the back wall at its full height, a pilaster of gold at each side and the door between them.
- **The lifts** are a platform three tiles by two: a slab eight texels thick whose top stands two over its floor, bronze plates `92,100,140` in a rim of gold with a gold ring and a ball's line at its middle, hazard chevrons of gold along its north and south edges. It rises at the original's pace (3.75 tiles a second) with the player on it, the field waiting, along a shaft whose wall is the face above; a pair of gold rails runs up that wall from the platform's corners to the floor above.
- **Carpets** lie on the floor from a way in to where it leads, a texel above it: deep blue `48,70,150` edged with gold in the Champion's room, crimson `170,44,56` edged with gold in the hallway and the Hall of Fame, a border three texels in and a small diamond every tile.
- **The Champion's piers** are four blocks of marble two tiles by three and 76 texels high, panelled, with a band of gold at head and foot. **The Hall of Fame's machine** stands against the back wall: a console of dark steel `60,66,96` three tiles wide and 20 texels high edged in gold, six sockets for Poké Balls in a ring on its top, and behind it a screen 60 by 44 in a gold frame, lit blue `96,170,236` with a ball's outline. As the Champion's team is entered (plan 02 · S14) a Poké Ball stands in each socket for each of the team, laid one after another: the field's own ball (red `218,62,58` over white `240,240,236`, a band and a button of `58,52,72`, outlined), ten texels across on a card of twelve, upright on the console's top. Statues on plinths line the hallway and the Hall where the original's pillars stand.
- **Don't**: light a room of the League white, or let a door move while the player can act: the door shuts and opens inside its script, and the lift holds the field until it has stopped.

### Characters

- **Sculpted with the SDF kit** (G6): each character is one smooth body of signed-distance shapes (`SdfModel`: ellipsoids, round cones, round boxes, tori, cylinders) joined with smooth unions, carved with cuts and coloured with paint, meshed by surface nets into a single skinned mesh (`SdfMesher`, cells of 1/112 unit, 34,000 to 42,000 vertices) and cached on disk (`SdfCache`). The same model is the 3D trainer in battle and the source of the field sprites (`CharacterModels`).
- **Proportions**: chibi. Children (the player, the rival, youngsters, lasses) are about 1.2 units tall with a head of radius 0.27, nearly half their height; adults have a longer body and a slightly smaller head (radius 0.25), then `CharacterStyle.Height` sizes each type. The player reads at about 130 px tall on screen.
- **Parts**: mitten hands with a thumb; shoes with a sole in their own colour (white under red or blue shoes); sleeves ending in a cuff, or short sleeves at two thirds of the upper arm; a collar or a scarf; stripes painted across the shirt (light on a coloured shirt, the accent colour on a white one); skirts and coat tails on a bone of their own so they swing; the player's backpack with straps and a buckle; a beard (`CharacterStyle.Beard`, plan 11 · C6) in the hair's colour, a full mass round the chin and jaw below the mouth with a sideburn up to each ear, never over the mouth or the cheeks, so the face's patch keeps the eyes and the mouth; glasses (`CharacterStyle.Glasses`, the frame's colour) are no shape but part of the face, drawn by both faces below. Colour edges are painted crisp (waistline, sleeves, soles), never blended.
- **Hair is sculpted in locks**: tapering tubes laid along the skull from the crown (from the hairline, for swept hair) to pointed tips, over a snug cap, with small blends so grooves show between them; bangs end just above the eyes; long hair falls over a smooth mass behind and swings; spiky hair stands in tufts. Under a beret or cap the locks start below its edge, so no hair shows through the hat, and they lie close enough at the back that the hat's band doesn't show between them. A hat sits on the hair, never in it: the nurse's cap stands on top of the hair, and a cap's peak comes out of the crown's front edge with the bangs below it. White hair is a shaded grey-white (`190,194,210`), never pure white. A bow (`Headwear.Bow`) sits on the hair at the top of the back of the head: two loops 0.8 of the head's radius across, tilted up and out, round a knot, with two short tails falling behind, in its colour with the knot in its band's. A wrestler's mask (`Headwear.Mask`) is a hood over the crown, the sides and the back down to the jaw, the face left bare, with a crest in its band's colour from the brow to the nape; no lock of hair grows under it. A fedora (`Headwear.Fedora`) is a cap's crown taller and rounder, a brim all round at the crown's foot (1.45 of the head's radius across the sides, 1.35 to the front, so the eyes still show from the field's camera) and a band in its band's colour just above the brim; the locks start below its edge as under a cap. Four cuts came with the second half's cast (plan 11 · C6): an afro (`HairCut.Afro`), a round mass of hair over the crown, the back and the sides, its front stopping at the brow, with a crown of small round curls on its surface, no bangs and never a lock; a bun (`HairCut.Bun`), the swept cut's locks combed back from the hairline into a round knot high on the back of the head; braids (`HairCut.Braids`), the short cut with its bangs and two plaits from under the ears down over the front of the shoulders to the chest, beads one after another, each tied at its end in the band's colour (`hatBand`, which a look without a hat may set) with a short tuft below; bunches (`HairCut.Bunches`), the short cut with its bangs and two thick tails tied high at the sides of the head, standing out and curling down to the jaw and swinging with the hair, tied in the band's colour. Every cut stays inside the sprite's frame: no wider than 0.82 of a unit and no higher than 1.5.
- **The named cast** (plan 11 · C5, C6): someone the story names is a look of their own under the original's object name (`roark`, `mira`, `crasher_wake`), or under their overlay's type where the original has no object for them (`bebe`, `poketch_president`), and is known at a glance by hair, hat and colours, never by a face alone: Cheryl's long chestnut hair and a long green skirt under a peach blouse; Mira, a small girl with long pale-gold hair under a big orange bow and a pink dress; Bebe's short dark hair and an orange cardigan over a cream top; Crasher Wake, the tallest, in a blue mask with a yellow crest, bare-chested over blue trousers and yellow boots; the Pokétch Company's president, a man with swept grey hair and a moustache in a navy suit with a red tie. The second half's (C6): Commander Saturn's spiky steel-blue hair over the team's pale uniform and dark trousers; Riley in a navy fedora and a long navy coat over a blue shirt; Byron, big and bearded, spiky brown hair and a steel-blue work shirt over brown trousers; Candice's dark braids tied in white over a green sweater and a navy skirt; Volkner's spiky pale-gold hair and a navy jacket with a yellow collar; Jasmine's brown bunches tied in orange and a white dress with a teal collar; Marley, small and pale, short black hair and a dark violet dress; Buck, tanned, spiky brown hair, a black vest with an orange collar and grey trousers; Aaron's spiky teal hair and a black-green coat with a yellow collar, a bug's colours; Bertha, short, silver hair in a bun and an ochre cardigan over a brown skirt; Flint's red afro over a black vest with an orange collar; Lucian's long purple hair, glasses and a burgundy coat; Palmer, the tallest of them, his son's spiky gold hair and orange over a green coat and cream trousers; Teala, the Pokémon Centers' attendant upstairs and the Battle Tower's, brown hair in a bun and a teal jacket and skirt; Professor Oak's spiky grey hair and a white lab coat over a red shirt and khaki trousers; Thorton, short, mauve-grey hair, glasses and a pale grey coat with an orange collar; Argenta's silver-lavender bun and a long purple coat with gold; Darach's swept auburn hair and a butler's black tailcoat with a white cravat; Dahlia's dark afro and a red dress with gold; Lady Caitlin's long pale-gold hair and a long pink gown. A Leader, a member of the Elite Four, a Commander and a Frontier Brain stand in battle as their own look.
- **Faces in battle**: a smooth texture (128 px, anti-aliased, cut out round the features) laid over the front of the head: dark rim, an iris lighter toward the bottom, a pupil, two white highlights (upper left, lower right), a heavier upper lid, lash flicks for long lashes, brows only where the bangs leave the brow bare, a small mouth (under the moustache, if any), and glasses where the look has them: a round rim in the frame's colour round each eye, a bridge between them and a short temple outward, there with the eyes shut too. Blush is painted on the cheeks of the model itself. Expressions: neutral, happy, surprised, sad, angry, each also with the eyes shut for blinking.
- **Pixel faces on sprites**: a smooth face shrunk to sprite size turns to mush, so each baked frame gets a face stamped where the point between the eyes lands: eyes two texels wide and three tall (lash row, then a white highlight on the upper left beside the iris, then iris), three texels apart; a mouth of one to three texels two rows below; one eye in profile, none from behind. A blink is a two-texel line. Glasses are a one-texel rim round each eye (four texels wide, five tall) with the bridge between them on the eyes' top row, or round the one eye in profile with two texels of temple behind it; the eyes are stamped over them.
- **Light on faces**: the normals of the front of the head are bent mostly upward (toward `0, 0.75, 0.66`), so a face lights evenly under any light from above and the edge of the shade never splits it, in profile too.
- **Light on hair**: the hair's normals are bent most of the way (70 %) toward those of the smooth mass it fills: a sphere round the head, stretched into a capsule down the back for long hair. The shade's edge and the sheen then run cleanly across the head instead of breaking into a blotch on every lock; the locks still show in the silhouette, the outline and the grooves between them.
- **Animation** (`CharacterAnimation`, 19 bones): idle breathing with the arms lowered from the sculpted A-pose; walk and run cycles in which the hips drop as the legs spread, so a foot stays on the ground, with arms swinging against the legs (the run leans forward with the elbows bent); the ledge hop; emotes (wave, surprised, nod, cheer, and since plan 11 · C11 bow, shrug, point and laugh). A bow bends the body forward from the waist with the head down and the arms hanging; a shrug lifts both shoulders, brings the forearms out with the hands open and tilts the head; a point holds the right arm straight out ahead at the shoulder's height, the head turned a little after it; a laugh tips the head back and shakes the shoulders. Every emote eases in from the idle and ends at it. A jump on the spot is the ledge hop's clip played where the person stands, lifted eight texels at its height. What the field plays on cue is a script's (`act`, `mood`; `docs/scripts.md`): a gesture is an emote played over the idle as many times as asked, a mood the face held until the script changes it or the place is come to again; the start under a trainer's "!" still wins over both. Hair, bag and skirt swing a beat behind the body. Raised hands stay beside the big head, never behind it ("\o/", not straight up).
- **Sprites** (`CharacterSprites`): 40×58 texels, orthographic, seen from 24° above, studio light from the upper left, the pixel face stamped on, then a one-texel outline tinted from the neighbouring colour (`PixelCanvas.OutlinePass`). Frames for each of the four facings: 2 idle breaths, 8 walk, 8 run, 3 hop (the jump on the spot shows them too) and 6 per emote (strips `wave`, `surprised`, `nod`, `cheer`, `bow`, `shrug`, `point` and `laugh`; the throw is drawn in battle only); blinking and expressions are frames of their own, so a mood is a face on any strip. Drawn as upright cards lit by the scene (sky × 0.62 + sun × 0.78), casting real shadows, with a soft contact blob under the feet. A trainer who spots the player starts (the surprised emote) under the "!".
- **Hand-drawn overrides**: a 40×58 PNG at `overrides/sprites/<TYPE>/<facing>_<strip>_<frame>[_blink][_<expression>].png` next to the game (for example `PLAYER/down_walk_3.png`) replaces that baked frame.
- **Pokémon in the field** (plan 10 · F1, `FieldSprites`): baked from the Pokémon's own 3D model as the people's sprites are (orthographic, 24° above, the same studio light, a one-texel outline from `OutlinePass`), at the same 32 texels to the tile, so sizes compare: a Pokémon stands `0.3 + 0.75 ×` its height in metres tiles tall (from 0.4 to 1.9; the player is about 1.7), a Machop a little over half the player and a Steelix taller. The card is square, 32 texels under 0.7 tiles, 48 to 1.6, 64 above (`FieldSprites.ClassOf`), and the model is shrunk until it fits it from every side. Its lowest point stands on the card's bottom row but one and a half, which is the ground. Four facings with two idle frames each (breathed out and in on the idle's beat); no blink, no stamped face (its eyes are the model's). Drawn like the people: an upright card lit and casting, a contact blob three quarters of a person's. While a sprite isn't baked yet a Poké Ball stands in its place. A Pokémon of the map that moves about hops two texels on its steps and shows its two frames in turn, the second while it is off the ground, a hop to each tile, until it has walk frames (plan 10 · F2). A Pokémon of the team out of its ball (plan 06 · R16c: in Amity Square, a step behind the player) is a Pokémon of the map like any other: the same card, the same blob, the same hop, and it keeps to the tile the player has just left, so it is never drawn over the player but where the player walks north with it behind, when the top of a tall one crosses the player's feet as anyone's would who stood there. On the water the player rides the Pokémon whose Surf it is: its sprite from the side or the back the player faces, a fifth of it under the water, the rider sitting a little over halfway up what shows; the round swimmer stands in while the sprite bakes. A square PNG of 32, 48 or 64 at `overrides/sprites/pokemon/<NAME>/<facing>_idle_<frame>.png` replaces a baked frame (both frames of a facing are needed).

- **Berry patches** (plan 06 · R14a, `BerryArt`): a patch of soft soil is a card 32 texels wide and 40 tall standing on its tile, drawn like the field's things (`DrawThings`). Its foot is a mound of loam 28 texels across and 10 high, lit from the upper left in three tones with seven crumbs of darker earth; mulch darkens it by a step and its crumbs turn to pale straw. What grows in it goes stage by stage: a seed showing in the top of the mound, a sprout of two leaves, a stem of four, a bush of six leaves on three stems with three flowers (pale petals round a yellow eye) and the same bush with three berries five texels across, each lit on one side, shaded on the other and hung from a dark stalk. A berry's colour is its strongest flavour's, as a Poffin's is: spicy red, dry blue, sweet pink, bitter green, sour yellow. The original's tile of soil and the plants' sprites are not copied: the stages and the colours are its numbers; the art is ours.

### Life

What moves in the field (G9). All of it is pixel art in motion: whole texels, a few frames, flat shades, and it fades in two or three steps of alpha, never smoothly. The one exception is mist (fog, and the haze of a sandstorm or a blizzard), which is soft like the depth-of-field blur it lies in.

- **Wind**: everything that sways (grass, flowers, leaves) sways more as a gust passes. Gusts cross the map from the west, one about every twelve seconds, swelling the sway from three quarters of its usual size to a third more and leaning tips a little eastward. Weather raises it: a little in hail, half as much again in rain, more in heavy rain, and twice in a thunderstorm, heavy snow, a blizzard or a sandstorm.
- **Footprints**: on the ground that keeps them in Platinum (its sand, and snow of every depth) each step leaves one shoe print, 6 by 11 texels, left and right alternately, in a darker shade of the ground (`196,170,118` on sand, `140,160,206` on snow). They stay three seconds, then fade in two steps over three more.
- **Dust**: a run raises one puff behind each step, a little to the side of the foot that made it, and a hop lands in two: a round cloud in three frames (6, 10 and 12 texels across) over 0.36 s, in the colour of the ground it comes from (path `214,196,150`, sand `232,218,170`, dirt `170,134,98`, snow `214,226,244`, anything else `200,196,190`), rising three texels as it goes.
- **Tall grass**: stepping in throws up four leaf bits (6 by 4 texels, lighter than the grass so they show against it: `168,226,112` and `110,196,92`, with a dark rim), two to each side: the inner pair fly up 18 texels, the outer pair lower and wider, each turning over at the top of its arc, and they are down again in 0.5 s.
- **Water**: a swimmer leaves a ring on the water behind every stroke (three frames, 8 to 14 texels across, `210,236,255`, gone in 0.7 s); a puddle gives a small ring and two drops, water ankle deep a ring alone, and the water someone has just ridden out onto a wide ring and four drops that fly higher.
- **Waterfalls** fall: the sheet's streaks move down four texels eight times a second.
- **Fountains and turbines**: a fountain plays in four frames at six a second (the rings in its basin travel outward, the jet's top bobs a texel, drops fall away from it in an arc); a wind turbine's three blades turn in four frames of thirty degrees at six a second, one full turn in two seconds. Like the waterfall, they are redrawn in place, frame by frame, never slid.
- **Weather** is in the air, on the ground and in the light. What falls is drawn over the scene in the picture's own pixels (3 px blocks at 1080p) and is blown the way the wind goes, eastward. Mist is a soft cloud texture in two layers that drift at different paces. Under any of them part of the sun is lost, and half of what level ground loses by it comes down from the whole sky instead, so shadows grow faint before the picture grows dark:

| Weather | In the air | On the ground | Sun | Fog |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cloudy | nothing | | 0.45 | a little |
| Rain | 220 slanting streaks of three blocks, `176,200,236` | 360 drops a second land in view: a fleck `236,242,255` on the ground, and on water one in three leaves a small ring | 0.3, colours a fifth duller | a little |
| Heavy rain | 380 streaks of four blocks | 720 drops a second | 0.22, colours a fifth duller | some |
| Thunderstorm | as heavy rain; lightning whitens the picture twice within a quarter of a second, once in every eight seconds at a moment of its own (three to thirteen seconds apart) | 720 drops a second | 0.2, colours a fifth duller | some |
| Snow | 140 flakes of 3 and 6 px, white, swaying as they fall | | 0.6 | a little |
| Heavy snow | 320 flakes of 3 to 9 px driven hard, each with a short tail; a thin white haze | | 0.35 | near and pale |
| Blizzard | 460 such flakes driven nearly level; two layers of white haze | | 0.25 | near and pale |
| Hail | 180 pellets of 6 px, `224,238,255`, falling fast and steep | 180 pellets a second land with a fleck | 0.4 | a little |
| Fog | two layers of pale mist `232,236,244` | | 0.5 | hides what is ten tiles off |
| Sandstorm | 240 streaks of sand `226,198,140` driven hard; two layers of sand haze | | 0.55 | near and sand-coloured |
| Ash | 110 grey flakes of 3 and 6 px falling slowly | | 0.75 | a little |

These are the kinds Platinum's map headers name. Five places (the south of Route 212, Route 213, Route 216, Acuity Lakefront and Snowpoint City) take theirs from a calendar in Platinum; until that calendar is imported with their areas they have the weather they have most days.

- **Doors** open: as someone steps up to a door that leads somewhere, it opens in two frames over 0.2 s (a wooden door swings in against its frame; glass doors slide apart) on a dark room `40,34,48`, and it shuts again 0.4 s after someone has stepped out of it.
- **Obstacles giving way** (plan 02 · S2): a tree that is cut down is gone in a burst of six leaf bits in the tall grass's two greens, thrown higher and wider than the grass's, and a puff of dust; a rock that is smashed in a cloud of three puffs of its own brown `168,128,92` and six chips in its three browns flung out low; a snowball of the Snowpoint Gym that a slide bursts in three puffs of snow `244,248,255` and six lumps in its white `240,246,255` and blue `176,196,230` thrown up and out to both sides. All take the leaves' half second. A boulder pushed with Strength slides a whole tile in the original's slow walk (16 frames at 30 a second, 0.53 s) while the player walks on the spot behind it, leaning in.
- **Wild Pokémon stirring** (plan 06 · R13): a patch of grass the Poké Radar set shaking parts as round someone's feet, from a point that jumps from side to side twelve times a second (seven texels either way for a hard shake, four for a soft one), and every 0.6 s throws up the tall grass's leaf bits, two for a soft shake and four for a hard one, with two drops of sparkle `255,246,196` over a shiny one. A honey tree that Pokémon have come to sheds leaves from its crown every 0.6 s, two, four or six by how hard it shakes.
- **Poison in the field**: as poison bites, the field (not the interface over it) is washed in `150,60,190` for 0.4 s in two steps of alpha, 110 then 55.
- **Flash and Defog**: Flash lifts a cave's dark at once (the circle round the player gives way to the cave's own light, under the same fade the dark came down with); Defog clears the fog's two layers and the light goes back to the clear day's.
- **Emote bubbles**: a white bubble 20 by 22 texels with a tail, over the head, popping up over three frames: `!` in red, `?` in blue, three dots, a note, a heart, `Zz`, a drop of sweat. It stays 0.9 s unless told otherwise.
- **The camera** follows the player's height smoothly (stairs, bridges, a hop) and can be sent to look at another spot and back, easing in and out over the time it is given (for the story's scenes).
- **Into battle**: the field flashes white twice in a quarter of a second, then closes. Which way depends, as in Platinum, on who is met and on whether their first Pokémon is of a higher level than the player's first: a pinwheel of eight dark blades for a wild Pokémon, and the picture breaking into shards from the middle outward for a stronger one; shutters from both sides for a trainer, crossed with shutters from above and below for a stronger one. The battle opens through an iris for a wild Pokémon and through the same shape in reverse for the others. Closing takes 0.9 s and opening 0.5 s; the dark is `14,14,22`, never pure black. Those four are the ways of every ground but water and a cave's floor; the ground the battle is fought on, two at once, the Battle Frontier and Team Galactic have ways of their own (plan 09 · L13), below, and the Gym Leaders, the Elite Four and the Champion are met on a band with their portrait (plan 09 · L15), below those. The legendary and the mythical Pokémon are met with a camera of their own (plan 09 · L14), below the bands. (The diamonds growing from a grid that stood in for a Gym Leader's way since plan 04 · G9 went with the bands: no way in of the original's is theirs.)

  **By place and by whom** (plan 09 · L13; `SceneTransition.ForBattle` reads who is met as the original's `EncEffects_GetEffectPair` and `CutInEffects_ForBattle` do, its quirks kept). The twelve ways keep the two flashes, the dark and the half second of opening, and each takes about as long as the original's, counted from its frames. After the flashes some have a *lead*, in which nothing is dark yet; then the dark closes. What rides over the picture (a Poké Ball, a mark) is clean vector shapes in flat shades, like the dark itself, and is seen only as the field closes: the battle opens on the dark's own shape in reverse, or through the iris after a wild Pokémon. The three ways in which several things come one after another (three balls, six shards) close at an even pace; the others with the ease of the first four.

  | Way in | Lead | How the dark closes | Length (lead, closing) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | A wild Pokémon on water | the picture wavers (the scene effects' ripple, a texel further every tenth of a second up to four, two waves a second) | still wavering, the picture sinks: the dark rises from its foot, its edge a wave 320 units long and 18 high, in blocks 6 units wide and 3 tall | 1.2 s (0.35, 0.6) |
  | A stronger one on water | the same, to six texels and three waves a second | the dark comes from the foot and from the head at once, two such edges meeting in the middle | 1.2 s (0.35, 0.6) |
  | A wild Pokémon in a cave | none | the dark closes in a ring on the player, wherever they stand on the picture | 1.0 s (0, 0.75) |
  | A stronger one in a cave | none | the same ring with a toothed edge, twenty-four teeth a fifth of its radius deep: a mouth of rock shutting | 1.0 s (0, 0.75) |
  | A trainer on water | the picture wavers, and a Poké Ball spins in at its middle, growing from nothing to 96 units across in one full turn | the dark wells out from under the ball to the corners, a disc whose rim waves in twelve lobes a twentieth of its radius deep; the ball turns on until all is dark | 1.2 s (0.4, 0.55) |
  | A stronger trainer on water | the picture wavers, to six texels | three balls rise from the foot one after another, at a sixth, five sixths and the half of the width, each turning once and drawing its third of the picture dark up behind it | 1.35 s (0.2, 0.9) |
  | A trainer in a cave | a ball falls from above the picture to its middle in one turn, growing from 24 units across to 96 | the dark shuts on it from above and below, two lids curved like the ball's halves; the ball shrinks away as they meet | 1.3 s (0.45, 0.6) |
  | A stronger trainer in a cave | none | three balls fall from above one after another, at the half, thirteen sixteenths and three sixteenths of the width, each tearing a strip of dark down the picture behind it, 96 units wide with teeth 12 deep; then the strips widen until they meet | 1.45 s (0, 1.2) |
  | Two at once (a double battle, a tag battle, two wild Pokémon) | none | four pieces slide in from the corners, a quarter of the picture each, leaving a cross that thins to nothing | 1.05 s (0, 0.8) |
  | The Battle Frontier (its battles, a Frontier Brain anywhere, a link battle) | the Frontier's mark comes up at the middle in three steps of alpha (90, 170, 255): a ring 300 units across in the print's gold `232,180,60` with a five-pointed star in it, both rimmed in the dark so they read on a pale floor | a five-bladed iris closes on the mark, turning a fifth of a turn, as the mark shrinks to nothing | 1.25 s (0.45, 0.55) |
  | A Team Galactic grunt | none | six shards of the Galactic mark fly in one after another from the picture's rim to its middle, each turning one to three turns and shrinking from three times its size to its own; behind each, its sixth of the picture goes dark from the rim inward; the mark, 300 units across, stands whole on the dark for the last sixth of the closing | 2.0 s (0, 1.75) |
  | A Commander or the boss | the Galactic mark rises out of the picture's middle, growing from nothing to 420 units across in half a second, as the picture dims under a veil of the dark in three steps (alpha 70, 130, 180), and stands | the mark's dark core grows: a lozenge that swallows the mark and then the picture | 2.1 s (1.0, 0.85) |

  The Poké Ball is our own: red `236,64,56` over white `250,250,252`, a band and a rim of the dark, a white button ringed in the dark.

  **Bands** (plan 09 · L15; `SceneTransition.BandAt` says what a band shows at a moment and `SceneTransition.Bands` whose colours it wears). A Gym Leader, one of the Elite Four and the Champion are met on a band across the picture with their portrait, after the beats of the original's and with nothing of its art: the banner, the portrait, the mark and the colours are ours. A band keeps the two flashes and the half second of opening, takes about as long as the original's, counted from its frames, and closes to **white** (`250,250,252`, never pure white), as the original's does (plan 09, decision 5).

  - **The band** is 300 units tall across the middle of the picture, in its base colour, with a 6-unit line of its light colour inside its head and its foot and a 4-unit line of its deep colour outside them. Speed lines run in it: nine rows 30 units apart, each of dashes 120 to 400 units long and 6 to 14 thick in the deep colour or the light, each row at its own pace (0.6 to 1.4 of 1500 units a second) and placed in whole units. They run away from whoever the portrait before them faces: to the right in a Leader's band, and outward from the middle in the League's.
  - **A card** stands in the band behind each portrait: a panel of the band's light colour with slanted sides (skew 0.2), 380 units wide, between the band's lines, its head leaning away from the middle of the picture (the League's two open like a V round the mark). A Gym Leader wears their Gym's colours, which are the band's (Gardenia's jacket is the band's green, Bertha's cardigan its ochre): before the card a bust never stands on its own colours, the ink of its outline reads all round it, and a silhouette is at its darkest. The card is part of the band: the League's two ride their halves in, and a Leader's is there once the wipe has passed it.
  - **A portrait** is a bust of the person's own rig, the look the battle itself draws them in (the player's is their own, outfit and all), from the chest to over the hair or the hat, with the set face of the battle's kind (the angry one), turned 20° toward the middle of the picture and lit from the front, since the studio's high light leaves a band of shade under the jaw that reads as a beard this close. It is 440 units square, rendered once at 880 pixels, and stands with its foot on the band's foot, so the crown of the head breaks the band's upper edge. A silhouette is the picture's shape in one flat colour, `32,32,40`; lit, it is the picture as rendered. Until the rig is there a plain bust in the silhouette's colour stands in: a band never waits for a model.
  - **The mark** between is "VS" in Nunito Black, 150 units, white over a drop of the band's deep colour 8 units down and to the right. It stamps in as the original's does: three echoes in the band's light colour a tenth of a second apart, each shrinking from twice its size to its own in a fifth of a second (alpha 90, 130, 170), then the mark itself.
  - **The name** is the trainer's own in 52 Black, white, on a slanted plate (skew 0.2, 76 tall, radius 12) of the band's deep colour, with their class in capitals beside it in 24 ExtraBold. A Leader's plate stands to the left of the portrait and low in the band, its end tucked in behind the bust, with the class over its head end in the band's deep colour; the League's hangs from the band's foot under their portrait, with the class under it in the band's light colour.
  - **The light**: a white flash over the whole picture rises in three steps (0.25, 0.5, 0.75) over a tenth of a second, the silhouettes become the lit portraits at its height, and it falls back in steps. The picture round the band lies under the dark from then on (alpha 200; 130 round the League's band while its portraits are silhouettes).
  - **The white** closes as a clean shape, as the dark does: for a Leader a bar that widens from the band's middle row to the head and the foot of the picture; for the League a slash along the picture's rising diagonal, from the lower left corner to the upper right, that widens to the other two as the portraits fly apart along the falling one. The battle opens on the same shape in reverse, out of the white: no frame between the two is dark.

  | Beat (seconds after the flashes) | A Gym Leader's band | The Elite Four's and the Champion's |
  |---|---|---|
  | The band opens | 0 to 0.2: it wipes in from the right behind a toothed edge (teeth 50 units tall and 120 deep) | 0 to 0.2: two halves slide in from the left and the right, the player's portrait on the one and theirs on the other, both silhouettes, as the picture round them dims |
  | The mark | 0.55 to 1.05, at 0.28 of the width | 0.2 to 0.7, at the middle, where the halves have met |
  | The portraits | 1.1 to 1.25: theirs slides in from the right as a silhouette, to 0.78 of the width | with the halves: the player's at 0.22 of the width, theirs at 0.78 |
  | The name | with the light | from 0.2 |
  | The light | the flash 1.6 to 1.8, lit at 1.7 | the flash 1.0 to 1.3, lit at 1.1 |
  | Held | to 2.7 | to 1.55; then to 2.6 the portraits tremble, thrown apart and at one another in turn every fifteenth of a second and further each time, to 15 units |
  | The white | 2.7 to 3.2 | 2.6 to 2.9, the name gone and the portraits flying apart, the player's up and to the left, theirs down and to the right |
  | Length (lead, closing), flashes included | 3.45 s (2.7, 0.5) | 3.15 s (2.6, 0.3) |

  The card behind a portrait is the band's light colour, but for the two whose hair is that colour: Volkner's is the pale blue of a spark, `196,224,252`, and Cynthia's a moonlit blue, `132,140,196`, between her hair's gold and her coat's night. A band's colours are chosen with its person on it (the harness's `gyms` and `league` shoot all thirteen, `g_band_<name>` and `lg_band_<name>`), never from the table alone.

  A Leader or a League member the table doesn't name (another region's, before its band is written) wears a plain band of steel grey (`120,132,152`, `64,72,92`, `204,212,224`).

  **The legendary's and the mythical's** (plan 09 · L14; `SceneTransition.LensAt` says what the camera does at a moment). Dialga, Palkia, Uxie, Azelf and Arceus are met the legendary's way; Shaymin, Giratina, Regigigas, Heatran, Darkrai, Rotom and the three Regis (but in Pal Park) the mythical's; by species, whatever the form, and before the check for two at once, as the original's rows stand. Mesprit, Cresselia and the three birds have rows of their own in the original for their music and are met as anyone is, by place, a double included. Both ways are the field's own camera moving, through the camera kit's rig over whatever lens the place has, and then a white that closes as a clean shape, as a band's does: they keep the two flashes and the half second of opening, and the battle opens on the same shape in reverse, out of the white. The numbers are the original's (`EncounterEffect_Legendary` and `EncounterEffect_Mythical`, thirty frames to the second, and its table of sixteen lenses), with two changes the field's art asks for. The lens changes its field of view and nothing else, never the pitch or the distance, because the upright things of the field are straightened for one pitch: the original's rush of the camera's distance is a rush of the lens, and its turns of the camera about the point it looks at are steps of that point, as far as the turn would swing a point four tiles off (`JoltReach`: four tiles times the sine of the turn, east for a turn about the upright and north for one that flattens the pitch, in whole texels). And the original's motion blur is left out: a smear is not pixel art. A lens is a factor of the place's own, as the kit's zoom is: the original's field of view against its field camera's (8.09° each side of the middle); 1 is the place's own. The camera is let go at the midpoint, under the white, so the field after the battle is looked at exactly as it was.

  | Seconds after the flashes | The legendary's | The mythical's |
  |---|---|---|

  | Length (lead, closing), flashes included | 4.15 s (1.9, 2.0) | 2.483 s (1.9, 0.333) |

- **The Galactic mark** (plan 09 · L13, decision 6; `GalacticMark`): Team Galactic's mark as this game draws it, which is not the original's. A lozenge (a square stood on its corner) cut like a stone into six facets round a dark core, with seams from the core to its top and bottom points and to the middle of each of its four sides. The facets are flat shades of their buildings' yellow band, lit from above: the upper two `248,228,128`, the two at the sides `232,204,76`, the lower two `196,164,48`. The seams and the rim are ink (`52,44,62`, or the dark of whatever it lies on), the core a lozenge a third as wide in the same ink, with a spark of the slit windows' teal `120,230,220` at its heart. It is one description (its facets as shapes, and a painter that lays the same shapes down in whole texels), so it is the same mark wherever it is shown: thrown at the picture as a grunt's or a Commander's battle begins, on the grunts' uniforms (plan 11 · C2), and over the doors of their buildings, where at fourteen texels only the lozenge and its core are left (the seams come in at 24 texels across, the spark at 48).
- **Scene effects** (plan 09 · L11; `SceneFx`): what a story's scene lights on a tile with a line of its script. Each is pixel art in cells of 32 texels (a tile), in whole texels and a few frames, and fades in the same two steps of alpha as everything here (170, then 90) over its last stretch; each plays its own sound as it begins. Six kinds:

| Kind | What is drawn | Frames | Colours | Length (going) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Burst | light thrown out of a point half a tile above the ground: a core with four rays (14 texels across), then eight (24), then eight broken and thinning (30), a tenth of a second each; eight sparks fly out on the screen's plane to 40 texels in half a second, slowing, those thrown downward kept low, each a star of 7 texels, then a cross of 5, then a dot of 2 that lingers to the end | 3 and 3 | core and rays `255,244,208`; sparks `255,246,196` | 0.6 s (the last 0.3) |
| Beam | a column standing on the tile, four tiles tall and two more fading (170, then 90), with a glow lying round its foot (28 by 14 texels, half alpha) and two motes of 2 texels rising up it; it grows from 12 texels wide to 18 a texel each side at a time over 0.2 s, breathes between 16 and 18 ten times a second, and narrows the same way as it goes; its edges step in and out a texel every four rows | 4 | core `248,252,255`, edges `206,232,255`, its outermost texel `150,196,240` | 1.5 s (0.2) |
| Rift | a tear standing in the air, a tile wide and two tall, its foot half a tile up: a slit for 0.12 s, half open until 0.25 s, then open, its ragged rim crawling through four frames at eight a second, shedding a mote of its rim to each side; it closes the same way | 6 | rim `255,214,255` and `214,110,246`, inside `30,14,48` with specks `90,52,128` | 3 s (0.25) |
| Shadow | a dark shape with wings spread lying on the ground, three tiles by two, crossing sixteen tiles the way it is sent, from eight before its tile to eight beyond, in whole texels; its wings beat in two frames, spread and drawn in, twice a second | 2 | `20,14,36` at alpha 120, its rim at 60 | 2 s (0.25) |
| Ripple | the whole picture's rows pushed sideways by whole texels of the field (a texel is three pixels at 1080p, a row 1/360 of the height) on a sine 24 rows long that travels two waves a second, stepped thirty times a second; it reaches one texel, then two, then three a tenth of a second apart, and steps back down | — | — | 1.2 s (0.3) |
| Sparkles | eight points of light rising round the tile, within three quarters of a tile either side and from a tenth of a tile to a tile and a tenth up, each a star of 7 texels, a cross of 5, a dot of 2 and gone, over 0.5 s of a cycle of 0.6 s begun at a moment of its own, rising a texel every sixteenth of a second | 3 | `255,246,196` | 1.5 s (0.5) |

A burst whitens the whole picture over the composite, as lightning does: 85 % white for its first twentieth of a second, then 50 %, then 20 % until a fifth of a second, then nothing. The effects are light and are drawn undimmed at every hour; only the shadow, which is dark, is drawn as it is. A held effect (a beam on a machine) plays until it is stopped, a held burst or shadow over and over, and a stopped one goes as it would at its end; coming to another place clears them all.

- **Set pieces** (plan 09 · L12; `SetPieces`): what a scene does to someone standing in it, and a lift's ride. Each is a function of the time since its script's line, stepped thirty times a second in whole texels, and the script waits for it; where they stand, and who can be spoken to, never changes.
  - *A shake*: whoever is shaken is drawn three texels east of their tile, back on it, three texels west and back, a thirtieth of a second each, so many times over (Regigigas eight times as it wakes, a little over a second). Their shadow goes with them.
  - *A flicker*: whoever flickers is not drawn for a fifth of a second, then drawn for a fifth, changing so many times in all (Cresselia six: gone, back, gone, back, gone, back, and then it is taken off the map). Nothing fades: they are there or they are not.
  - *A lift's shudder*: while a car is between floors the whole picture is thrown a texel up for a thirtieth of a second in every four, and as it stops two texels down for a fifteenth. It is the car that moves, so nothing in it moves against anything else.

### Light, shadow and grading (day)

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Sun direction | normalize(−0.6, 0.8, 0.42) |
| Sun colour | 0.66, 0.55, 0.40 (golden) |
| Sky / ground ambient | 0.50, 0.55, 0.74 / 0.46, 0.44, 0.40 |
| Diffuse | smooth Lambert (the pixel art carries the shading) |
| Shadows | soft and without grain: nine lookups of the shadow map on High, one texel apart, each the graphics card's own filtered comparison of the four texels round it, so that an edge is one even ramp four texels wide (about a dozen pixels at 4K). Four lookups on Medium, one on Low. Lookups further apart than a texel leave steps in the edge. On a machine where the card can't be asked to compare, a Poisson disc 1.8 texels in radius, rotated per pixel, stands in |
| Cloud shade | 20 % dimmer sunlight in broad patches that drift slowly across the map |
| Fog | toward `0.72, 0.82, 0.92`, 18 % at the far edge (starts 44 units from the camera, full at 72) |
| Ambient occlusion | screen-space, quarter resolution, read from a half-resolution copy of the depth buffer, radius 0.45 units, strength 0.4: grounds walls, props and sprites |
| Tilt-shift | a blur growing to 6 texels in radius (a disc of 12 taps; within 2.5 texels five taps are as dense as the texels and do), plus 95 % mix toward a wide blur outside a ±30 % focus band |
| Bloom | threshold 0.86, strength 0.38 |
| Grade | saturation 1.10, contrast 1.08, shadows × (0.88, 0.92, 1.10), highlights × (1.07, 1.00, 0.90) |
| Vignette | 0.26 |
| Rooms | their own warm lamp light at every hour; tilt-shift 4, depth of field 60 %, focus ±36 %, bloom threshold 0.93 at 0.22 |

The camera target and every sprite snap to the texel grid (1/32 unit), so pixel art scrolls in whole texels and doesn't shimmer. The other times of day are under *Time of day* below.

## Battles (3D)

### Models

- Pokémon and trainers are real 3D models on the platforms (no pixelation), lit and shadowed with the stage. Trainers are the SDF characters of the field, skinned on the GPU, with their smooth faces.
- Size rule: a Pokémon covers the part of the screen its 128-px sprite frame would, i.e. the frame spans the platform's width ÷ 158 px (opponent) or ÷ 150 px (player).
- Shading: two-tone cel ramp with a soft terminator (`smoothstep(0, 0.12, N·L)`), hemisphere ambient × 1.08, rim light 0.30.
- Outlines: offset shells of the SDF (the surface pushed out, meshed on its own and skinned with the body, drawn with front faces culled), so creases, brims and gaps between limbs never show spots of ink; colour = surface mixed toward `34,26,40`. A character's shell is 0.009 units out; a Pokémon's is half a pixel of its 128-px sprite frame (`WorldPerPixel × 0.55`).
- Flashes (send-out, hit, recall, the dark silhouette of a wild Pokémon before the camera settles) are colour blends in the shader, not extra sprites.

### Pokémon (version 2, G7)

- **One smooth body** sculpted with the SDF kit (`PokeBuilder` on `SdfModel`): ellipsoids, round cones, flattened spikes, rings and boxes joined with small smooth blends (2.5 cm for body parts, 1–1.5 cm for spikes and claws, sharp for a jaw's lip), meshed at 128 cells across the model's largest dimension (17,000–47,000 vertices) and cached on disk. Colour regions that shouldn't change the shape (masks, bands, a flame's heart) are paint with a soft edge 1.4 cm wide, or they follow the mesh's vertices in steps.
- **Materials**: fur and feathers matt (`Fur`), smooth hides with a small sheen (`Scales`), shells, claws, beaks, teeth and horns hard and glossy (`Shell`), gold and steel `Metal`, leaves `Leaf`, flames `Glow`.
- **Body plans** set the skeleton and how the clips move it: biped, quadruped, bird, serpent, fish, floating. Every plan has a root at the feet and a body bone at the middle of the torso; legs hang from the root so they stay planted while the body breathes above them; head, jaw, arms, wings, tail, ears, leaves, flames and fins have bones of their own; a serpent is a chain of segments.
- **Eyes and markings are decals**, never sculpted: each is a 96-px square of an atlas laid over the triangles under it (projected along its normal, lifted a hair off the surface) and cut out where it is empty. The eye or marking fills the middle half of its square, so coarse triangles never clip it. Eyes come in five states, each painted with anti-aliased shapes: **open** (a dark oval whose lower part is the iris, lighter toward the bottom, with a big glint upper left and a small one lower right; or a white eye ringed in ink with a pupil looking a little inward), **shut** (a gentle curve, for blinks and fainting), **squeezed** (a chevron pointing to the nose, when hit) and **fierce** (a lid pressed toward the inner corner, painted in the colour of the skin round the eye, while attacking) and **smiling** (the lid shut in an arch, its middle raised, the shut curve turned over: a cheer, plan 25 · Y5; a species whose eyes are always closed smiles too). A species whose eyes are always closed (Abra, Snorlax) shows them shut in every state but the squeeze; one that always glares (the Gastly line, Honchkrow) shows them fierce whenever they are open. A white eye's white can be another colour (Hoothoot's red, Spiritomb's lime). Markings are dots, rings, four- and five-pointed stars, bars, wavy lines, zigzags, triangles, diamonds and smiles (a shallow arc, its ends turned up: Ditto's, Lapras's, Dragonite's and Mew's mouths).
- **Hand-built models** (plan 03 · D6–D9, and decision 3 since): Platinum's Sinnoh Pokédex is sculpted species by species, and the popular species from outside it follow batch by batch from Kanto, Johto, Hoenn, Unova, Kalos, Alola, the rest of Sinnoh, Galar, Hisui and Paldea, our own sculpts after each design's shapes, colours and proportions, with the same kit and rules. Membranes and fins are thin: a bat's wing is skin over its arm and finger bones, filled triangle by triangle from the wrist and scalloped between the fingers; an insect's or a fish's fin is a flat oval rimmed in a darker or paler colour. Rock is lumps over a body (Geodude's line) or a chain of separate boulders with a crease between each (Onix); honeycomb is hexagonal cells. A wing whose two faces are coloured differently is two membranes laid back to back, its finger bones in the back one so the front stays clean (Charizard's, teal in front and orange behind). A lightning bolt is a flat zigzag (Pikachu's line), a curl a tube wound flat into a spiral (the Clefairy line's foreheads), whiskers thin pale tubes. A leaf with a jagged edge is a blade with leaflets swept past its edges (Ivysaur's and Venusaur's palm leaves); a snake rests on its coils laid on the ground (the Ekans line); a flame is lit from within, pale at its heart. A spiral on a belly is painted in short strokes along its curve (the Poliwag line); a crab's claw is a swollen hand and two fingers bowed apart round an open gap, closing at their tips (Krabby's line); a bell's or a pitcher's mouth is a hollow carved in and painted dark, a fleshy lip round it (Bellsprout's line); spots on a tilted cap are painted where its surface lies (Paras's line); shaggy fur is tufts of points over a body, bent down as far as the fur hangs (Venonat, Galarian Meowth, the Mankey line, the Growlithe line's manes). Fins, leaves and crests that come to a point are blades, flat to the side they face (Remoraid's fins, Leafeon's leaves, Gallade's crest); a leaf or a frond that is widest in the middle is an oval flat to the side it faces (Tropius's wings). Vines are tubes looped over a ball, the face left bare (Tangela's line); fur that hangs is points pointing down round a body (Piloswine's line); a tiger's stripes are short bands painted across the surface, never rings round it (Electabuzz's line); a drill is a cone with grooves round it (Rhydon's line); a body of flat faces is blocks joined without blending (Porygon). An ammonite's spiral is painted along its shell's sides in short strokes, with lines across its back where its chambers meet (the Omanyte line); a seahorse's tail curls forward into a spiral under it (the Horsea line); a star stands on edge, its five points flattened front to back and the jewel at its core set in a ring of gold (Staryu's line; Starmie's second star is turned half a point behind the first). A bird's spread wing is an arm with long feathers fanned from it, rounded or pointed like spikes (the legendary birds), and a streamer is a flat ribbon of pieces that reach well past each other, so its edges hardly dip where they meet (Articuno's tail). Long narrow leaves arch from a crown (Exeggutor's). White down a serpent's rising neck is painted a stroke on each of the neck's bones, since paint colours only its own bone (Dratini's line). A flower is petals round a disc, flat to the way it faces: up from a head (Skiploom), forward as a face (Sunflora), two in the hair (Bellossom). Coral is branches with round tips and a twig forking off each (Corsola). A puffer's spikes stand all over it but for its face and the root of its tail (Qwilfish). The legendary beasts stand on long legs with broad paws, a storm cloud a row of lumps over the back (Raikou), a flowing mane a flat ribbon (Suicune), smoke puffs of three rounds (Entei). A wing that ends in fingers is feathers spread from its wrist (Lugia); antlers are tubes with tines forking off them (Stantler). A lily pad is a flat disc with a rim, its wedges marks on its top (the Lotad line); an acorn's cap is ringed in painted steps (Seedot); a halo floats free over its wearer (Shedinja, which is in two pieces by design); an eye on a wing is a mark ringed in paint (Masquerain). A crescent is a disc with a smaller disc carved from its side (Lunatone); a sun's rays are blades fanned round a ball in its own plane (Solrock); a spring is a tube wound in a helix (Spoink); a cut gem is a flattened oval with a paler ring on its face (Sableye's eyes and chest). A golem of stone or ice is blocks joined with little rounding, and its face seven dots in its own pattern (the Regis); a body of steel plates is discs and limbs with grey joints (the Beldum line); strands twisted together are two tubes wound round one path (Deoxys's arms); a crescent of wings is a flat disc with its middle carved away before the body goes in (Mega Salamence). A drill is a cone banded round with paint (Mega Excadrill); stone is blocks with rounded edges, crystals jutting from it (the Roggenrola line); a crest of an element is its own shape on one build (the elemental monkeys: a tuft of leaves, a curl or row of flame, rounds of water). A thing carried is held on the bone of the hand that holds it and touches both hands (Timburr's log, Gurdurr's beam, Conkeldurr's pillars), or on the tail that holds it (Yamask's mask); a hood is a shell cut open at the face, the face added after the cut and the cut wide enough to show the whole face (Sewaddle's leaf, Mega Scrafty's, Chespin's); a centipede curled into a wheel looks out of a hollow carved in its middle (Whirlipede); rock in layers is a block painted in thin flat bands (Crustle); brows of flame are a row of tongues, not a horn (Darmanitan). A cell in gel is a pale body set in a ball of green gel, its face standing out of the gel's front (the Solosis line); a gear is a steel ring with square teeth round a sunken face and a raised core, and gears that mesh stand in one plane (the Klink line); a jellyfish's tentacles are broad ribbons hanging from its collar (Frillish's line); a gown in tiers is flared cones cut flat at the ground (Gothitelle); a season is one build in the season's colours with what it wears on its antlers (Deerling and Sawsbuck). A seal of clay is a square panel with a spiral pressed into it (the Golett line); a head hidden in fur is a hood of shaggy tufts over an open jaw, and a head at the end of an arm the same hood made smaller (the Deino line); a moth's six wings are flat petals round it in one plane, kept narrow enough to stand apart (Volcarona); the genies of the forces of nature ride a heap of white puffs on the root bone, their arms crossed in front of them (Tornadus, Thundurus, Landorus); a turbine is a drum ringed with bands round a tail's middle (Reshiram, the White Kyurem) and a generator a drum at a tail's end, its rim lit (Zekrom, the Black Kyurem); a fusion is one build with the other dragon's wings and tail (Kyurem's); a drive is the colour of a slot on one build (Genesect). A shuriken of water is a flat disc with blades fanned in its own plane (Ash-Greninja's, the one Mega Greninja hangs from); a butterfly's patterns are one build in each pattern's colours, its wings banded round their roots, spotted and rimmed (Vivillon); a flower a fairy holds or sits on is petals round a disc on the bone that bears it, and a wreath is rounds of flowers about the head with strands of small ones hanging on a thread (the Flabébé line); a trim is one build with the trim's shapes on the head and tail and its colour at the legs (Furfrou); a haunted sword's eye is on the front of its crossguard and its cloth arcs over the pommel and falls beside the blade to a frayed end (the Honedge line), and a shield held in front touches the cloths that hold it (Aegislash). A hand with a face of its own carries its own eyes (Binacle's two hands, four eyes in all); a slime is a puddle of a darker shade with the body rising out of it (the Goomy line), and a shell of metal over it a smooth gray dome with a spiral drawn on each side (the Hisuian Sliggoo and Goodra); a jack-o'-lantern's face is marks that glow, never carved (Gourgeist); the sizes of a Pokémon that comes in sizes are one build at each size's scale and fill (the Pumpkaboo line); a shrimp's great claw stands to one side of its face, never over its eyes (Clauncher's line); a sail down a neck is a curtain of fins, widest behind the head (Aurorus); antlers of light are a beam with tines, each tine tipped in its own colour (Xerneas); a hand that floats free is a piece of its own on a bone of its own, as Haunter's (Hoopa Unbound's four). A Pokémon of several styles is one build in each style's colours and dress (Oricorio's four); a school is one great fish whose skin is many small fish, each laid on the surface and tied to it by a short stalk so the school stays one piece (Wishiwashi's School Form); a hood of leaves is green paint over the back of the head with two leaves falling from its crown to its cheeks (Dartrix); a cage of legs is tubes arched from the crown down to the ground, each on a bone of its own, the front ones parted to show the face inside (Toxapex). A Pokémon that carries a memory or shows a core is one build in its colour (Silvally's fin and eyes, Minior's cores); a disguise whose neck is broken is the same head turned about the neck, its scrawled face and ears turning with it (the busted Mimikyu); armour of scales is discs laid on the surface along its normal, each with a heart painted at its middle (the Jangmo-o line). A Pokémon fused with another is the other's build, its colours changed where the design changes them and the fusing one's armour laid over it (Necrozma's Dusk Mane and Dawn Wings); a hex nut is six slabs, each reaching exactly to the corners, where the next takes over, or the corners stand out like a cog's teeth (Meltan and Melmetal). A Pokémon that holds a plate is one build in its type's colours, its grey and the gems on its wheel taking the type's (Arceus), and a wheel round a body is a ring tipped toward the head with four arms crossing it into the body, so it stays one piece; smoke and cloud are a heap of round puffs, never a column (Mega Heatran); a drum's face is a disc laid on a flattened ellipsoid, never a capsule's rounded end (the Gigantamax Rillaboom); a rider on what it has become is a small copy of its build standing in it (the Gigantamax Cinderace on its fireball, the Gigantamax Inteleon on its tower); a flower worn as a bonnet tips back so the face shows under its brim (Gossifleur), and a face set in front of a great ball is lighter than the leaves that frame it, so it doesn't sink into them (Eldegoss). A cup is a round bowl cut off flat at its rim and hollowed, its tea filling the hollow down to its bottom, or the hollow under the tea is a closed pocket that the soundness check counts as a loose piece (Sinistea); a pie's lattice is strips laid along the crust's own surface both ways (Appletun); a centipede is segments each ringed with a darker band, a pair of little legs on each that touches the ground (the Sizzlipede line); a twister is a tube winding up and out from a point (the Gigantamax Sandaconda); a catch is held between the open jaws of the beak, on the head's bone (Cramorant's). A Pokémon of many creams and sweets is one build in each cream's colours with the sweet it wears tucked into each lock, a swirled cream striped round its locks, its peak and its skirt (Alcremie's 63); a squad is its troopers in a line turned a little aside, so that each one's face clears the one in front (Falinks); a moth's wings face forward, as a butterfly's do, or the camera sees them edge on (Frosmoth); a face on a flat block keeps its decals clear of the pillars and crown that join the block, by more than the reach of their blend (Stonjourner). A flier's hat like a stealth plane is blades flat to the sky (Drakloak, Dragapult), and a little one riding on it a small copy of its build, its eyes kept to the middle of its small face so their squares lie on the face all round (the Dreepys); a rider on a mount is a small copy of its build seated on the mount's back on the body's bone, reins a thin tube from its hands to the mount's muzzle (Calyrex's Ice and Shadow Riders); a war horse of ice stands in clusters of crystal points round each hoof (Glastrier), and a mane or tail of smoke is wavy strands streaming down (Spectrier); a spring is rings stacked down a cone, each touching it, or they fall apart (Regieleki); a building of a Pokémon is blocks with little rounding, a wall of glass a flat point down its front (Duraludon and its Gigantamax form); a braid is a chain of small rounds along the body (Zacian), a crowned hero's armour gold plates laid over the build (the Crowned Sword and Shield). A heart drawn in the air is a thin loop on the heart's own curve standing on its point on the head, and a heart on the skin two lobes of paint leaning together to a point (Enamorus); a cloak of peat is a grey dome over the back with brown flaps hanging round its edge (Ursaluna); an axe held on a forearm is a narrow blade turned aside, or from the front it reads as a wing (Kleavor); a boa of water is a string of beads close enough to touch, or it falls apart (Quaquaval); a ball of thread is a white ball with lines of thread painted round it every way (Tarountula), and thread held between legs thin tubes from knee to knee and down (Spidops). A family is its members as one piece, holding paws, the ears of two side by side staggered front to back so they never touch, and a little one's eyes a little bigger for its size, so they stay whole on its small face (Tandemaus, Maushold); an ear rolled up like a bun is a thick ring on its edge filled with a softer roll (Fidough, Dachsbun); salt is blocks with little rounding, banded brown and white like strata, its eyes orange slits (the Nacli line); a tumbleweed is a cage of branches arching up from a knot at its foot, its eyes held up inside it on a stem so it stays one piece, and a wreath of branches rings laid a little askew (Bramblin, Brambleghast); a blade of ghostly fire carries a thin ridge down its middle, which keeps its point whole (Ceruledge). Two heads on one body are each on a bone of their own, a neck to each (Scovillain); what a Pokémon holds up or pushes along is joined to it by its limbs, so the two stay one piece (Rellor's ball, Rabsca's); a great hammer is a thick cylinder capped flat at both ends, on the bone of the hand that holds it (Tinkaton); a garden eel is a tube rising from the ground and bowing over at its top (the Wiglett line); a bud of crystal that bursts open stands its petals round it on stalks of crystal, so it stays one piece (Mega Glimmora); ports along a worm of many segments are painted, since its segments overlap and a decal there finds no one bone under it (Orthworm). An eye's half-height is at least a cell and a half: a model as tall as Miraidon (1.44) has cells of 0.012 and eyes of 0.017, and smaller ones find too few triangles to cover their square. A creature sitting in a bowl reaches down into the hollow the bowl's cut leaves, or it floats there as a piece of its own (Sinistcha); a chain is links each touching the last, and a collar of links ends at the link the chain leaves it from (Okidogi). The mesh is 128 cells across a model's largest dimension, so a very tall design is kept no taller than its look needs and its thin parts at least a cell thick (Alolan Exeggutor). Porygon-Z's parts float apart, as the design has them, and so does Shedinja's halo. Fliers and floaters hang 12 % of their height above their platform, as the generated ones do. Eyes follow the design: two as a rule; none for Zubat, for Ralts (under its hair), for Nosepass (shut in a black band), for Yanmega (its eyes are its head), for Piloswine (under its fur), for Deino and Zweilous (under their hoods of fur) for Staryu's line (its core is its face), for Chinchou (its crossed eyes are marks) for the Regis, Regigigas, Regieleki and Regidrago (dots in their place), for Type: Null (behind its mask), for the riders of the Gigantamax Cinderace and Inteleon (too small for eyes to show), for the Gigantamax Flapple and Sandaconda (an apple and a twister, no face showing) and the Gigantamax Alcremie (a cake), for Nihilego, Xurkitree, Celesteela, Kartana and Blacephalon (no face at all), for Eternatus and its Eternamax (a skull with no eyes showing) for Roggenrola and Boldore (the hexagon on their front is an ear), for the Nacli line (orange slits, marks that never blink), for Bramblin and Brambleghast (spindles held in their branches), for Orthworm (only its mouth), for Greavard (under its fur), for Houndstone (hollows in its skull) and for Poltchageist and Sinistcha (swirls painted on caddy, bowl and matcha); one for Lunatone, Beldum, Honedge and Aegislash (on the crossguard; Doublade has one on each sword), Dhelmise (a porthole on its wheel), Meltan and Melmetal (in the hole of their nut), Trevenant (in a dark hollow of its trunk), Sigilyph (the two eyespots on its body are marks, which never blink), Litwick (wax over the other), Darkrai and its Mega (the other under the plume), Rolycoly (its one great eye), Runerigus (in the top of its slab), Klang's and Klinklang's small gear (the great gear's face is marks), Unown, Kirlia (the other under its hair), Magnemite, Charcadet (the other under its shell of charcoal) and the Duskull line; three for Magneton and Magnezone and for Sandy Shocks (one on each of its units); four for Vanilluxe's two faces, for Drakloak (its own and the Dreepy's on its hat) for Calyrex's Ice and Shadow Riders (the horse's and Calyrex's) for Tandemaus (two mice) and for Scovillain and its Mega (a pair on each head); six for Dragapult (its own and its two Dreepys'), for Wugtrio (a pair on each of its three), for Iron Jugulis (its head's and the heads' on its arms) and for Maushold's family of three, eight for its family of four; Joltik's and Galvantula's small eyes, which never blink, are marks; seven round Claydol's head; a pair on each of Combee's three faces, on each of Dugtrio's and Dodrio's three heads and on Doduo's two, on each of Exeggcute's six eggs and Exeggutor's three heads (four for the Alolan, one on its tail), on each of Weezing's two heads and Binacle's two hands, and on Kangaskhan and its baby.
- **Generated models** (plan 03 · D5): every species without a hand-built model is sculpted from its data with the same kit, the same rules (smooth bodies, paint for patches, decals for eyes and marks, the materials below) and the same body plans. Chibi proportions shrink with evolution: basic species and babies have the biggest heads and shortest legs, final stages and legendaries the longest. Colours: the Pokédex colour as the body, nudged in hue and value by the evolutionary line so families share a shade; an accent from the types (a flame's orange, a leaf's green) where it stands apart from the body; a pale belly and muzzle; dark paws, stripes and masks. Fliers and floaters hang 12 % of their height above their platform.
- **Forms** (plan 03 · D11): a form (a Mega, a regional form, a Gigantamax form, Deoxys's formes) is a model of its own, generated as its species would be from the form's types, stats and size and in the form's own Pokédex colour (black for Mega Charizard X, white for Alolan Vulpix), as one of its species' family, by Platinum's values whatever rules the game is played by. Every form of a hand-built species is hand-built like the species. Platinum's own: Rotom's appliances are the appliance in Rotom's orange with Rotom's eyes, its plasma limbs in the appliance's own colour; Giratina's Origin Forme floats as a banded serpent with six shadowy tendrils; the cloaks and the East Sea keep their species' build in other materials and colours; the Unown's letters are the A's eye in its ring with strokes of the same black in the front plane, the signs (! and ?) with the eye half shut. The later games': a regional form keeps its species' build in its region's colours and materials; a Mega is its species grown into its Mega Evolution's shape (Mega Alakazam's five spoons float round its head, as the design has them); a Gigantamax form carries Gigantamax's red clouds over it, puffs strung on a wisp so they hang together; Pikachu wears its caps and costumes over its own sculpt, a female's heart at the tip of its tail with the costumes. A form that looks just like its species (Mothim's cloaks, the partner Pikachu and Eevee) shows its sculpt, and a model file named after a form (`Charizard-Mega-X.glb`) replaces any of them.
- **Model files** (`overrides/models`, [`docs/model-files.md`](../model-files.md)): a glTF model replaces a species' sculpt as it is, lit by the character shader with material 0 (or glow and metal where its materials say), outlined by a shell half a sprite pixel out along smoothed normals, scaled to the same size rule.
- **Clips** (`PokemonAnimation`), layered over the idle loop: *idle* (breathing, head sway, tail swish, ear flicks every few seconds, wings flapping when airborne, flames flickering); *physical* (drawn back, then a strike forward with the jaw open, arms swung through and a lunge across the field); *special* (rears up gathering power with arms and wings spread, then thrusts toward the target with a cry, without moving from its place); *status* (a hop and a nod); *hit* (recoil, squash, limbs flung out); *faint* (the legs give way, then a biped topples onto its front, a quadruped sinks onto its belly with its legs splayed, a flier drops onto its side; then it sinks into the platform); *entry* (lands with a squash out of the ball, then stands tall with a cry). A physical strike and a special move's release peak when the damage lands, 0.35 s into a 0.65 s move.
