  - **Bodies that answer** (plan 25 · Y5; Platinum draws none of these, so they are our own): *dodge*, as a move that misses passes it, 0.8 s with its peak at the impact (0.35 s): a biped or a quadruped hops aside, a bird banks and slips aside, a fish or a floater dips and slips aside, a serpent coils, drawing its head in and leaning aside; each comes back to where it stood. The way aside is away from the miss's path (a miss flies wide on one side of its target; the dodge takes the other), a third of the body's height for one that hops, a quarter for one that banks or dips and a fifth for a serpent, which mostly coils. *Tired*, for as long as its HP bar is in the red (a fifth or less, as the bar shows it, never the rules' HP): the head lowers, the arms, wings, ears and tail droop, a flier hangs lower, and a slow deep breath (one every 2.6 s) takes over from most of the idle's; it eases in and out over 0.6 s as the bar crosses the line. *Cheer*, 1.2 s: two hops with the head thrown back and the arms, wings and tail up, for the player's Pokémon on the field as the battle is won or a Pokémon caught, and for the foe's as the player loses. *Charge*, through a charge turn (Solar Beam's light, Skull Bash's tuck, Sky Attack's glow), from the line that tells of it until the move is let go next turn: the body gathers itself low, the head down and drawn in, the arms and wings folded, a little tremble in it; it eases in over 0.4 s and goes as the move's own lunge begins, and a type's aura gathers round it (the move's charge half; each charge move's own comes with its batch of Y6 to Y10). Every one starts and ends at the idle pose with the feet where they were.
- **The pose's fields and the eyes' order** (plan 09 · L2 keeps this list; plan 25 · Y5 and plan 10 · F2, F6 and F10 add to it in the places given here, whichever lands first).
  - `PokePose`'s fields stand in groups, in this order, and a new field goes at the end of its own group, never between two that are there: the clock (`Time`); the layered clips (`Blink`, `Attack`, `Kind`, `Hurt`, `Faint`, `Entry`); what lasts on the body (`Asleep`, `Frozen`: L2); a battle's answers (`Dodge`, `Tired`, `Cheer`, `Charge`, `Stat`: Y5; the dodge and the cheer are their clips' progress, tiredness and the charge how much of them shows, and `Stat` a stat's change within its 0.6 s, above nought for a rise and below for a fall); the walk (`Walk`, `Moving`: F2); the camp's faces (`Happy`, `Sleepy`, `LookAt`: F6); a held look (`Fierce`: F10).
  - The eyes (`PokemonAnimation.EyesOf`) show the first of these that holds:
    1. a faint, past a fifth of its clip: shut;
    2. asleep: shut, whatever else happens to it (a sleeper that is hit, or that strikes in its sleep with Snore or Sleep Talk, keeps them shut);
    3. a hit: squeezed;
    4. an attack that is no status move, or a charge (Y5): fierce;
    5. a held fierce look (F10): fierce;
    6. a stat falling, squeezed for 0.6 s, or rising, fierce for 0.6 s (Y5);
    7. frozen: open and held, and nothing below this line reaches a frozen face;
    8. a cheer (Y5): smiling, shut in a smile's curve; the camp's faces (F6) stand here too;
    9. tiredness (Y5): longer blinks, shut for 0.45 s in every 2.6 s, in place of the blink below;
    10. a blink: shut;
    11. open.
  - A species' own eyes go over this as before (one whose eyes are always closed, one that always glares).
  - A frozen body is held the same way: its idle loop is played at one moment whatever the clock says (the loop's start), so it neither breathes, sways nor flaps, and a flier hangs still. A clip that answers something (a hit's recoil, the faint) still plays over the held idle.

### Materials

Character and Pokémon surfaces carry a material (G6, G7), which the character shader lights differently. Highlights are one crisp band where the half vector meets the normal (`smoothstep(0.42, 0.5, …)`), never a smooth gradient.

| Material | Terminator (`smoothstep(0, w, N·L)`) | Highlight band (strength, sharpness) | Own light | Shade |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Default | 0.12 | none | none | as lit (models without materials) |
| Skin | 0.22 | none | none | warm: sky light × `1.10, 0.96, 0.94` where unlit |
| Cloth | 0.16 | none | none | rim × 0.9 |
| Hair | 0.10 | 0.14, 10 (a soft sheen) | none | |
| Leather (shoes, bags) | 0.08 | 0.40, 36 | none | |
| Plastic (soles, brims, buttons) | 0.10 | 0.35, 48 | none | |
| Metal (buckles, clasps) | 0.06 | 0.60, 64 | none | slightly cool |
| Glow (the Rift, flames) | 0.12 | none | 0.85 | rim × 0.5 |
| Fur and feathers | 0.20 | none | none | a touch warm, rim × 1.15 |
| Scales and smooth hide | 0.12 | 0.12, 26 | none | |
| Shell (shells, claws, beaks, teeth, horns) | 0.08 | 0.36, 30 | none | rim × 0.9 |
| Leaf | 0.22 | 0.06, 8 | none | green bounce `0.94, 1.06, 0.94`, rim × 1.25 |

### Stage

- Textures are smooth, mipmapped and filtered (`SoftTextures`): meadow `114,190,100` with light `138,202,104` and deep `90,168,94` patches a few metres across; platform tops lighter in the middle (`150,220,124` → `100,182,96`) with one soft worn ring.
- Foliage is modelled (`SoftFoliage`): tiered pines with drooping rims and dark undersides; round trees made of overlapping blobs whose normals bend toward the canopy centre so the crown shades as one soft mass; seven-blade tufts, dark at the root, light at the tip. Colour comes from vertex gradients.
- Scenery takes a soft cel band (`BattleRamp` 0.85).
- Sky (`SkyPainter`): zenith `78,146,226`, middle `140,194,244` at 22 % height, hazy horizon `224,238,250` at 46 %; soft cumulus with a white crown and a cool `196,212,236` underside, drifting slowly.

### Arenas (G8)

The stage depends on where the battle starts (`ArenaSpec`, `BattleArenas`): outdoors the ground under the player picks it, and a map can name its own (data-files.md). Every arena keeps the same layout (platforms, camera) and the smooth style: vertex gradients and soft textures, modelled foliage and rock, no texture noise.

| Arena | Ground and platforms | Around the field | Light (`ArtLook.ArenaRig`) | In the air (`ArenaFx`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Grass (routes, towns) | meadow, grass tops on dirt rims | the map's trees, hills; a lake if the map has one | the time of day's battle rig | fireflies at night |
| Forest | darker meadow with leaf litter, mossy tops | five rows of trees, trees closing in at both sides, bushes, ferns, fallen logs, mushrooms | battle rig × 0.8 sun, green shade and haze (fog +15 %), vignette +0.16; after dark only a dark green haze, so the Pokémon stay clear | slanting light shafts and pollen by day, fireflies at night |
| Cave | warm grey-brown rock floor, flat stone tops | rock walls all round (lumpy masses, dark at the foot, lit on top), stalagmites, boulders, blue and violet crystals that glow | fixed: torch-warm key from the upper left `0.58,0.45,0.31`, cool blue shade, dark haze (55 %), vignette 0.42 | dim warm dust |
| Water | open water to the horizon | the platforms are rocky islets with sandy tops and foam rings; islands and sea stacks far off | the time of day's battle rig | sun glints on the water |
| Snow | white-blue snowfield, packed-snow tops | pines laden with snow, drifts, capped rocks, white mountains | the battle rig with a paler overcast sky, light bounced up off the ground (ground ambient × 1.3), saturation × 0.88, contrast 1.0 | falling snow |
| Sand | rippled sand, sandstone tops | dunes, banded mesas, dry shrubs, bleached rocks | the battle rig, hotter and hazier: warm sun, warm fog, more bloom | sand blowing across |
| Indoors | floorboards, round rugs | panelled walls with daylit windows, bookshelves, potted plants | the room's light of the time of day | dust in the light |
| Gym (per type) | polished tiles in the type's tones, the type's emblem between the platforms, raised drums ringed with light | walls with banners in the type's colour, columns with lamps, the type's props (boulders, planters, pools, candles, girders, ice crystals, tesla coils, braziers, training posts) | fixed: neutral key, ambient leaning 16 % toward the type, bloom 0.32 | the type's matter (embers, bubbles, leaves, sparks, snow, wisps, motes) |
| League (Aaron, Bertha, Flint, Lucian; the Champion) | as a gym, darker and grander; the Champion's room white and gold | as a gym | as a gym, ambient × 0.78, bloom 0.45, vignette 0.38 | as a gym, more of it |

Walls, rock faces and rooms take shadows but cast none, so they never darken the platforms. Lamps, crystals and glowing rims are added light (the stage's light pass).

### Camera (G8)

`BattleCamera` directs the shots from the battle's state alone; the overview is the framing above (9° pitch, 24° field of view).

- **Intro**: the sweep in from the side (0.55 rad round and 35 % further out, easing into the overview over 1.8 s).
- **Send-out**: a closer shot of the platform a Pokémon is thrown onto, until it has come out (3.6 × its height in frame).
- **The foe's shots stand in front of the player's platform** (2.5 units or more): a foe too big to frame from there gets a wider lens (up to 40°) rather than a camera among the player's trainer and Pokémon.
- **A move**: the attacker as it winds up (3 × its height in frame; the player's Pokémon over its left shoulder with the foe beyond, the foe from the front), then a **cut** to the target 0.24 s in, just before the impact (3 × its height; two targets are framed together), back to the overview by 0.95 s. A status move on the user stays on the user.
- **Critical hit**: a cut punch-in to 2.1 × the target's height at the impact, for 0.4 s.
- **Thrown ball**: over to the trainer running in; from behind them, turning to where the ball is going and zooming in (to 15° at the narrowest, so the arc stays in frame) as it flies; a cut to the ball opening over the foe; then close on the ball on the platform (2 units in frame) while it wobbles and clicks.
- **Faint**: toward the fainting Pokémon.
- Shots ease into each other (time constant 0.18 s); only attacker-to-target and the critical punch-in cut, and any move that would carry the camera across the player's platform (from behind it to in front of it, or back) or within 3 units of it, where the trainer or the player's Pokémon would loom up in front of the lens. While the player chooses a command the camera is always on the overview. Hits shake it (more for critical and super-effective ones; Earthquake and Hyper Beam add their own).

### Move effects (G8)

- **One kit** (`BattleFx`): streams of particles from the user to the target, beams, forked bolts, bursts thrown from the impact, shockwaves (rings growing on the ground or facing the camera), auras (rings and motes rising round the user), rain from above, a contact flash, slashes, orbs, waves, glints and screen flashes. Every effect is a function of its cue's age and seed: nothing is simulated.
- **Shapes** come from one atlas (`FxTextures`, 128-px cells, eight by eight since plan 25 · Y6: G8's 32 in the first four rows, the rest filled as the move batches ask): glows, sparks, rings and beams are light, added on top so they bloom; flames, drops, leaves, shards, rocks, feathers, petals, hearts, notes and letters are matter, alpha-blended and back to front. All are white or grey and take their type's colour. Y6 added six, all matter: a **fist** seen from the front (a rounded block, three knuckle lines, the thumb across), a **foot** from the side (a sole, a heel, toes to the right), an open **palm** (four fingers up, the thumb out), a narrowed **eye** (a lens of white, a dark iris and a glint), **cracks** running out from a point (a jagged line each way and two branches) and an **exclamation mark**. Y7 added five more, all matter: a **hexagon** of a shield (a pale panel, a brighter rim and a gleam across its upper half), a **target** (a ring with four ticks pointing in and a dot in the middle), a **beak** from the side (pointing right, a line along its gape), a **horn** (a curved point, ridged at its base) and a **seed** (an oval with a seam down it).
- **A template per type and category** (`MoveFx`): a physical move lands with a flash of contact and a burst of its type (flames, drops, sparks, leaves, shards, rocks, feathers, bubbles, wisps, petals, stars); a special move sends something across (Fire a stream of flames, Water a stream of drops, Electric a bolt, Grass spinning leaves, Ice and Dragon and Steel a beam, Fighting and Normal an orb of power, Poison sludge in an arc, Ground an eruption under the target, Flying gusts, Psychic rings, Bug a swarm, Rock thrown rocks, Ghost a shadow orb, Dark rings, Fairy sparkles); a status move wraps the user in an aura of its colour, or sends waves at the foe. 146 moves have effects of their own (Thunder's bolt from the sky, Flamethrower, Surf's wave, Hyper Beam, Swords Dance, the fangs of Bite, claws and slashes, Earthquake's shockwaves, sound waves for Growl, hearts for Charm, powders, weather).
- **Moves of their own** (plan 25 · Y6 to Y10, then Y11 to Y15 for the later games' moves): every move is given a look and a sound of its own, a batch at a time in the order the story shows them (`MoveSightings` in the tests: the first place, in plan 01's order of opening, where a trainer, a wild Pokémon at its level or a TM found or given shows the move; the water and the rods wait for HM03 and each rod). **Beats read, shapes and sounds our own**: each batch first reads the original's animation script of each move for its beats only (its parts in order, when it lands, whether the background darkens, who shakes, the names of its sounds); its particle files, sprites and palettes are art and are never opened. A recipe lives in the batch's file (`MoveFx.Platinum1.cs` for Y6's first chapter, and on), built from the kit's primitives, and replaces G8's look of the move where there was one. **What every recipe holds to**: it lands at 0.35 s with the damage (`FxList.Land`, which the landing sets, and which a recipe that lands its own way sets itself; a status move "lands" when it reaches its target or wraps its user), it is over by 1.3 s, it flashes at most 0.5 and under half a second, and at no age does it draw more quads and ribbons than `MoveFx.MaxQuads` (124: the heaviest of G8's looks, Surf's wave on a critical, super-effective hit, as Y6 measured it; a batch's look that wants more is cut down, the cap is never raised). **Its sound** is `use_<move>` (`docs/sound-effects.md`, "Moves"); a move whose batch hasn't come plays its type's template and its type's sound. The moves that are the user's own voice (Growl, Roar, Hyper Voice, Howl, Chatter) are heard as its cry in modes of their own. **The ground**: a cue carries the battle's terrain, and Secret Power's look goes by it (leaves on grass, sand and the marsh's mud as grains, a spray on water, rocks in a cave or on a mountain, ice on snow, rings in the League's and the Frontier's halls, a blow of stars anywhere else), as Camouflage's wash takes the ground's colours. Y6's first chapter, in its own words: Leer is a red eye narrowing over the user twice, glinting, and red rings rolling to the foe; Pound is an open hand swinging in and slapping; Scratch three white claw lines raked down, Fury Swipes two sets crossing; Tackle dust kicked up as the user runs and a ring where it meets; Withdraw rings of shell closing in round the user; Bide a red glow beating round it; Return gold stars round the user flying to the target; Quick Attack a white streak left behind; Fake Out two hands clapping before the target with a small flash; Poison Gas purple clouds that swell round the target and linger; Screech pale pink waves and the air round the target jittering; Growth leaves spiralling up the user; Leech Life a sting and red motes drawn back; Low Kick a foot sweeping in low with dust; Teleport a column of pink light rising through the user; Water Sport and Mud Sport drops thrown up round the user and raining back; Defense Curl a pale swirl curling in; Rock Smash a fist, cracks and stone thrown off; Aerial Ace a long bright slash after the speed lines; Astonish a wisp leaping and an exclamation mark over the target's head; Brick Break the edge of a hand chopping down and the thing cracking; Counter the user flaring orange and a fist back; Flash a white burst flooding the screen (0.45); Focus Punch a fist gathered before the user and flying in heavy; Force Palm a palm and pale blue rings of force with sparks; Rock Polish grit swirled off and the user glinting; Take Down dust, a hard blow and stars over the user from the recoil; Harden a grey sheen climbing it; Headbutt a ring and stars circling the target's head; Pursuit a dark shadow streaking after it; Rock Throw two stones lobbed and dust where they land; Stealth Rock five pointed stones lobbed to settle round the foe's platform (what lies there after is "What lies on the field"); Focus Energy orange streaks rushing into the user and its aura rising. Growl (its notes in a warm tint), Supersonic (pale yellow rings), Tail Whip (two white arcs of the swish), Absorb, Bubble, Ember, Splash, Water Gun, Earthquake and Rock Slide keep G8's looks, now theirs with a sound of their own. Y7's first part (Route 204 to Team Galactic's Eterna Building, sixty-seven moves): String Shot white threads shot across and wound round the target in bands; Rock Tomb four rocks dropped round the target one after another, each with dust and a jolt, standing a moment; Water Pulse a ball of water rolling across in pulsing rings and breaking in rings; Bullet Seed a rapid string of seeds, a pop where each lands; Charge sparks drawn into the user and little bolts crackling round it as its glow swells; Charm hearts hopping up round the user and one big heart floating to the foe; Leech Seed three seeds lobbed onto the target, sprouting leaves; Poison Sting a long needle and a purple puff; Stun Spore a yellow cloud puffed over the target that sprinkles down crackling; Wing Attack two wings sweeping in from either side and crossing, feathers flying; Pluck a beak jabbing three times; Peck a beak jabbing twice; Baton Pass a baton spun up over the user, glinting; Foresight a target ring closing on the foe and a glint in it; Lock-On the same ring tightening in steps, red; Karate Chop the edge of a hand chopping across; Mega Drain and Giga Drain green motes spiralling back to the user, which glows pale (Giga Drain more of them, with leaves); Mud Bomb a ball of mud lobbed and bursting in splatter; Mud-Slap clumps of mud flung from the user's feet, splattering; Rage the user flaring red, an anger mark, and the blow; Sand Attack two handfuls of sand fanned at the target and a cloud left over it; Sonic Boom three pale crescents in quick succession; Hypnosis the user's eyes glowing and rings rolling to the target, a swirl over its head; Thunderbolt three bolts struck down onto the target from above with sparks and a pale flash (0.25); Bite fangs closing once; Crunch fangs closing hard and shards breaking off; Feint Attack dark smoke where the user was and dark smoke at the target's far side as the blow lands; Toxic purple sludge welling up round the target and popping; Agility zigzag streaks either side of the user, twice; Aqua Jet a jet of water from the user to the target and its spray; Body Slam a wide ring on the ground where the weight lands, and dust; Confuse Ray a wobbling orb of pale light circling the target's head; Fire Punch a flaming fist and flames; Fire Spin a column of flame spiralling round the target; Hidden Power six orbs circling the user, then converging on the target; Magnet Bomb four steel orbs arcing in and bursting in sparks; Rain Dance dark clouds gathering high over the field and rain falling from them; Spark the user crackling yellow, sparks on the target; Swift a fan of stars curving in with trails; Thunder Wave a ring of electricity tightening round the target, crackling; Confusion pink rings rippling on the target, wobbling; Gust a whirlwind at the target, feathers in it; Horn Attack a horn thrust twice; Rollout a rolling wheel of dust carried in; Sleep Talk Z's rising and a pale wave of a mumble; Curse the user flaring red and dark wisps rising round it; Endure a red pulse bracing round the user; Frustration an anger mark popping and a flurry of blows; Mean Look a great dark eye opening over the target and purple rings closing in; Meditate calm rings and motes floating up round the user; Protect a pale hexagonal shield before the user, glinting; Reflect a wall of hexagonal panels before the user and a glint sweeping across; Safeguard a soft ring of light settling round the user, sparkles in it; Spite a pale wisp drawn out of the target, flickering; Night Shade a dark aura round the user and a ribbon of shadow to the target; Substitute a puff of smoke and a pop; Cut one long slash; Thief a hand darting in and back with a glint; Grass Knot blades of grass rising and tightening round the target's feet; Magical Leaf leaves spiralling in with sparkling trails; Razor Leaf spinning blades in a flat arc; Smokescreen a ball of smoke lobbed that bursts into a dark cloud; Sunny Day a sun over the field with rays and a warm glow (0.3); Recycle three arrows circling the user; Taunt a hand beckoning over the user and an anger mark over the target; Night Slash a dark crescent slashed across, then a second. Y7's second part (Route 206 to Route 208, thirty-nine moves, `MoveFx.Platinum3.cs`, batch `Y7b`, no new shape in the atlas): Double Team four pale afterimages sliding out either side of the user, swaying, and home again; Endeavor the user flaring yellow four times as four small blows land; Flame Wheel a wheel of flame winding up round the user, rolling across and breaking into fire on the target; Fury Cutter three cuts one after another, each longer and brighter, green scraps flying; Knock Off a fist driven up under the target's head and the thing it held popping off, spinning away; Magnitude the ground heaving in three jolts, cracking under the target and throwing up rocks, how hard being the seed's (the shake runs from 0.45 to 1); Roar (a look only; the sound is the user's cry) great arcs of voice rolling over the target and wind streaking past it and away; Seismic Toss streaks flinging the target up and bringing it down, the ground answering twice; Self-Destruct the user glowing hotter and swelling, sparks drawn in, then going off (flash 0.45); Explosion the same on a greater scale, a fireball and a pillar of smoke standing over the field; Slam a whipping tail ribbon coming over and down, a ring on the ground; Stomp a great foot dropped on the target from above, a shadow growing under it, held a moment; Block three slabs closing in left, right and over the target, locking with a clang; Imprison five bars dropped in turn in front of the target, a seal ringing the ground; Detect a bright eye opening over the user's head with rays and a halo pulsing twice; Mind Reader an eye over the target's head and a band of light scanning down its body twice; Disable the colour draining out of the target, a dark cross stamped on it and grey smoke sinking; Dragon Rage three ribbons of blue-violet fire weaving across and the blast they break into, shaking twice; Extrasensory three pulses of force sent over, each squeezing rings in on the target; Sand Tomb sand whirling up round the target in a narrowing spiral; Sandstorm gusts of sand streaking across the whole field and a haze drifting through; Tickle feathers brushing over the target in sways, sparkles and giggling waves; Uproar rings of noise spreading from the user again and again, notes bursting from it; Double Slap an open hand from the left, then one from the right; Follow Me a shaft of light on the user and stars popping over it three times; Sing three wavering lines of a stave from the user to the target with notes riding them, and Z's gathering on it; Bug Bite a pair of jaws snapping across the target twice, green juice flying; Encore five beats of a clap, each a ring and a flick of notes and stars, arrows circling the target; Flail the user thrashing in streaks of agitation, then a flurry of little blows; Ice Fang two great fangs of ice closing, frost and shards thrown off; Lucky Chant a golden ring turning on the ground round the user's side with stars riding it and a column of light; Mimic a pale ghost of the target lifting off it and floating across to the user; Rest a glow settling, a bubble swelling at the nose, Z's and little green crosses of healing; Revenge a dark red aura gathering, then a fist driven in trailing red; Rock Blast a volley of four rocks flung one after another, chips where each lands; Snore a big Z and great arcs of snore sent over, rumbling; Sweet Scent pink petals drifting across in lazy curves, a haze and sparkles on the target; Twister a funnel of wind standing up on the target, debris whirling in it; Worry Seed a seed lobbed high onto the target's head that splits, sweat flying and a small grey cloud gathering over it. Y8's first part (Hearthome City's gate to the Café Cabin on Route 210, fifty-one moves, `MoveFx.Platinum4.cs`, batch `Y8`, no new shape in the atlas; Y8's second part is `Y8b`): Bubble Beam a stream of bubbles with a body behind each to the target, which bob and sway round it before they pop; Mach Punch speed lines and a fist with three ghosts trailing it; Metal Claw the user glinting steel and two silver claws raked across the target, crossing; Future Sight an eye opening over the user and rings of foresight rolling to the foe, a haze turning over it; Pain Split red-pink sparks flowing each way, crossing, both glowing in turn and shuddering; Psybeam a ribbon of three wobbling colours with rings running down it and the target tinted purple; Shadow Ball shadow gathering into a dark orb that is thrown and bursts in a cloud; Shadow Claw a cloud of shadow thickening behind the target and two great ghostly claws raked across it; Shadow Sneak a dark streak along the ground and a shadowy hand striking up from under the target; Spit Up a great glob spat out and bursting in drops; Stockpile energy drawn in twice, a star kept over the head for each; Sucker Punch a dark fist from the target's blind side; Swallow the stored energy drawn into the mouth, a gulp and a healing light; Will-O-Wisp pale blue wisps circling the user, streaming across and setting the target alight; Minimize rings and arrows closing in on the user; Payback a dark gold aura, four heavy blows and coins flying; Attract a ring of pink hearts circling the target's head, more floating up; Amnesia thoughts drifting up out of the user's head as pale wisps; Aqua Ring a veil of water settling as a turning ring, bubbles riding it; Copycat twin trails spiralling from the foe to the user, which flashes gold; Fury Attack needles jabbing in from either side in quick turns; Jump Kick a foot in a high arc and down on the target, spinning, with ghosts of it behind; Light Screen a curtain of golden light rising before the user, shimmering, a glint running along it; Nasty Plot two red eyes narrowing over the user and dark wisps coiling up; Refresh a soft shower of light falling and washing through; Soft-Boiled a cream egg forming over the user, cracking, healing light spilling; Steel Wing two blades of steel crossing over the target with glints; Sweet Kiss a big heart popping over the head, small ones bursting from it and stars circling in a daze; Whirlwind a great wind swirling across the field and carrying the target with it, leaves and dust flying; Chatter (a look only; the sound is the user's cry) notes of every colour bursting from the mouth in a babble; Mirror Move (nothing of its own in the original) a mirror of silver light before the user, flashing; Haze a grey mist rolling across the whole field; Defog a bright wind sweeping the field, shoving grey cloud before it; Dig dirt spurting at the user's feet, a mound running along the ground and the user bursting up under the target; Yawn a drowsy cloud breathed out and drifting to the target, Z's rising; Acupressure three points of the body pressed in turn, each pinging a ring; Air Cutter two crescents of wind crossing on the target; False Swipe one clean pale slash pulled at the last, ending in a soft ring and stars; Feint two false thrusts flickering in before the true one; Metronome a pendulum swinging beside the user, ticking, and a ring of every colour popping where it settles; Pin Missile three needles lobbed one after another, each sinking in with a flash; Roost white feathers floating down onto the user and a healing light; Scary Face a hideous face snapping up over the target, two red eyes and a row of fangs, flickering; Shock Wave sparks gathering and a crooked bolt jumping straight across, crackling over the target; Slash three quick bright cuts, the last the longest, a flare at the end of each; Swagger the user puffing up in a ring of stars, the target stung red with an anger mark popping twice; Thunder Shock a small hard crack of electricity on the target, forks of it leaping off; Toxic Spikes five black-purple spikes lobbed to the foe's side and standing in the ground, a poison puff at each; Wake-Up Slap four quick slaps from alternate sides, the last waking it with a bang and Z's flying off; Cosmic Power a night sky closing round the user, stars drifting up through it and three small worlds in orbit; Lick a long tongue sweeping up across the target, leaving it slimed. **The darkened background** that the original's scripts ask for in many of these (Charge, Thunderbolt, Swift, Hidden Power, Curse, Mean Look, Spite, Night Shade, String Shot) is not drawn: a dim over the whole picture would darken the Pokémon and the effects with it, and the stage's own dim is a renderer's change of its own (plan 25, Y7's owed list).
- **Colours by type**: Fire `255,128,36`, Water `64,146,255`, Electric `255,222,50`, Grass `96,196,72`, Ice `140,218,255`, Fighting `236,110,50`, Poison `172,78,210`, Ground `206,164,92`, Flying `176,206,255`, Psychic `255,92,176`, Bug `168,204,40`, Rock `184,158,110`, Ghost `132,92,210`, Dragon `112,96,250`, Dark `110,80,120`, Steel `196,208,228`, Fairy `255,150,214`, Normal `250,246,226`; each with a light colour for cores and sparks and a dark one for matter in shade.
- **Timing**: the impact lands 0.35 s in, with the damage; effects end by 1.3 s. A miss flies wide and past and lands nothing; a move that does nothing ends in a puff of smoke; a critical hit lands bigger with a white flash and a ring; a super-effective hit lands bigger with a second ring and a harder shake.
- **Screen flashes** at most 0.5 strong and under half a second (Thunder, Hyper Beam, Fire Blast, Blizzard, critical hits, Explosion); Sunny Day's warm glow is softer and longer.
- **With the scene off** (plan 08 · P1, the options' Battle Scene; `BattleEngine.Scene`): as Platinum's switch has it (`BattleSystem_AreAnimationsOn`), nothing on this list plays but Transform's shape. No lunge and no doll stepping aside; no move's effect, no attack sent ahead landing; no stat's streaks, no heal's motes, no burst of a new status, none of the turn's moments, no weather's cue, no dodge, no cheer and no charge's crouch or aura (plan 25 · Y5: a charge's crouch goes with the lunge it stands in for); and none of their sounds. Tiredness stays: it is a state, as a condition is. What stays: the move's own sound, a hit's flicker, shake and sound (a critical hit's flash and ring are its effect's, and go with it), every send-out, recall, faint and ball, the Substitute's doll and its swaps, the weather's layer (it eases in with no cue), what lies on the field and what lasts on a Pokémon. What a line carries lands as the line appears, not 0.35 s in, so the HP bar drains with the text; the lines and their order are the rules' either way.
- **Marks on a Pokémon**: a stat rising sends warm streaks up round it, a stat falling cool streaks down; a heal is green motes and rings rising; a new status shows as flames (burn), purple bubbles (poison), crackling bolts (paralysis), drifting Z's (sleep) or a burst of ice (freeze).
  - **The turn's moments** (plan 25 · Y3; `MoveFx.Moments.cs`): what the end of a turn and the checks before a move do to a Pokémon, each a mark of its own, so the turn can be read from the picture: who is poisoned, who is seeded and by whom, which item went off. The list is the original's battle animations that are not moves (`BATTLE_ANIMATION_…`), and each is named after its original; **every look is our own**. A moment plays on the line that tells of it (the log's `MomentShown`), from the overview, for 1.1 s; it lands 0.35 s in, like a move, and a moment that hurts or heals **lands its HP then**: the bar drains or fills with no flinch of the body, no shake of the camera, no sound of a hit and no green aura of a heal, as the original skips the sprite's blink after one (`SYSCTL_SKIP_SPRITE_BLINK`). None flashes the screen: the turn's end is several of them in a row. Sizes are in the Pokémon's height *h*.
    - **Read from the overview, on any body.** Three rules the first pictures taught:
      - *A cast is matter.* The tint a moment lays over a body as it bites (purple for poison, pale blue for ice, red for a temper) is alpha-blended in its own colour: light added to a brown body only brightens it.
      - *What goes round a body is split at its middle.* The camera stands on the +Z side of the field, so whatever circles a Pokémon is drawn over its body while on that side of it and depth-tested behind it: flames, rocks, drops and grains whirl round a body of any width, where depth-testing all of them hid every one that a round body's own bulk covered. A band is three arcs for the same reason: the one toward the camera over the body, lit; the two behind hidden by it, and at half strength and unlit where a body narrower than the band lets them show, so they read as behind.
      - *Nothing lies flat for the far platform.* The lens sees the foe's platform at about 7°, so a ring or a swirl lying on the ground there is a thread. A band dips toward the camera (its plane tilted 29°), and a whirl on the ground has two more standing up in it, facing the lens, low round the legs.
    - *As each plays on its turn*: **asleep**, three Z's (`210,226,255`) drifting up from its head over a slow breath of pale light; **poisoned**, nine purple bubbles (0.07 to 0.11 *h*) rising through it that burst into rings as the poison bites, under a purple cast; **burned**, eight flames licking up its body and embers thrown off as the burn bites; **frozen**, eight shards of ice closing in round it and locking with a small glint under a pale blue cast; **paralysed**, three fits of bolts across its body with sparks jumping off; **confused**, three yellow stars (`255,226,90`) circling over its head under a turning swirl (the same mark as it becomes confused); **infatuated**, four pink hearts (`255,120,170`) rising from its head, swaying.
    - *What the turn's end takes or gives*: **curse**, a dark nail (`96,60,140`) driven down into it from above, wisps and a ring thrown out as it strikes; **nightmare**, a dark cloud of three puffs (`34,22,52`) over its head, six wisps circling it and closing in, and two red points of light in the cloud as it bites; **Leech Seed**, three leaves sprouting at its feet and twelve motes (a pale core in a green halo, `120,255,110`) leaving its body one after another for the Pokémon in the place the seed was sown from, in a low arc, with a glint where they arrive; **Ingrain**, six brown roots (`176,130,80` and `110,76,44`) growing in over the ground from 0.85 *h* out and up into its feet, lights running along them, while two rings close on it over the ground and sparks rise through a green cast.
    - *The seven binds*, each its move's own grip: **Bind**, two tan bands (`214,186,140`) tightening round its body, holding a beat and letting go, with a few stars as they bite; **Wrap**, three green coils (`120,170,80`) tilted one against the next; **Fire Spin**, a ring of fire on the ground and eighteen flames whirling round it and rising; **Magma Storm**, glowing rocks and flames whirling wider, embers thrown up from a red glow on the ground; **Clamp**, two pale arcs of shell (`186,206,226`) snapping shut on it from either side with a spark; **Whirlpool**, a blue swirl turning under it and two standing in it round its legs, drops running round its body and a splash as it bites; **Sand Tomb**, the same whirl in sand (`236,212,150`), drawing grains in and down round its legs under low dust.
    - *Items and a level*: **a held item** going off (where the original plays its held-item animation: Leftovers, a berry eaten, a Focus Sash, a Quick Claw…) shows just over its holder's head (1.1 *h*): a berry as a small round fruit (`236,84,110`, about a quarter of *h* across) with a dark leaf (`40,132,52`), popping up and bitten away into crumbs; anything else as a gold ring (`255,226,130`) closing on a glint. **A bag item** used on a Pokémon on the field is a glint over its head and pale motes (`214,244,255`) falling through it onto a ring at its feet. **An escape item** is a burst of pale smoke at the feet of the player's Pokémon, a second puff rolling up through it, and a few stars. **A level** is gold light (`255,232,140`) rising round the Pokémon: streaks, a ring climbing from its feet to its head and three stars popping over it, under the level's panel, which stands over the line.
    - *The Great Marsh*: **happy** (bait lands), three yellow notes hopping up from its head, each in a pale halo so it reads against leaves, with two sparkles; **eating**, a morsel of bait (`222,176,112`, rimmed `140,96,56`) over its head, where a held berry shows too (before its own body a morsel was lost on a brown one, and nobody can say where a body's mouth is), a third of it gone with each of three bites: a pale crescent snaps into it from one side and then the other, and pale crumbs fall from each; **angry** (mud lands), a red cast over its face, three puffs of steam off its head to either side and a jagged red mark (dark under bright) beating beside it.
    - **An attack sent ahead landing** (Future Sight, Doom Desire) plays its move's own cue in its landing half, from the place it was sent from (the log's `AheadLanded`): a glint there, then for Future Sight three rings of its type's light falling onto the target from that side and closing on it, for Doom Desire a pillar of pale light out of the sky with a flash of 0.25; then the move's own landing. The camera goes to the target at once: nobody need stand where it was sent from.
    - **What shows behind a doll** holds for these as it does for the other marks: none plays on a Pokémon behind a Substitute or out of sight, but the escape item's and a level's, and Leech Seed's plays only if both its ends can be seen. Each is a pure function of its cue's age and seed and draws at most 40 quads and ribbons (`MoveFx.MomentCap`). Sounds: *docs/sound-effects.md*, "Battles".
  - **A condition that lasts** (plan 09 · L2; `MoveFx.Conditions.cs`): a Pokémon that has a condition shows it for as long as it has it, not only as it is given and as it bites: at every menu, through every line, for turns on end. **Every look is our own**: between its moments Platinum shows a condition only as the letters on the HP box. So each is small and slow and keeps to the edge of the body or just over its head: it must never cover the Pokémon or its HP box, and it must read from the overview on both platforms. Sizes are in the Pokémon's height *h*.
    - *The body's own* (the pose and the shader; no quads): **asleep**, its eyes are shut; **frozen**, it is held still at one moment of its idle loop (no breath, no sway, no blink) under an ice-blue cast (`150,214,255`, 40 % over its own colours, shimmering by 4 %); **poisoned**, a purple cast (`172,78,210`) pulses over it, to 24 % and away over 0.9 s, every 2.4 s; **badly poisoned** deepens it, a darker violet (`128,48,176`) to 34 % every 1.5 s; **paralysed**, a yellow glint (`255,232,90`, 22 %) for a tenth of a second as each fit of sparks begins. A cast is blended into the model's own colours by the shader's flash, so it is matter and keeps its colour on any body (light added to a brown body only brightens it), and it gives way to the flash of a send-out, a recall and a wild Pokémon's silhouette.
    - *Beside this body*. A body's height says little of its width: a Bidoof is as wide as it is tall, an Empoleon a quarter as wide, and marks set out by height alone sat inside the one and floated a body's width off the other. So the battle measures each Pokémon as it is turned to the lens (`FxPlaces.HalfWidth`: half its silhouette's width *w*, from the sprite framing of that view, kept between a fifth and three fifths of its height so a tail or a pair of wings doesn't send things far off), and what stands beside a body stands off its middle by *w*: it hugs a narrow body and clears a wide one. What is over its head goes by *h*, and the ring round its head by both.
    - *The marks about it*, each a loop in its own age:
      - **asleep**: three Z's (`214,228,255`, each over a shadow of its own, `70,96,170`, a sixth of its size down and to the right, so a pale letter reads on a pale sky and on leaves) rising one after another from beside its head, up and to its right: from just outside the ring a confused head's stars keep to, at 1.0 *h*, to 0.26 *h* further right at 1.34 *h*, growing from 0.06 to 0.11 *h*, one every 0.8 s, each 2.4 s on its way.
      - **poisoned**: four bubbles (0.07 to 0.1 *h*: a purple body, `150,62,190` at three fifths' strength, under a pale rim and glint, since a bubble that is only its rim is lost on leaves) rising at the edge of its body, 0.65 to 1 *w* either side of its middle, from 0.3 to 1.1 *h*, wobbling, each 1.9 s on its way and gone at the top in a small ring. **Badly poisoned**: six, darker (`104,36,150` under a rim of `206,120,236`), each 1.3 s on its way.
      - **burned**: four small flames (0.16 to 0.2 *h* across their quads, the flame itself half of that: an orange body `255,128,36` that is matter, with a pale heart added) on its flanks (0.95 *w* out, at 0.42 and 0.5 *h*), a shoulder (0.62 *w*, 0.8 *h*) and its head (0.35 *w*, 0.99 *h*), each licking up 0.08 *h* and starting again twice a second, flickering.
      - **paralysed**: every 1.7 s a fit of a quarter of a second: two short crooked bolts across its body, 0.05 *h* wide (yellow matter `255,222,50` with a white core added), within 0.95 *w* of its middle and 0.42 *h* of its waist, and three sparks jumping off; nothing between fits.
      - **frozen**: a shell of ice: six shards (0.15 to 0.21 *h*) standing round its feet on a ring 1.05 *w* out, two more (0.14 *h*) leaning off its shoulders at 0.95 *w* and 0.58 *h*, pale blue (`140,218,255` and `236,252,255`) at four fifths' strength so the body shows through them; those behind it are hidden by it. A glint pops on one of them every 1.3 s.
      - **confused**: three yellow stars (`255,226,90` on a rim of `176,120,20`, 0.075 *h*) circling its head at 1.08 *h*, once round in 2.4 s, on a ring 0.8 *w* out (never under 0.2 nor over 0.32 *h*) and two fifths as deep; behind the head they are hidden by it.
      - **infatuated**: two pink hearts (`255,120,170` on a rim of `196,60,120`, 0.07 to 0.095 *h*) rising one after the other from over its head, to its left: 0.1 to 0.26 *h* left of its middle, from 1.1 to 1.34 *h*, swaying, each 2 s on its way.
    - *Sharing the Pokémon*. A condition, confusion and love are three marks and can show at once: the Z's keep to its right, the hearts to its left, the stars round its head. **A moment or a mark given plays alone**: while a turn's moment, a condition being given, a stat's change or a heal plays on a Pokémon, the marks about it stand back (out over 0.15 s, back over the cue's last quarter of a second), and the body's own (the shut eyes, the hold, the cast) stays. So poison's bite is the moment's nine bubbles and their rings, and afterwards the four go on rising; a Pokémon put to sleep shows the drifting Z's of its falling asleep, and then the three that last.
    - *Coming and going*. A mark eases in over 0.4 s from the line that tells of the condition and out over 0.4 s on the line that says it is over: a cure, waking, a thaw, snapping out of confusion, the one loved leaving the field. A faint and a recall take it with the Pokémon. A Pokémon that comes in with a condition (asleep on the bench, poisoned before the battle) shows it from the line that sends it out, once it is out of its ball; a confusion handed on by Baton Pass comes in the same way.
    - *What isn't shown*: no mark about a Pokémon behind a Substitute's doll, out of sight in the middle of a move, still in its ball, fainting, being called back or drawn into a ball, as for every other mark on a Pokémon (the original's `BattleSystem_ShouldShowStatusEffect`). The body's own shows wherever the body does.
    - The looks are set by the log's events (`StatusChanged`, `VolatileChanged`, `CameInWith`, a send-out, a recall, a faint), never read from the rules, and brought level with them when a menu opens, with nothing played. Each is a pure function of its age, how far it has come in and a seed from the looks' own count, and none flashes the screen or shakes the camera. Caps, in quads and ribbons (`MoveFx.ConditionCap`): asleep 6, poisoned 8, badly poisoned 12, burned 8, paralysed 7, frozen 10, confused 6, infatuated 4; a Pokémon with a condition, confused and in love at once draws 22 at the most, against a moment's 40.

### Poké Ball (G8)

- **A 3D model** (`BattleBall`): two hollow halves sculpted with the SDF kit, hinged at the back, with a dark band round the seam and a white button in a dark ring at the front, glossy (`Plastic`), outlined like the Pokémon. Kinds by colour: Poké Ball red over white; Great Ball blue with red patches either side; Ultra Ball black with a yellow H; Master Ball purple with two pink domes and a white M; Premier Ball white with a red band; the others in their own colours with a stripe or spots.
- **Thrown by the trainer**, overarm (the `Throw` emote lets go 0.3 s in). At a send-out the trainer throws it onto the platform and steps aside; it opens in a white burst and the Pokémon grows out of the light. Later send-outs come in from the trainer's side of the field.
- **A capture**: the player runs in at the left of their platform and throws; the ball spins end over end along a high arc, pops open over the foe and draws it in with red light, shuts, drops onto the platform with a bounce, wobbles once per successful shake check with a pause after each, and then clicks shut with three stars (it stays on the platform) or bursts open in white light as the Pokémon breaks out.

- **Each its own ball** (plan 25 · Y4). A Pokémon comes out of, and goes back into, the ball it was caught in (`Pokemon.Ball`); one never caught, a trainer's, an Egg's hatchling or a ball the model has no kind for (the Park Ball) is in a Poké Ball, as Platinum makes a trainer's (plan 25, decision 5). The model has sixteen kinds: the fifteen above and the Cherish Ball, red over black with a gold band and gold stripe (`200,40,50`, `50,40,50`, `240,200,80`).
- **The release** (`BattleBall.ReleaseOf`, `BallRelease.Emit`): every ball opens in the flash, ring and fourteen sparks the Poké Ball has always had (white `255,255,255`, light `255,246,200`: unchanged for it), tinted from its look, with matter of its own added, at most 28 quads more, all gone by 0.6 s after the lid opens:
  | Ball | Flash / light | Its own |
  |---|---|---|
  | Poké | white / cream | nothing |
  | Great | pale blue / blue | blue sparks flung out as streaks |
  | Ultra | pale gold / gold | gold stars and a few puffs of black smoke |
  | Master | lilac / violet | a violet swirl ring and pink stars |
  | Premier | white / red | a second, red ring and falling white feathers |
  | Safari | pale green / green | green leaves falling |
  | Net | teal / deep blue | a lattice of crosses spreading out |
  | Dive | pale blue / sea blue | bubbles rising |
  | Nest | yellow-green / green | yellow and green leaves drifting down |
  | Repeat | orange / yellow | two more rings, each after the last |
  | Timer | white / red | two slow rings and ticking dots |
  | Luxury | gold / gold | gold motes rising and glints |
  | Dusk | violet / orange | dark motes rising and one orange glint |
  | Heal | pink / white | hearts rising |
  | Quick | yellow / blue | fast streaks flung out |
  | Cherish | red / gold | red petals and white stars |
  The release is a pure function of the ball's look, the time since the lid opened and a seed. A Pokémon that breaks out of a thrown ball bursts in that ball's release and keeps that ball.
- **The recall** (`BallRecall`, 0.9 s, `BattleAnimator.RecallTime`): the Pokémon's own ball flies in from where the next send-out would come from (the far side of its platform, the trainer having stepped off), growing, to a little over the Pokémon's middle; the lid swings open; a red beam of three ribbons and a red glow draw the Pokémon in as it shrinks (over the middle third); the lid shuts; the ball flies out the way it came, shrinking, and is off the field by the end. It then takes the Pokémon's ball's look, so a Luxury Ball takes it back in gold and black.

### Weather in battle (plan 25 · Y1)

- **What lasts while the weather does** (`ArenaFx.Weather`), eased in over a second as the weather begins and out over a second as it stops, in the scene and behind whatever stands in front of it:
  - *Rain*: thin streaks falling steeply, slanted a little toward the camera, rings spreading where drops land on both platforms and on the ground between them, and a faint grey veil over the far field.
  - *Harsh sun*: warm motes drifting up through the light, soft haze over the platforms and a glare in the upper left of the sky.
  - *Sandstorm*: streaks blown across the field from left to right, low and fast, with clouds of dust rolling through them.
  - *Hail*: stones falling fast and bouncing once off the platforms and the ground, small and white with a blue cast.
  - *Fog*: three low layers of soft cloud drifting at different speeds, thickest at the platforms' height.
- **The cue** (`ArenaFx.WeatherCue`, 1.2 s) plays over the layer as the weather begins and again on each turn it goes on (the turn's line, "Rain continues to fall.", carries the log's `WeatherContinues`, as the original plays the weather's animation every turn): a heavier shower splashing on both platforms; a warm flash with rays slanting down; a wall of sand sweeping across; a flurry of hail; fog rolling in from the left.
- **The light** (`ArtLook.InBattleWeather`): rain, hail, a sandstorm and fog take the light of the field's weather they come from (`Weathers.FromBattle`, the way back from `Weathers.InBattle`: rain as the field's rain, hail as its hail), and the sky is drawn toward a grey of its own brightness (rain and hail cool, the sandstorm ochre, fog pale) with the stars hidden. The harsh sun, which the field has no weather for, has a rig of its own (`ArtLook.HarshSun`): the key 34 % stronger and warmer, warm light from the sky and ground, bloom +0.18 on a threshold 0.08 lower, a paler, warmer sky. A weather clearing and the next coming in are blended by how far each has come.
- **The later games' weathers** (plan 06 · R19b), our own looks like the rest:
  - *Snow* (hail's place under the modern rules): white flakes (`246,250,255`) 0.08 to 0.15 units across drifting down slowly through rain's box, each swaying as it falls and never bouncing; its cue is a quicker flurry of them; its light is hail's, its sound hail's.
  - *Extremely harsh sun*: the harsh sun's layer with a second, whiter glare over the sun's and a pale warm wash breathing over the far ground; its light, cue and sound are the harsh sun's.
  - *Heavy rain*: rain's layer twice over, the second a little fainter and out of step; its light hides more of the sky (85 %) than rain's; its cue and sound are rain's.
  - *Strong winds*: long pale gusts (`236,246,252`) streaming across the field from the left at every height the lens takes in, with a few wisps curling as they go; the sky only thinned (35 %); its sound is a sandstorm's.
- **Sounds**: each weather has its own as it begins and as it goes on (`weather_rain`, `weather_sun`, `weather_sandstorm`, `weather_hail`, `weather_fog`; *docs/sound-effects.md*).
- Everything is a pure function of the weather's age, the battle's clock and the cue's seed: nothing is simulated.
- **A streak must be wide enough to see** (plan 25 · Y2, the first session to look at these on a screen): the streak's shape shows a line about a sixth of its quad's width, so at their first numbers rain was under a pixel wide at 1080p and a sandstorm a dozen threads. Rain's streaks are drawn 2.5 times those widths and a sandstorm's and hail's twice, each at its own length (`ArenaFx.WeatherStreak`): rain about two to four pixels wide near the camera, sand three to six. And they fall **where the lens looks**: rain in a box of 23 by 6 by 33 units from the camera to the far trees (`ArenaFx.RainMin`, `RainMax`), a sandstorm's sand in one of 26 by 4.5 by 32, where the sky's whole box is four times the rain's and left a handful of streaks in the picture.

### The Substitute (plan 25 · Y1)

- **The doll** (`SubstituteDoll`) is our own small figure, not the original's: a plush doll of undyed cloth (`226,210,176`, its feet and ears a shade darker) with a round head, two nubs of ears, stub arms, button eyes, two cheeks of thread, a patch on its belly (`196,118,92`) and a seam stitched down its front and over its head. It is sculpted with the SDF kit in `Cloth`, meshed once and cached like the Poké Ball, preloaded with it, and outlined like the Pokémon. It stands three quarters of its platform's radius tall (times the double battle's fit), a little in front of its Pokémon's spot, facing the other side.
- **Up** (0.7 s): the Pokémon shrinks out of sight over the first 45 % while the doll falls from above and lands with a squash and a ring of dust.
- **Standing**: it breathes (a slow squash of 3 %). A hit that lands on it makes it flinch: a tilt back and a jolt with a few stars (0.45 s). For its owner's own move it slides aside, the Pokémon grows back to make the move, and the two swap back once the move has played.
- **Down** (0.6 s): it whitens and shrinks into a puff of smoke while the Pokémon grows back. A doll passed by Baton Pass fades with the Pokémon that leaves and drops in again once the one that comes in is out of its ball.
- **What shows behind a doll** (the original's `BattleSystem_ShouldShowStatusEffect`): a mark on the Pokémon (a stat's change, a heal, a condition given) isn't played while a doll stands in front of it or it is out of sight; the weather's cue and a move's own effect, which lands on the doll, play as ever.
- The doll is put up and taken down by the log's lines (`SubstituteChanged`), never read from the rules; its looks are pure functions of its ages and the animator's seed. Sounds: `substitute_in`, `substitute_out`.

### What lies on the field (plan 25 · Y2)

Platinum draws a hazard, a screen or Trick Room only as its move is used, and afterwards only lines that have scrolled away tell of them. Here each is drawn for as long as it lasts. **All of these looks are our own**: none is the original's, and none is taken from a later game.

- **Small and low, never over a body.** Everything lies on a platform or the ground, stands low on its rim, or hangs above a Pokémon's head. On the arc of a rim that faces the camera, where a thing comes between the lens and the Pokémon, nothing stands higher than a ninth of the platform's radius (the panes, which reach the feet and no further); what stands taller (the stones, the charms, a puff of mist) keeps to the rest of the rim, 65° clear of the camera either way. All of it sits in the scene (depth-tested), so a body hides what is behind it. Sizes are in the platform's radius *R* (the player's 1.2, the foe's 2.4), so both sides read the same on screen.
- **Matter keeps its colour, light is added.** A caltrop, a stone, a pane's glass, the grid's lines, a star and a mote are alpha-blended in their own colour, because light alone washes out to white over a bright field; their edges, glints and halos are light added on top.
- **What lies in wait**, on the side it was scattered on:
  - *Spikes*: one ring of steel caltrops (`196,204,220`, their crossed point `120,128,150`) round the rim for each layer, at 0.95, 0.84 and 0.73 *R*, ten to a ring, each 0.06 *R* tall. A new layer falls from above, each caltrop in its own moment, and settles with a hop; fading, they sink into the ground.
  - *Toxic Spikes*: the same caltrops in purple (`186,120,236`, their crossed point `120,64,160`), in rings between Spikes' at 0.895 and 0.785 *R*, with bubbles of poison (`214,150,255`) rising from three of them at a time.
  - *Stealth Rock*: five pointed stones (`168,150,122`, a splinter of `196,180,150` standing out of each) hovering 0.22 to 0.39 *R* over the platform's top, just outside the rim at 1.08 *R*, on the flanks and the far side, each bobbing and turning on its own slow rhythm over a blot of shade.
- **Screens and veils**, on the side that put them up:
  - *Reflect*: a low pane of warm glass (`255,196,110`) along the 100° of the rim that face the other side, from the platform's top to 0.05 *R*, with a bright upper edge and a glint sliding along it. It rises from its foot and spreads from its middle as it comes in.
  - *Light Screen*: the same pane in cool glass (`140,214,255`), standing on Reflect's: from 0.055 to 0.105 *R*. With both up the two make one pane of two colours.
  - *Safeguard*: a pale green ring (`190,255,214`) lying round the platform's foot at 1.12 *R*, breathing slowly, with motes rising a hand's breadth from it. A veil is drawn as its foot: a dome over the Pokémon would cover it.
  - *Mist*: wisps of white mist lying flat round the rim in two thin layers that drift opposite ways, and four low puffs standing on the rim clear of the camera.
  - *Tailwind*: pale streaks of wind (`214,236,255`) running low along the ground past both flanks of the platform, from behind the side toward the other.
  - *Lucky Chant*: three small charms, pink stars (`255,186,224`), hovering just over the rim on the flanks and the far side, bobbing, each with a twinkle now and then.
- **The rooms**:
  - *Trick Room*: a grid of violet lines (`214,120,255`) on the ground under the whole field, seven each way, rising as walls 3.2 units high behind the foe's platform and at both sides, with two rails along each. No wall stands between the camera and the field. Each line is a line of the world from end to end (a ribbon, not a sprite turned to the camera), so the grid runs away from the lens as the ground does; the lines grow out from the middle as it comes in, and a wave passes along them while it lasts.
  - *Gravity*: the stage's light is lowered (`ArtLook.UnderGravity`: the key and both ambients to 84 %, the shadows drawn a little toward violet, the vignette 0.08 stronger), and short violet-grey streaks (`170,150,210`) sink to the ground round both platforms, faster as they fall.
- **Sent ahead**, over the place it waits for:
  - *A Wish*: a small gold star (`255,226,110`) in a soft halo over the head of whoever stands there, a little to the left, bobbing, its rays turning slowly.
  - *Future Sight*: a mote of violet (`214,96,240`) beside the star's place, circling on the spot, a ring leaving it every second and a half. *Doom Desire* is the same in pale steel (`206,214,236`) with a cross of light.
- **The later games'** (plan 06 · R19b):
  - *Aurora Veil*: two curtains of the northern lights, green (`120,255,196`) under pink (`236,150,255`), hanging over the rim on the flanks and the far side, 0.3 and 0.46 *R* over the top and 0.42 and 0.3 *R* deep, rippling, with glints running down them; they rise into being as it comes in. Like the stones, they keep 65° clear of the camera.
  - *Sticky Web*: a web of silver threads (`226,230,240`) lying just over the platform's top, eight spokes out to the rim and two rings sagging between them at 0.42 and 0.78 *R*, dew glinting along a thread now and then. A body hides what is under it; the spokes run out from the middle as it comes in.
  - *A terrain*, on both platforms at once, one terrain at a time: a wash of its colour lying on the top and a low band of it standing round the far rim (0.12 *R* deep: a wash alone is a thread on the foe's platform), and its own pieces on the far rim. *Electric* (`255,236,110`): sparks leaping for a moment along the ground. *Grassy* (`96,196,92`): blades of grass swaying. *Misty* (`255,176,226`): puffs of pink mist drifting round, with sparkles rising. *Psychic* (`196,110,255`): rings spreading over the top and violet motes rising from the rim.
  - The whole field with every kind up at once, Aurora Veil and Sticky Web on both sides and the heaviest terrain among them, draws at most 479 quads and ribbons (`BattleFieldFx.MaxQuads`).
- **Coming and going.** A look comes in over 0.6 s from the line that tells of it (the move's own effect plays on the line before) and fades over 0.5 s on the line that says it is gone: a screen wearing off, Brick Break, Defog, Rapid Spin, a Poison type taking up the Toxic Spikes, a Wish coming true, an attack sent ahead landing. Where the rules drop a Wish or an attack sent ahead with no line (nobody stands in its place), an event between lines takes it down.
- The looks are set by the log's events (`SideConditionChanged`, `FieldConditionChanged`, `PlaceConditionChanged`), never read from the rules, and brought level with them when a menu opens; each is a pure function of its ages, the battle's clock and a seed from the animator, and none draws more than its cap of quads (`BattleFieldFx.Cap`). No pass and no render target is added: the panes and the grid are quads and ribbons of the effects' own atlas.

### Light, shadow and grading (day)

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Sun | normalize(−0.5, 0.9, 0.5), colour 0.47, 0.43, 0.35 |
| Sky / ground ambient | 0.54, 0.60, 0.72 / 0.50, 0.50, 0.40 |
| Shadows and cloud shade | as in the field; cloud shade 16 % |
| Fog | aerial perspective toward the sky's horizon colour, 35 % at the hills (from 40 to 140 units) |
| Ambient occlusion | radius 0.6 units, strength 0.6: contact shade under Pokémon, tufts and tree crowns |
| Stage outlines | drawn where depth steps, on the near side only, 1 px wide, darkening the surface by up to 35 % (inverse depth is flat across the ground, so only silhouettes and creases fire) |
| Tilt-shift | radius 2 outside ±50 %; no wide blur |
| Bloom | threshold 0.86, strength 0.28 |
| Grade | saturation 1.05, contrast 1.05, shadows × (0.94, 0.97, 1.06), highlights × (1.04, 1.01, 0.95), vignette 0.10 |

## Time of day

The world follows the computer's clock, like the DS's real-time clock, with Platinum's five light states (pret/pokeplatinum, `src/rtc.c`). The options screen can fix a time. Each state has a light rig for the field and one for battles (`ArtLook.FieldRigFor`, `BattleRigFor`); within half an hour of a change the two rigs blend.

| Time | Hours | Field light | Field grade and extras | Battle sky (zenith → horizon) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Morning | 4–9 | pale gold sun, lower in the sky; soft blue shade | mist (fog 32 %), gentler contrast | `104,150,214` → peach `246,224,206` |
| Day | 10–16 | golden sun, violet-blue shade | as in the tables above | `78,146,226` → `224,238,250` |
| Twilight | 17–19 | low orange sun `0.90,0.56,0.32`, magenta-violet shade, long shadows | more bloom and saturation; lamps come on (60 %) and windows start to glow (45 %), their pools still faint | `54,62,128` → orange `252,168,108`, pink clouds |
| Night | 20–23 | cool moonlight `0.27,0.31,0.44` | saturation 0.80, vignette 0.40; street lamps, windows and glass doors fully lit, warm light pools on the ground under them, bloom from the lit glass | `8,14,38` → `40,58,100`, stars, dark clouds |
| Late night | 0–3 | dimmer moonlight | saturation 0.72, vignette 0.45; the lamps, shops and signs burn on, but most homes have gone dark | `4,8,26` → `28,42,80`, stars |

- Lit glass is marked in the art, texel by texel (see *Buildings*, "Lights after dark"): it turns to warm lamplight `255,204,117`, scaled by the texel's own brightness, by the rig's amounts for public lights and for homes.
- Night battles stay a little brighter than the night field and gain a stronger rim light (0.55), so Pokémon read clearly.
- Light never drops to black: the darkest ambient is about 0.15, and nights lean blue, never grey.

## Resolution (4K)

- The game's screen is **3840×2160**. Everything is still laid out in **1920×1080 layout units** (`GameEngine.VirtualWidth/Height`); the screen is drawn at `RenderScale = 2` real pixels per unit, so every size in this guide is in layout units unless it says otherwise.
- The interface is drawn through a ×2 camera straight into the 4K screen: text comes from large font atlases and shapes from signed distances, so both are sharp at 4K. Edges are anti-aliased over one real pixel, not one layout unit (`UiShapes.PixelScale`).
- Pixel art keeps its whole-number scales: a field texel is about 6 real pixels, a party icon texel is 8.
- The window shows the 4K screen scaled to fit. Full screen on a 4K display shows it 1:1; a smaller window downsamples it, which also anti-aliases it. The first run picks the largest listed window size the monitor can show.
- The full-screen window is one row taller than the display (`WindowSettings.FullscreenSize`). A borderless OpenGL window that matches the monitor exactly is taken over by the graphics driver as exclusive full screen and flickers on NVIDIA cards.

## Quality presets

| Preset | 3D scene resolution | Anti-aliasing | Shadow taps | Shadow map | Ambient occlusion | Wide depth of field |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| High (default) | 3840×2160 | FXAA in a window wider than 2880; in a smaller one the downscale to the window does it | 9 | 2048 | yes | yes |
| Medium | 2880×1620 | FXAA in a window wider than 2160 | 4 | 2048 | yes | yes |
| Low | 1920×1080 | FXAA in a window wider than 1440 | 1 | 1024 | no | no |

The interface is always drawn at 4K; the presets change only the 3D scenes. FXAA is for a window that shows the scene at more than three quarters of its size: a smaller window smooths the scene by scaling it down, and FXAA would cost half a millisecond for nothing. Options are saved in `settings.json`.

A frame must stay under 8 ms on High. In the harness's 1080p window after G11 (plan 04): towns and routes 5.0 to 6.6 ms, a room 3.6 ms, battles 6.0 to 7.5 ms with a move's effect at its height; Medium takes 0.4 to 1.1 ms less and Low 1 to 2 ms less. One millisecond of each of those is the hidden window's own swap of its buffers (three in a window as large as a 4K display, where FXAA adds 0.4 more), and readings move by two or three tenths between runs. The harness's `profile` mode says where a frame goes; what was learned from it:

- A frame is spent filling 4K, not on geometry: a few hundred thousand triangles and a hundred or two meshes cost a tenth of what shading every pixel does. An effect is weighed by what it adds per pixel.
- A 4K texture is slow to read at scattered places. What needs the picture or its depth small (the wide blur, the glow, ambient occlusion) reads a half-resolution copy made once a frame.
- A scene seen from low down (a battle's stage) lays its depth down before it is shaded, so each pixel is shaded once.

## Interface (vector)

### Typography

- **Nunito** (SIL Open Font License, see `CREDITS.md`) in three weights: Bold for running text, ExtraBold for labels and dialogue, Black for names, numbers and buttons.
- Scale (layout units): 18–20 captions and tags · 24–26 small labels · 30–32 numbers and move names · 36–42 names, prompts and dialogue · 52 screen titles · 60 the FIGHT button.
- Tracking +0.8 up to 20 px, +0.4 up to 39 px, 0 above. Level reads as a small muted "Lv" before a large number.
- No drop shadows on text over panels; white text on coloured buttons gets a 2 px shadow at 20 % black.
- **Beyond Latin-1** (plan 17 · N5, the Latin half): a character is drawn as itself if Nunito has a glyph for it, in the same weights, size and tracking as the rest of the line. The first face is Latin-1 as it always was (English and every text within it pay nothing). A **second face** of the same typeface and atlas size (160 px, mipmapped) holds Latin Extended-A (Romanian ă â î and the cedilla ş ţ, Polish, Czech, Turkish, Hungarian, the Baltic languages, French œ), the comma-below letters Ș ș Ț ț that Romanian proper uses, the curly and low quotation marks „ ” ‘ ’ and the guillemets, the euro and the trade mark; it is made the first time a text holds one of them. A text is cut into runs by the face that has each glyph and the runs are set one after another on one baseline, the tracking between two runs as between two letters, so mixed text looks like one line. Nunito lacks ĳ and ſ, the arrows and the gender marks: they stay a question mark (the marks are drawn as shapes, as before). The no-break space and the narrow no-break space (French sets one before ? ! : ;) are drawn as the plain space's width and are not break points. The title's lettering stays Latin-1 and é.

### Colour tokens (`ModernUi`)

| Token | Value | Use |
|---|---|---|
| Ink | `36,44,68` | text |
| Muted | `110,120,148` | secondary labels |
| Frame | `52,64,96` | panel borders |
| Panel | white → `234,240,248` | panel fill (top to bottom) |
| Shadow | `14,22,46` at 31 % | drop shadows |
| Red / Gold / Green / Blue | `232,72,76` / `240,176,56` / `56,182,104` / `70,136,232` | FIGHT / BAG / POKÉMON / RUN, and accents |
| Track | `62,72,98` | bar backgrounds |
| HP | `70,214,110` > 50 %, `246,196,50` > 20 %, `240,72,64` | HP fill |
| EXP | `72,176,250` | EXP bar |
| Selection | `240,104,70` | selected card border and glow |
| Type colours | `Palette.GetTypeColor` | type pills, move bands |
| Party backdrop | `44,112,146` → `24,60,98`, 28° stripes of 5 % white | Pokémon menu |

### Shapes and components

- Everything is drawn by `UiShapes` (signed-distance rounded rectangles anti-aliased over one real pixel, vertical gradients, borders, slanted sides and blurred shadows; rings, round-ended lines and triangles for icons and arrows; `Glow`, a soft ellipse of light that fades from its middle to nothing at its rim). No raylib rounded rectangles, triangles or lines in the kit: their edges are jagged.
- The components live in `ModernUi` (`Panel`, `Card`, `Button`, `HpBar`, `Bar`, `ExpBar`, `TypePill`, `StatusPill`, `Tag`, `Portrait`, `Hints`, `Prompt`, `Badge`, `Dim`); a screen composes these and adds no drawing of its own beyond text.
- Translucent is a colour, never a hole: a translucent panel, plate or shine is laid over what is under it, and the picture itself stays opaque everywhere, because the window shows it over black. The 2D pass writes no alpha at all (`GameEngine.Draw` masks it), so nothing drawn there needs to care.
- Panels: radius 22–34, 4 px Frame border, shadow blur 22 at offset (0, 8).
- Cards (anything that can be chosen: party slots, option rows, title entries): a panel whose selected state is a 6 px Selection border and a glow; gold instead while a Pokémon is being moved. An empty slot is a faint white outline.
- Pills: types in the type's colour, statuses as three letters (PSN, BRN, PAR, SLP, FRZ, FNT) in the status colour, short tags ("IN BATTLE") in a token colour. Gender is a vector mark after the name, blue ♂ or pink ♀.
- Icons (`UiIcons`): our own one-colour signs built from the same shapes, white on a coloured chip; on a selected row the chip turns white and the sign takes the colour.
- Key hints: a white key cap and a label on a translucent pill, right-aligned at the top of a screen (on a Frame-coloured pill when they sit on a light panel).
- Prompts: a question over the dimmed screen, a muted line of explanation, and a row of buttons; the safe answer comes first and is the one highlighted.
- Buttons: pills; gradient 18 % lighter at the top to 12 % darker at the bottom, border 35 % darker, a soft shine over the top 38 %. Selected: a 6 px white ring and a glow in the button's colour.
- HP boxes: slanted sides (skew ±0.2), name + gender left, level right, HP bar under; the player's box adds HP numbers and an EXP line.
- Caught mark: in a battle with no trainer, a foe whose species the Pokédex has as caught has a small Poké Ball (the bag's ball icon, 0.8× the name's size) 12 after its name and gender, before its status pill, in the single box and the double battle's compact boxes alike; never on a trainer's Pokémon nor on the player's side (Platinum's `HEALTHBOX_INFO_CAUGHT_SPECIES`).
- HP bar: pill with an amber "HP" tag 2.3× its height.
- Dialogue: wide panel near the bottom, speaker in a red pill tag on its top edge (so the line itself doesn't repeat the name), a bobbing red arrow when the line is complete.
- Layout grid: 1920×1080 layout units (drawn at 4K), 48–64 margins, 24–32 gutters. Battle: opponent box top-left, player box right above the commands, prompt bottom-left, a large FIGHT button with BAG, POKÉMON and RUN stacked beside it.

### Field menus and notices

- **Start menu** (`StartMenu`): a panel 440 wide on the right that slides in. Platinum's order (Pokédex, Pokémon, Bag, the player's name, Save, Options), then CLOSE, then QUIT GAME under a rule. Each row has its icon on a chip; the selected row is a Selection-coloured pill with white text.
- **Leaving the game**: QUIT GAME asks "Quit the game?" with KEEP PLAYING (highlighted), SAVE AND QUIT and QUIT. The title menu has a QUIT entry too, with no question because nothing can be lost there. The window's close button (plan 12 · Q1, `ClosePrompt`) asks "Close the game?" in the same prompt (`ModernUi.Prompt`: the screen dimmed, the panel rising 60 into place over 0.18 s) over whatever is on screen, which stands still under it: KEEP PLAYING (highlighted), SAVE AND QUIT and QUIT where the game can be saved (in the field with nothing under way, as the start menu's SAVE), and KEEP PLAYING and QUIT anywhere else (a battle, a scene, a menu, the introduction), with a line that says the game can't be saved now. B is KEEP PLAYING. On the title screen before any game has begun (and the options opened from it) the close button ends the game at once, as its QUIT does. Esc is never the close button: it is "back".
- **Location sign** (`LocationSign`): on arriving outdoors (entering a town or route, stepping out of a building) a slanted plate with the place's name drops in at the top left, holds 2.4 s and lifts away. Its colour bar says what kind of place it is: green for routes, blue for lakes, gold for towns and cities. Rooms have no sign.
- **Notices** (`Toast`): one line in white on a dark pill at the top centre ("Game saved."), 2.6 s; a notice that replaces another does not slide in again.
- **Questions in the field** (`ChoiceBox`, `ModernUi.DrawChoices`; plan 02 · S1): a question is a line of the text box like any other, written out at the text speed, with no arrow to go on once it is whole. Its answers then slide in from the right on a panel of their own that stands on the right end of the text box (its right edge the box's, 22 above it): a row 72 high to an answer in 34 Black, the panel as wide as its longest answer and no narrower than 260, the answer under the cursor a Selection-coloured pill with white text as in the start menu. "Yes" is first and under the cursor when it opens; the cancel button answers "No", or picks the last entry of a longer menu, which is where a way out goes. A menu holds six answers at most. The panel slides away as the answer is given and the box goes on.
- **A field move's cut-in** (plan 02 · S2): once "X used Cut!" has been read, a band of the dark notice colour `36,44,68` opens across the screen 300 high, its middle a little above the screen's, edged top and bottom with a stripe of Selection four high; the Pokémon's front sprite (the menu's, at twice its size) runs in from the right, holds in the middle a little left of centre while the move's name stands beside it in 52 Black on a slanted plate, and runs out to the left; the band closes behind it. 0.18 s to open, 0.3 s in, 0.45 s held, 0.25 s out, 0.12 s to close: about 1.3 s in all, and the script waits for it. Whatever the move does (a tree falling, a rock breaking apart) happens as the band closes.
- **The Pokétch** (`PoketchView`, plan 02 · S2): the handheld's lower screen as a watch the player wears, shown over the field at the bottom right when the Pokétch button is pressed and put away the same way; the player goes on walking under it. A body 456 by 368 in the Frame colour with a rim a shade lighter, radius 40, standing 32 from the screen's right and bottom edges, a button on its right side (the original's, which changes the app); its screen 360 by 296 is an LCD of our own, a pale green `176,204,160` drawn in one dark green `40,62,48` and a middle green `112,146,104`, in blocks of 8 so it reads as a coarse display, never anti-aliased. It slides up in 0.2 s. The Color Changer swaps the three greens for one of seven other sets of three (yellow, orange, red, purple, blue, teal, white: `ModernUi.LcdColors`), each as far from its paper to its ink as the greens are. Taken in hand (plan 06 · R14b) the player stands still and a cursor stands for the stylus: four corners of ink a block thick round the button it is on, a block outside it, never over what the button shows. Everything an app draws keeps to the blocks of 8 on the screen's 45 by 37 (`PoketchAppState.Columns`, `Rows`), in the three tones and the seven-segment figures; words are Black 24 or 28 in the ink. A Pokémon on the Pokétch is its menu icon in the screen's tones by each pixel's brightness, as the original turns its icons into the LCD's shades: dark in the ink, the middle in the middle tone, light in the middle tone faintly. The apps:
  - **Digital watch**: the hour and the minute in seven-segment figures 96 tall, a colon that blinks each second, and the day of the week under them; a touch turns the backlight on or off, the paper a quarter of the way to white.
  - **Pedometer**: the steps taken since it was last reset, in the same figures, five digits, STEPS under them and a RESET button 15 blocks by 6 at the bottom, filled with the middle tone for a fifth of a second as it is touched.
  - **Party status**: the team in two rows of three, each Pokémon's menu icon over an HP bar of its own in the LCD's greens; a fainted one is dark, one with a status has a small mark; one touched hops two blocks and lands in 0.4 s.
  - **Analog watch**: a round dial of blocks 33 across in the middle tone, a block of ink at each hour and two by two at the quarters, the minute hand 14 blocks long and the hour hand 9 drawn block by block from a middle of two by two; a touch lights the paper for half a second.
  - **Calendar**: the month's name at the top, then the days in rows of seven from Sunday, each a cell 6 blocks by 5 with its number (Sundays in the middle tone); today's cell is filled with ink and its number in the paper, and a marked day is filled in the middle tone, its number in ink.
  - **Link searcher**: TOUCH TO SEARCH FOR PEOPLE NEARBY; while it searches, two rings of blocks spreading from the middle under SEARCHING...; then the number found in the seven-segment figures over NOBODY NEARBY.
