  - **Calculator**: ten places across the top (rows 1 to 5), each a figure 3 blocks by 5 a block apart from column 5, its unlit cells shown faintly as an "8" of the middle green at a low alpha, so the display reads as an LCD's; the point and the minus sign take a place each, and the error is an "E" in all ten. The operation waiting (+, -, × or ÷, also 3 by 5) stands at the far left. A rule of the middle green under the display (row 7). The keys below in four rows of five, 8 by 6, a block apart (rows 9, 16, 23 and 30), laid out as the original's: 7 8 9 C / 4 5 6 + - / 1 2 3 × ÷ / 0 . =, the 0, C and = keys two wide; each an ink outline with its sign in Black 28, filled with the middle green for 0.15 s when touched.
  - **Memo pad**: a page of 36 by 35 blocks from the top left (a block in from the edges), a block to a dot, written in the ink; a rule of the middle green down column 37; at the right the eraser (rows 3 to 15) above the pen (rows 21 to 33), buttons 6 by 13, the tool in hand shown pressed in. The eraser is a block of ink 2 by 7 with its lower three rows rubber-coloured, the pen a stick of ink 2 by 7 with a point.
  - **Counter**: four figures 7 by 13 (rows 4 to 16), a block apart, centred; under them the button, 19 by 13, an ink outline round a cap of the middle green on a shadow of ink, the cap pushed down a block onto it for 0.15 s when touched.
  - **Coin toss**: a coin 11 blocks across lying in the lower middle (rows 23 to 33), an ink rim round its face: heads is a ball of our own (top half the middle green, bottom the paper, a band and a button of ink), tails the middle green with a cross of the paper. Thrown, it rises to about 20 blocks above where it lies and bounces three times lower, its face narrowing and widening as it turns (the middle green inside the rim), with a shadow of the middle green on the ground under it that narrows the higher it is.
  - **Roulette**: the memo pad's page (pen only), the arrow turning over its middle: a shaft of ink from 4 blocks behind the hub to 13 in front, a head of two strokes 3½ blocks back from the tip, and a hub 3 by 3 with a dot of the paper. START, STOP and CLEAR down the right, 6 by 8 (rows 4, 15 and 26), as a triangle, a square and a cross; a button that can't be used now is shown pressed in (STOP while the arrow is still, START and CLEAR while it turns).
  - **Dot art**: 24 dots by 20, a block each, in the middle of the screen (columns 10 to 33, rows 8 to 27) inside a frame of the middle green a block wide; its four shades are the paper, the middle green at a low alpha, the middle green and the ink. Until it is first touched it shows our own picture, a Poké Ball.
  - **Color changer**: the colour's name in Black 40 at the top (row 6), SCREEN COLOR under it in Black 24 in the middle green; the slider's eight swatches across the lower half (row 22), 4 by 6 and a block apart from column 3, each filled with its own colour's paper round a band of its own ink and outlined in the screen's ink; a track of ink under them (row 30) with a knob 2 by 3 under the colour chosen.
  - **Kitchen timer**: minutes and seconds in figures 7 by 13 (rows 10 to 22, columns 6, 14, 24 and 32) with a colon of two blocks between the pairs; while the time is being set, an arrow 7 by 3 over and under each figure (rows 6 and 24), a triangle of ink; a bell 4 by 6 at each side of the figures (a dome of ink, its mouth of the middle green, a clapper), the two swinging a block out and back at every ring; START, STOP and RESET along the bottom (row 30), 13 by 6 with their names in Black 24, START or STOP shown pressed in as the timer's state has it (STOP as it opens and while it is set or paused, START while it counts or rings).
  - **The map of Sinnoh** (shared by the Berry Searcher and the Marking Map; `PoketchMap`): a block for each chunk of the overworld, as the Pokédex's area page and Fly lay the region out, columns 0 to 29 and rows 5 to 29 (the original's map leaves out the empty north too), 30 blocks by 25 with its top left at column 7, row 6. Sea and nothing are the paper, land the middle green, towns the ink; the picture is read once from the region's habitats file and drawn as runs of blocks along each row. A hidden place (Fullmoon and Newmoon Islands, the Spring Path, the Seabreak Path) is sea until its variable says it is known, land after. Whatever stands on the map is a mark of 3 by 3 blocks centred on its cell, each block in the ink, or in the paper where it falls on a town: the player is a solid square shown 0.6 of each second, off the overworld at the place they last went in from (`PoketchContext.Outside`).
  - **Dowsing machine**: the tiles round the player, 15 across and 14 down, two blocks to a tile (columns 7 to 36, rows 4 to 31), a dot of the middle green at each tile's corner and an edge of the middle green round them; the player's tile in the ink in the middle. A touch sends a ring of ink, a block wide, out from the tile touched to 4.4 tiles in 0.6 s; items found then blink four times a second for 0.8 s, each a tile of ink, one noticed from further off ringed by one or two squares of the middle green a block out, and the ring goes out again, until the player takes a step.
  - **Berry searcher**: the map, a block of ink (the paper on a town) on each cell where a patch is in fruit, and the player. Touched, the picture comes back into focus in 0.3 s: squares of three cells, then two, then one, each square the tone of its first cell.
  - **Marking map**: the map, the roamers on the loose as a saltire, the six markers (a ring, a square, a triangle, a heart, a cross and an I-beam), the one moved last on top, and the player over everything. A marker picked up blinks four times a second until it is put down.
  - **Trainer counter**: the chain going now along the top: CHAIN in Black 22 at the top left, its species' icon under it (10 by 10 blocks, rows 4 to 13) and its count in figures 7 by 13 from column 17 (rows 1 to 13), its leading noughts not shown; three dashes of the middle green with no chain. A rule of the middle green across row 15. Under it the three best chains as a podium of buttons 13 by 16: the best in the middle (column 16, row 16), the second to its right (column 31, row 18), the third to its left (column 1, row 20), each with its species' icon over its count in figures 3 by 5; an empty record has a dash of the middle green. A record touched hops five blocks up and down in 16 frames. Every icon on the Pokétch's map apps is its menu icon read into blocks once: the ink where it is dark, the middle green where it is light, nothing where it is clear (the original turns its icons into the LCD's shades by their brightness).
  - **Friendship checker**: the team's menu icons, 64 across in the screen's tones, wander the whole screen, each turned the way it goes, bouncing off the edges and off one another; a spot of ground that can be touched is one block of the middle tone. A Pokémon showing that it likes the player has one to three hearts of 3 by 3 blocks in the ink over its head; one in the air has a flat shadow of the middle tone under where it stood, and rises and falls in whole blocks.
  - **Day-care checker**: the yard's fence along the bottom in the middle tone, rails and a post every four blocks; over it the two Pokémon left at the Day Care, icons 80 across, facing one another, with an egg of blocks between them when there is one; across the top each one's level in small seven-segment figures (4 blocks by 7) with no leading noughts, and its gender's sign in blocks after them. Touched, the picture dissolves back in from the paper in cells of two blocks over two thirds of a second.
  - **Pokémon history**: the last twelve Pokémon caught or given, four to a row in three rows, read like a page from the oldest at the top left to the newest at the bottom right; an icon 64 across in each cell of 10 by 11 blocks, and a short dash of the middle tone in a cell not filled yet.
  - **Move tester**: the target's two types at the top right and the move's type at the bottom left (under a MOVE label in the middle tone), each named in capitals between two arrow buttons of blocks; the pressed arrow fills with the middle tone. Five exclamation marks of blocks at the top left are lit in the ink as far as the move works (none for no effect, three for normal, five for four times) and faint beyond; the words for it run along the bottom.
  - **Matchup checker**: a meter of three big hearts at the top, lit in the ink for each heart the pair earns (flashing for the best); under it two heart-shaped fish of blocks, each a forked tail and a heart on its side with an eye of paper, swim toward one another a step for each heart, kiss under a small heart for the best match and turn their backs and swim apart for none; at the bottom each side's Pokémon in its own button, 48 across, and the check button between them with a heart in it, shown pressed while the fish swim or when there is no pair to check.
- **The Safari Game** (plan 01 · M7): while a game is on in the Great Marsh, a panel 300 by 96 at the top right, 32 from the edges, as the other panels are: SAFARI BALLS and STEPS as labels over their numbers in Black 36, side by side; a number turns Red at a fifth of its start or under (6 balls, 100 steps). It shows whenever the field does, the Pokétch and the text box included, and goes when the game ends.
- **Story scenes** (plan 02 · S1): a script has the field to itself, and the interface shows nothing of that but its text. Its fade is plain black over the field and under the text box, so a line can be read on a black screen; it takes 0.4 s unless the scene says otherwise. People a scene sends walking keep the trainers' walking pace (4.5 tiles a second, 8 in a hurry) and come to rest in an eighth of a second. A shake of the camera moves the picture up and down by whole texels thirty times a second, six texels at its start and dying away over its length (half a second unless said).
- **A scene skipped** (plan 12 · Q20; `SkipHold`, `ModernUi.SkipRing`): with the options' "Skip scenes" on `Seen` or `Any` (off unless chosen: then nothing of this is drawn or read), the menu button held for one second while a scene that may be skipped runs skips it. **The hold's ring** is all the interface shows of it: a pill of the notices' dark (`58,70,104` over `36,44,68` at 245, the white rim at 60 and 3 wide, as a notice's) 56 high, its right end 64 from the screen's right edge and its middle 60 from the top, which is the middle of a cinema bar, so over a scene's bars it sits inside the top one; SKIP in 24 Black, white, 24 in from its left end, and at its right end a ring 36 across and 5 wide, 12 in from the pill's end: a track of white at 70, and over it the fill in white, growing clockwise from twelve o'clock in step with the hold (laid as strokes with round ends, 48 to the turn). It is drawn only while the button is down and the scene may be skipped: it is there from the first frame of a press, empties and goes the moment the button is let go, and one long press skips one stretch (the button is let go before it counts again). It is drawn over the field, the script's black screen, the cinema bars, the text box and a question's answers; beside a Safari Game's panel, whose corner that is, it stands 24 to the panel's left. A question is never skipped, so nothing is drawn while one is on the screen. When the ring is full the text on the screen is put away and the whole picture goes to black in 0.2 s; behind it everyone is put where the scene leaves them; and the picture opens again in 0.3 s on the scene's end, or on whatever waits for the player (a question, a battle, a screen), which then goes on at its own pace. Nothing is heard of what was skipped; a change of music is kept.
- **The camera kit** (plan 09 · L4; `CameraRig`): what a scene does with the field's camera besides sending it to a spot. It sits over whatever camera the place has (the open country's, a cave's, the zoomed-in one, a room's), and every move eases out of where it is and into where it goes on the same curve as a pan (3t² − 2t³).

| Move | What it does | Numbers |
|---|---|---|
| Pan | the camera looks at a tile's middle instead of the player, and stays | 0.8 s unless said; from a spot it was sent to already it eases on from there |
| Follow | the camera keeps on someone else's feet, wherever they walk; `player` brings it back without touching the lens or the bars | 0.8 s to get onto them |
| Path | the camera travels through the middles of up to eight tiles, from where it looks | 4 tiles a second unless said (a quarter of a tile to 16); it gathers that pace over half a second and loses it over the last half second, and holds it between (a path too short for both halves them) |
| Zoom | the lens alone: a factor of 1 is the place's own, 2 shows everything twice as large, 0.5 twice as much ground. The field of view changes (tan of its half divided by the factor) and never the pitch or the distance, so upright things stand as straight at every zoom | 0.5 to 4; 0.8 s unless said, eased on the factor's ratio so the picture grows evenly |
| Bars | the title's own cinema bars: plain black, 120 units at the top and the bottom, sliding in and out in whole units | 0.4 s unless said |
| Slow motion | the field at a share of its pace for a time, then back by itself | a tenth to the whole of its pace, for 2 s unless said (60 at most), easing in and out over 0.2 s |
| Release | everything back: the camera to the player, the lens to the place's own, the bars away, time to its pace | 0.6 s unless said |

  The bars lie over the field, its weather and a script's black screen, and under the text box, the answers and every panel, so a line is read across the lower bar. Slow motion slows what the field does (people's steps and gestures, the player's, prints and dust, weather, a scene's effects, a puzzle's pieces) and nothing a scene is timed by: the camera's own moves, the bars, the text, a bubble, a fade and a script's waits all keep the clock's time, so a scene's lines mean the same seconds whatever the pace. A zoom out shows more ground, and what is drawn follows the lens: the ground in view, the people on it, the chunks kept ready, and the shadow map, which is spread over the wider view (its texels grow with it). In a room, which is seen whole, a zoom in lets the camera look where it is sent as far as the zoom leaves room: by the share of the room the zoom has taken out of the picture, and no further. A scene that ends, however it ends, releases what it left.

### Battle panels

- **Switching**: the field dims and the team appears as six cards, three to a row, under a one-line question. Each card has the icon, name, level, HP bar and numbers, and a tag: IN BATTLE, a status, or FNT (the card is greyed). The player's HP box is hidden behind the cards.
- **Battle Style Shift's offer** (plan 08 · P1, the options' Battle Style): when a trainer's Pokémon has fainted and the next is chosen, the battle stops on one line in the message box that names it and asks ("Ace Trainer Vera has Starly coming out next. Will you change Pokémon?"), with Yes and No in the choice box as for a move to learn, the cursor on Yes as the original's. Yes opens the switching cards, whose question then names the Pokémon coming ("Starly is coming out next. Send out which Pokémon?") and whose hints are Send out and Stay in; backing out of them stays in. No, B or a confirm stays in. The player's HP box is hidden while the question is open, as behind the cards.
- **Bag** (plan 06 · R11): BAG opens Platinum's four battle pockets over the dimmed field, under a head panel that says BAG: HP/PP RESTORE, STATUS RESTORE, POKé BALLS and BATTLE ITEMS as buttons two to a row in their pockets' colours, each saying how many kinds it holds, and a wide grey button under them for the last item used (named on it). A pocket shows its items as cards two to a row, four rows to a page (pixel icon on a pale disc, name, ×count), the chosen one gold-edged and described on a panel to their right. An item for one Pokémon goes on to the team as six cards, three to a row (portrait, name, level, HP bar and numbers, IN BATTLE and a status), under a head that names the item; an item for one move (an Ether) then shows the evolution scene's move panel on the right (`MovePick`), titled with the question. The player's HP box is hidden behind all of these, as behind the switching cards. Item icons are 20×20 pixel art shown at 3×: the ball itself, a spray bottle whose colour says which medicine, a crystal for a Revive.
- **The bag shut** (plan 14 · B14, a Master Trainer's battle; plan 13 · V5's run rule sets the same): where nothing may be used from the bag, the BAG button keeps its place in the commands and is painted a flat grey (`ModernUi.Closed`, 150 156 174) in place of its own colour, its sign and word still white over it, so the four commands stand as they always do. Choosing it plays the error sound and says why in the message box, "No items may be used in this battle!", and the commands come back; no pocket opens.
- **Trainer's team**: a dark tray hangs under the opponent's box with a ball for each Pokémon (grey once fainted) and a dot for each empty place.
- A message that doesn't fit on one line wraps to two.
- **A move to learn** (plan 06 · R10): when a Pokémon that knows four moves reaches a level with another to learn, the battle stops on its question in the message box, with Yes and No in the field's choice box (`DrawChoices`) standing 22 above the message box, its right edge on the box's. Choosing which move to forget shows the evolution scene's panel (`MoveChoice`: WANTS TO LEARN, the four moves and the new one) on the right, over the field, which stays in view; the message box keeps the question. The player's HP box is hidden while the question is open, as it is behind the switching cards. B keeps every move.
- **A level** (plan 08 · P5, `LevelUpPanel`): when a Pokémon's level rises, the line that says so ("X grew to Lv. N!", with its fanfare) stays in the message box and a panel opens over the field with it, as Platinum's summary window follows its level-up line. The panel is 560 wide and 484 tall, radius 30, its right edge on the message box's and its foot 22 above it, and slides in 80 from the right over 0.18 s. Its head is the Pokémon's menu icon at 2× on a disc of radius 44, its name and gender in Black 34 and, at the right, the level it reached; under a rule come six rows 48 apart in the original's order (Max HP, Attack, Defense, Sp. Atk, Sp. Def, Speed), each stat's name in ExtraBold 30 and its number right-aligned in Black 32 under a caption. It has two pages, and a press turns each: first GAINED, what the level added to each stat as +n in Green (+0 in Muted), then NOW, the new values in Ink; the second press closes it and the battle goes on, to the moves the level brings. The numbers are the rules' own (the log's `LevelRose.Before` and `After`), never read off the Pokémon the screen shows, so the gains add up to the values and both are what the summary says afterwards. The player's HP box is hidden while the panel is up, as behind a move to learn. A Rare Candy used from the bag opens the same panel over the team's cards, on the half of the screen the Pokémon's card isn't on, its foot 22 above the prompt, which says the level's line with the arrow that waits for a press (the key hint says Next); the move the level brings and its evolution wait for the panel's two presses.
- **Move hints** (plan 12 · Q10, the `MoveHints` option): where the option is on (the default since plan 06 · R19, every game being played by the modern rules), a damaging move's card says how it will do against a foe on a pill 28 tall hung on the card's top edge (12 above it, 16 over it, so it sits in the gap between rows and over nothing on the card): SUPER EFFECTIVE in Green, NOT VERY EFFECTIVE in Gold, NO EFFECT in Muted, each in white Black 16 after its sign (an upward triangle, a downward one, a cross, drawn with `UiShapes`). A plain matchup shows nothing. In a single battle the pill stands at the card's right, 24 in; in a double battle each foe has its own as they stand, the left foe's at the left of the move's name (150 in) and the right foe's at the card's right. The target menu hangs the same pill on each card that can be chosen, 24 in from its right. Nothing is shown for a status move, for a foe whose species the Pokédex hadn't seen before the battle began, or for a place that is empty. With the option off the cards are exactly as they were.
- **A trainer's line** (plan 11 · C12, the `TrainerLines` option): where the option is on (the default since plan 06 · R19, every game being played by the modern rules), a line a trainer of the other side says in battle is shown in the message box like any other, wrapped to two lines at most, with the trainer's name (the rival's as the player named him) on a pill 44 tall hung on the box's top edge (22 above it, 22 over it), 40 in from the box's left: the name in white Black 26 on Red, 22 in on each side, a soft shadow under it as under a hint pill. The battle's own lines have no pill. With the option off nothing is said and the box is exactly as it was.
- **The information panel** (plan 12 · Q11, `BattleMenuState.Info`): the `Item` action (C, the pad's left face button) opens it from the commands; Back, A or the same button close it, back on the commands as they were. The stage dims (150) under the foes' HP boxes, which stay bright over it; the player's are hidden, as behind the switching cards. Key hints at the top right (X Close).
  - **The strip**, under the foe's box (its top 240 from the screen's in a single battle, under the trainer's tray; 286 under a double battle's two boxes), tags 48 tall from 56 in: FIELD in Frame, then the weather in its own colour (rain Blue, harsh sunlight Selection, a sandstorm a darker Gold, hail sky blue 96 168 214, fog Muted), Trick Room violet (150 96 200) and Gravity in Frame, each with its turns; NOTHING IN EFFECT in Muted when there is nothing.
  - **The sheet**, from 352 to 1032: two panels 900 wide, FOE'S SIDE (a Red tag) on the left and YOUR SIDE (Blue) on the right, as the two sides stand on the screen. Each has its side's screens, veils and hazards as tags 40 tall (what helps in Blue, what harms in Red), or "No screens or hazards." in Muted, then a card for each of its Pokémon on the field, sharing the panel's height (300 at most): the icon at 2× on a disc, the name, status and level; ABILITY and ITEM as captions over their values in Black 28, or "Not seen yet" in Muted; under them the stat stages (ATTACK +2, a rise Green and a fall Red) and the passing conditions (a help Blue, a harm Red) as tags 36 tall, or "No changes." in Muted.
  - **What it may say**: the player's own Pokémon are known whole; a partner's and the foes' show only the ability and the item a line has named, and an item the player saw go, with nothing in its place, is "None". Counts are given where nothing hidden lengthens them (Trick Room, Gravity, Mist, Safeguard, Tailwind, Lucky Chant, Heal Block, Embargo, Magnet Rise, a Wish, a Future Sight, the Perish count, the player's own screens), never for the weather or the foe's screens (a held rock and Light Clay lengthen them) nor where the rules draw the count (confusion, Encore, Disable, Taunt, a bind).
- **Cursors** (`BattleEngine.MoveCursor`): a cursor moves the way its buttons lie. Among the commands, up and down go through FIGHT, BAG, POKÉMON and RUN in that order and round again; left and right cross between FIGHT and the stack beside it, and come back into the stack on the row the cursor stood on last. The move menu has no cursor on an empty place, and opens on the move that Pokémon chose last in this battle. Among a double battle's targets the cursor moves over the four cards as they lie and never stops on one that can't be aimed at: a step up or down that would land on one takes the card beside it. Enter answers a battle's menu, as it reads a battle's message on.

### Summary

One Pokémon on three panels: on the left its number, level, the 128-px sprite at 3× on a pale disc, name, category, type pills (with its status and a PKRS tag after them), nature and experience (points, to next level, bar); top right the six stats, each with its value and a bar whose length and colour follow the species' base stat (red under 50, amber under 80, green under 110, teal above; HP shows current / max in the HP colours); bottom right the moves as rows with type pill, name, category, power, accuracy and PP. Up and down step through the team.

Once the Contest Hall has been visited (plan 06 · R14c), left and right turn the right-hand side to a second page and back: the Pokémon's condition (below, "Poffins") on one panel as tall as the two, its chart in the middle and the sheen at its foot. Before then that page isn't there, as in Platinum. The last page is the Pokémon's ribbons (plan 06 · R17b; below, "Ribbons"), always there: the same panel with the ribbons as cards, and the button puts a cursor on them.

### Menu screens (G10)

Every full-screen menu is the same frame: the backdrop, its title at the top left, key hints at the top right, and its content between rows 132 and 1032 with 64 margins and 32 gutters. Its parts rise or slide into place over 0.25–0.45 s when it opens.

Shared parts (`ModernUi`, in `ModernUi.Lists.cs`):

- **List rows** (`ListRow`): 84 tall and 8 apart, radius 26, on a panel. What the row is about sits at its left on a pale disc (an item's icon at 3×, a Pokémon's at 1×), its name in Black 34, and one value right-aligned (a count "×5", a price). The chosen row is a Selection-coloured pill with white text, as in the start menu; the others have no fill. A row that can't be chosen now is Muted.
- **Scrolling** (`UiNav.Window`, `ScrollBar`): a list shows whole rows only. The cursor keeps two rows of context ahead of it where there are any, and wraps from the last row to the first. A thin bar at the panel's right edge (Rule-coloured track, Frame-coloured thumb) shows where the window is, and only when there is more than fits.
- **A key held down** (`HeldKey`): in a list too long to tap through, a press is one step and a key kept down goes on: after 0.32 s a step every 0.08 s, and from the tenth of those one every 0.04 s. Held steps stop at either end of the list, where a press wraps round, and the cursor's tick sounds at most every 0.07 s (`CursorTick`). The Pokédex's list and entries, a pocket's list in the bag and a shop's stock do this; short menus on those screens (an item's actions, "how many?", the party) take presses only.
- **Tabs** (`Tabs`): pills in a row across the screen under the title, each with its sign on a chip in its own colour and its name in Black 22. The chosen tab is filled with its colour, its chip white. Left and right change tab and wrap round.
- **Detail panel**: the thing chosen, large, on a pale disc (an item's icon at 6×, a Pokémon's sprite at 3×), its name in Black 44–56, tags, a rule, and its text in Bold 30 on 42 leading.
- **Money** (`Money`): our own mark, a P crossed by two bars, before the number in Black. Amounts on panels and in lists use it, never "$" or "¥".
- **Stepper** (`Stepper`): a number on a Frame-coloured pill between two arrows; left and right change it by one, up and down by ten, and it wraps between the least and the most.
- **Keyboard** (`Keyboard`, its cursor and rules in `NameEntry`): keys as small cards, 96 by 84 and 10 apart, in four rows of ten: the alphabet with a full stop, a hyphen, an apostrophe and a space, then the digits. The chosen key is a Selection-coloured pill. A fifth row has three wide keys: upper or lower case, delete, and OK. The keys turn to lower case by themselves after a name's first capital; the B button deletes, the Start button jumps to OK, and the cursor waits on OK once the name is full. Names are entered with the cursor and never typed, because the game's own keys are letters.

The screens:

- **Bag** (`BagScreen`): eight tabs, Platinum's pockets in its order (Items, Medicine, Poké Balls, TMs & HMs, Berries, Mail, Battle Items, Key Items), each in a colour of its own (gold, pink, red, violet, green, sky blue, orange, purple) and each remembering its own cursor. Under them the pocket's list on the left (eight rows) and the chosen item on the right: icon, name, its pocket as a tag, how many are in the bag, and its text; a TM or HM is described by its move, with the move's type, category, power, accuracy and PP under it. The A button opens what can be done with the item on a small panel at the foot of the detail panel (USE, GIVE, TOSS, REGISTER, CANCEL as the item allows, one row each, like the start menu's rows). Using or giving goes on to the party cards, which say ABLE or NOT ABLE for something used (a TM's tag is ABLE, NOT ABLE or LEARNED; ABLE is green). An item used on one move (an Ether, a PP Up) then shows the evolution scene's move panel on the right (`MovePick`: the Pokémon's four moves with their PP, the question as its title), and a TM taught to a Pokémon that knows four moves asks which to forget on the same panel. TOSS asks how many on a panel over the dimmed screen like the shop's: the item's icon and name, a stepper from 1 to as many as the bag holds.
- **Item icons** (`PixelArtGenerator.ItemIcon`): 20 by 20 pixel art, shown at 3× in rows and 6× on the detail disc. A kind has a shape: the ball itself, a spray bottle for medicine (its colour says which), a crystal for a Revive, a wrapped sweet for a Rare Candy, a flat disc in its move's type colour for a TM (a pale rim for an HM), a berry, an envelope, a key, a tonic bottle, a cut gem in its own colour for an evolution stone, a pouch for anything else.
- **Shop** (`ShopScreen`): the counter opens on its question, BUY, SELL and SEE YA!, three rows on a panel in the middle of the dimmed screen. Buying shows the stock on the left (nine rows, each with its price; a price the player can't pay is Red), the player's money on a card at the top right, the chosen item under it with how many are already in the bag. Selling shows the bag's items that sell in the same rows, each with what one fetches (half the price) and how many are held. Either asks how many on a panel over the dimmed screen: the item, a stepper from 1 to as many as the money buys or the bag holds (99 at most), and the total.
- **Wardrobe** (`WardrobeScreen`, plan 11 · C10): the player's clothes at home and a boutique's counter are one screen, titled WARDROBE or with the boutique's place. Five tabs, the slots (HATS red, TOPS blue, BOTTOMS violet, SHOES orange, BAGS green), each with its own sign. On the left a stage 520 wide with the player's figure on a pale floor disc, rendered live in what the cursor is on (three dots pulse at its top while a new outfit is being made); on the BAGS tab the figure turns half away so the bag shows. In the middle the slot's clothes as list rows (eight to the panel): the look's own first, its disc a gold star; then the free, the owned and those for sale, each disc the garment's colour ringed with its second, a crossed ring for none at all. A row ends in a WORN tag (green) for what is on, OWNED for what has been bought, or the price (Red if the money doesn't reach). On the right the money on a card and the garment's own card: its name in Black 40, its slot's tag and WORN or OWNED, the price, a rule and its line. Choosing something owned puts it on at once; something for sale asks "Buy the …?" on a panel over the dimmed screen with BUY (green) and NO (blue), and a garment bought is worn at once and owned for good.
- **Poffins** (plan 06 · R14c; `ModernUi.Poffins.cs`). Our own pictures, never the games': a Poffin is a round puff, the pot an iron ring seen from above.
  - **The flavours' colours**: spicy red (230 86 74), dry blue (74 132 228), sweet pink (238 118 172), bitter green (76 174 96), sour yellow (232 190 50). The condition's qualities take the colours of the flavours that raise them (cool red, beauty blue, cute pink, smart green, tough yellow).
  - **A Poffin** (`PoffinIcon`): a base a little wider than tall in its strongest flavour's colour, a domed top over it in its second flavour's (a lighter shade of the first for one flavour), a swirl and a shine on the dome. A Rich Poffin is caramel brown, an Overripe one plum, a Mild one cream, and a Foul one grey with a wisp of smoke rising from it.
  - **The pot** (`PoffinCookingScreen`, titled POFFIN COOKING): in the middle of the screen a cream table 940 wide, ringed with small red dots, and on it the pot: an iron ring (radius 340) over flames that peek out under its rim, more and taller in each of the three stages. In it the batter, the colour of its berries' flavours mixed (each as strong as the flavour) and a little cream (42 %): glossy and runny in the first stage, thicker in the second, a dough in the third. Pale swirls on it turn as the pot says the batter turns. A wooden spoon stands where the stylus is, its handle out over the rim. A gold arrow curves round the pot's top the way to stir: large and pulsing when the pot draws it again, smaller afterwards, red while the batter goes the other way. The berry falls in from above and leaves a ripple. Burning shows as grey spots on the batter and smoke, spilling as batter slopping over the rim.
  - **Beside the pot**: on the left a panel with the stages (three numbered dots: the current one gold, those done green), the time stirred, and how often it burned and spilled over; on the right a tall gauge of the batter's speed, orange at its foot where it burns, green in the middle, blue at the top where it spills (FAST in the last stage, where nothing spills), with a white bar for the speed now. A banner over the pot's foot says what happens: the berry going in, "Done!", and the warnings in Red.
  - **The results**: a card over the dimmed pot with the time, the spills, the burns and the smoothness as captions over rules on its left, and a second later the Poffin on a disc on its right, with its name and level. The text box then says it was put away and asks whether to cook another (YES, NO).
  - **The Poffin Case** (`PoffinCaseScreen`): six tabs, the five flavours in their colours and then ALL (slate); a Poffin is under every flavour it has. The list on the left, seven rows (the Poffin on the row's disc, its name, its level), newest first; under it the prompt on a panel 80 tall. On the right the chosen Poffin: how many the case holds of its hundred on a tag (Red when full), the Poffin at large on a disc, its name, its level on a tag of its colour and its smoothness on a Frame-coloured one, and under a rule its five flavours as bars in their colours, each with the quality it raises; a flavour it hasn't is Muted. The A button opens GIVE, TRASH and CANCEL like the bag's actions; TRASH asks over the dimmed screen, YES in Red and NO in Blue.
  - **Feeding**: GIVE goes to the party's cards; then the Pokémon's card on the left (its sprite at 3× on a disc, name, level and nature) over the Poffin's card, and its condition on the right. Given the Poffin, the Pokémon hops, the old shape stays as a white outline and the new one grows out of it over 0.8 s, and the prompt says how it took it (a flavour it likes, one it dislikes, or neither).
  - **The condition** (`ConditionChart`): a pentagon on a pale disc, cool straight up and then with the clock beauty, cute, smart and tough, each named on a pill of its colour at its point; three pentagon rules and a spoke to each point. The Pokémon's shape is filled in green (72 200 112) with a darker edge: nothing in a quality is a small pentagon at a seventh of the radius, 255 the rim, in a straight line between. Under it the sheen: twelve stars in a row, as many lit (gold, with a white heart) as the sheen gives (twelve at 255), twinkling in turn; the rest small and in the Rule colour.
- **Super Contests** (plan 06 · R17a; `SuperContestScreen`, `ModernUi.SuperContest.cs`). One screen through a whole contest, titled SUPER CONTEST with two tags beside the title: the rank in Frame (PRACTICE for a round held alone) and the quality in its colour (the condition's: cool red, beauty blue, cute pink, smart green, tough yellow). At the foot of every phase the announcer's box, a panel 148 tall across the screen with one or two lines at 34 and the advance arrow when a button moves it on. Our own pictures: nothing of the original's stage, judges or accessories is drawn.
  - **A contestant** is their Pokémon, as a menu sprite or a menu icon, never a trainer's figure. The four stand in the order of their entry numbers from the left, No. 1 to No. 4; the player is No. 4, and the player's tag is Selection where the others' are Frame.
  - **The line-up**: four cards across (the entry tag, the sprite at 2× on a Poké Ball disc, the Pokémon's name, the trainer's under it in Muted). The one being announced has the quality's glow and hops. The theme is then given on a band across the dimmed cards, framed in the quality's colour: THE THEME, its name at 76, and a tag with how many accessories and seconds there are.
  - **The dressing-up**: on the left a panel 700 wide with the theme's name, a tag of how many accessories are on of how many the rank allows (Red when full), the Pokémon at 3× on a disc with what it wears round it, and the clock: the seconds as a number over a bar that runs down, Green and then Red for the last ten. On the right the Fashion Case: a grid six across of cards, each an accessory's sign with how many are left in the case (a grey disc when none is), the cursor's card in Selection, five rows in view; under it the name of what the cursor is on.
  - **An accessory's sign** (`AccessoryIcon`): one of nine simple shapes by what the thing is (a fluff as three puffs, a pebble as a rounded block, a feather, leaf or scale as a slanted capsule with a rib, a moustache or barrette as a bow, a flower or sparkle as five petals round a middle, a claw, flame or drop as a teardrop, a mushroom, parasol or balloon as a head on a stick, a hat as a crown on a brim, a stage as a block on a wider foot), in the colour its name gives (Red Feather, Blue Scale) or its kind's own (a pebble grey, a coin gold), with a darker rim and a light spot. They are signs, not drawings of the accessories: 100 would be a sheet of art of their own (plan 09).
  - **A look**: the contestant on the left as in the dressing-up (an opponent's accessories stand where its dressing puts them), and on the right its name, its trainer, the theme on a tag, and the hearts: CONDITION a row of eight, DRESSING UP a row of four, the hearts won filled in heart red (236 84 110) one after another, six a second, each a little larger as it comes; the rest in Rule. Under them the total as a number at 84 beside one heart.
  - **The dance**: across the top the bar as a dark track (30 34 58), its second half a shade lighter; the beats are thin rules, every fourth taller; a hand in Selection crosses it once a bar. The lead's steps are white arrows where they fell, and each has a ring half a bar on with a pale arrow in it: where it is to be repeated. The player's own steps leave a dot under the track, Gold for an excellent one, Green for a good one, Muted for a miss. Under the bar the four Pokémon on discs, the lead's larger and in a light shade of the quality's colour with a LEAD tag; a step lifts the dancer for 0.3 s and shows its arrow on a disc of the grade's colour beside it; each has its points under its name. The four arrow keys are the four steps.
  - **The acting**: three judges' cards across the top left (the name, a HEAD tag in Gold on the middle one, five stars of voltage lit in Gold, and under them the menu icons of whoever has performed for them this turn); under them the four contestants as rows in the turn's order (the icon on a disc, name and trainer, the move shown with its quality's tag and "for" its judge, the turn's hearts as +N beside a heart, the total at the right); on the right the player's four moves as cards: the name, the quality's tag, the hearts it is worth, VOLTAGE UP in Green or VOLTAGE DOWN in Red where it moves the voltage in this contest, and its effect in a line; a move shown the turn before is Muted and says so. The card being chosen has the Selection border; while a turn plays, whoever is acting and the judge spoken of have the quality's glow, and each thing that happens is a line in the announcer's box that a button or 2.4 s moves on.
  - **The results**: four rows on one panel (the entry tag, the icon, name and trainer) each with a track on which the three rounds' bars follow one another, VISUAL pink (238 118 172), DANCE blue (74 132 228), ACTING gold (240 176 56), each growing over 1.4 s as its round is announced; a third of the track is a round's full length. Then the places at the right of the tracks, the winner's row framed in Gold and its icon hopping. A new ribbon is a rosette in the quality's colour (`Rosette`: twelve loops round a gold middle with a white star, two tails) in the announcer's box beside the line that gives it. A practice round shows one bar a contestant, in its round's colour, with the score.
- **Ball Capsules and Seals** (plan 06 · R17b; `SealCaseScreen`, `SealShopScreen`, `ModernUi.Capsules.cs`, `Graphics/SealFx.cs`). Our own pictures: a Seal is a sticker and a capsule a pale ball, never the games' sprites.
  - **A Seal's sticker** (`SealSticker`): a disc in the Seal's own colour with a darker edge and a white ring inside it, and on it the Seal's sign, a shape of the battle effects' atlas (`FxTextures`: a heart, a star, a slash, a puff, a spark, a bubble, a flame, a streamer, a petal, a note, a drop, a burst, a twinkle) or, for a letter Seal, its letter in the interface's type. The sign is white on a deep colour and a deep tone of the Seal's own on a pale one (`StickerInk`), so a white smoke and a yellow star keep their colours. A family's A to G differ in colour.
  - **A capsule** (`CapsuleBall`): a ball in the Disc colour with a rule across its middle and a white button on it, a soft edge under it, and its stickers where they were stuck, the first in front. The ball's radius is the capsule's sixty dots.
  - **The twelve**: titled BALL CAPSULES. A panel 1000 wide with four cards to a row, three rows, each a capsule with its number at the top left and, when it is on a ball, its Pokémon's menu icon on a white disc at the lower right; the cursor is the Red frame. Under it the prompt's panel (96 tall), one line. On the right the capsule under the cursor: BALL CAPSULE and No. N, a tag of how many Seals of eight, the capsule large, ON THE BALL OF and the Pokémon's name (or a line in Muted when it is on none), and at the foot the stage. A capsule's menu is the choice box beside its card.
  - **The stage** (`CapsuleStage`): a panel in the night's dark (40 46 78 down to 26 30 54) with a mound of ground, the Pokémon's menu sprite at 2× (the first of the team's for a capsule on none), and the capsule's burst drawn flat round it, clipped to the panel, played over and over with a rest of 1.3 s between. It is the battle's burst piece for piece (`SealFx.Motes`), a dot of the capsule 1.6 layout units.
  - **Changing one**: on the left the case, SEAL CASE with the page of pages on a tag, eight kinds to a page as cards two to a row (the sticker, the name, how many are left, faded at none), and under it the prompt (the Seal's name, what to do). On the right the capsule's panel: the ball at radius 264 inside a pale ring that marks how far a Seal can be carried, the Seal in hand a little larger with a Red ring, faded where it would fall off the ball; a tall stage at the right edge, captioned COMES OUT LIKE THIS. The hints say what the buttons do in each step (Pick up, Stick, Case or Ball, Keep).
  - **The merchant's stall**: titled SEAL MERCHANT, laid out as a shop: TODAY'S SEALS as rows (the sticker, the name, the price), the money's panel at the top right, and under it the Seal under the cursor with a line of what it gives and the stage showing it. "Buy how many?" is the shop's own card (the sticker, the price each, the stepper, the total).
  - **In battle** (`SealFx.Play`): the burst starts as the ball opens and lasts under two seconds; each Seal goes off after the rules' delay, at the rules' place (`CapsuleRules.BurstDelay`, `BurstOffset`), a dot of the capsule the Pokémon's size (held between 0.7 and 1.6) over 80 in the battle's units, round a point a third of the way up its body. Hearts float up and fade; stars are thrown out and fall; lines are rays; smoke is puffs that swell; sparks jump; bubbles rise and wobble; flames lick upward; a party Seal throws streamers; petals drift down turning; notes bob away; drops arc and fall; a burst is a bang with a ring; a twinkle is four-pointed glints. A letter is its 5×7 sign in dots of one colour, four across, which hang for a moment and scatter; letters read from the left for whoever looks at the screen, on either side. Additive pieces (sparks, flames, twinkles) are kept small: they bloom.
- **Ribbons** (plan 06 · R17b; `ModernUi.Ribbons.cs`). A ribbon's sign (`RibbonIcon`) is one of four shapes by what it is given for, in a colour of its own, never the games' picture: a **rosette** (a ring of petals round a gold button with a white star, two tails) for the Super Contests' and the Syndicate's, in the contest's colour, with one to four gold pips under it for the rank; a **bow** (two loops, a knot, two tails) for Hoenn's contests and Julia's seven; a **medal** (a disc on a V of band) for a Champion's, the Battle Tower's and the Footprint Ribbon, whose disc carries a paw; a **pennant** (a swallow-tailed strip with a star) for the Effort Ribbon and those only an event gave.
  - **The summary's page**: the right-hand side as one panel, RIBBONS with the count on a tag, then the Pokémon's ribbons in the original's order as cards six to a row, three rows in view. The button puts a cursor on them (the Red frame); the panel's foot is then a strip in the Disc colour with the ribbon's sign, its name at 40 and a line of what it is for in Muted at 28. A Pokémon with none shows one line in Muted in the panel's middle.
- **The Fashion Case** (plan 06 · R17b; `FashionCaseScreen`, `ModernUi.Ribbons.cs`): titled FASHION CASE, two tabs, ACCESSORIES (Pink) and BACKDROPS (Blue), the item button or left and right changing between them. The accessories are a grid eight wide of their signs (R17a's `AccessoryIcon`) with how many of each; the backdrops a list of eight rows, each a swatch of the backdrop and its name. On the right the thing under the cursor, large (a backdrop as a picture across the panel, 0.72 as tall as wide), its name, a tag of which of how many, and under a rule the contests' records: ENTERED, WON, RIBBONS, captions over numbers.
  - **A backdrop** (`BackdropArt`): a scene of our own for each of the eighteen, by its name: a sky or a wall in two tones and a few plain shapes (a stage's curtains and boards, a town's roofs under snow, a ringed planet among stars, clouds, dunes and a sun, a meadow's flowers, a grid of light, a sea with the sun on it, a dark with one faint rise). Small motifs scale with the picture, so the swatch reads as the large one does.
  - **A photograph** (`DrawContestPhoto`): titled CONTEST PHOTO. A cream print 1040 wide over the screen's backdrop, its picture (640 tall) inside a dark line: the Theater backdrop (red curtains at both sides, a cone of light, boards) under a wash of the contest's colour, the winner's menu sprite at 4×, the accessories it wore round it, and its ribbon's rosette in the top right corner. Under the picture the Pokémon's name at 52, the contest and rank in Muted at 32, and at the right the trainer's name and the day.
- **Pokédex** (`PokedexScreen`, plan 03 · D10): the list on the left, the entry on the right.
  - **The list**: the open Pokédex's name on a tag (SINNOH in Blue, KANTO in Green, NATIONAL in Red; a Pokédex a later region brings takes Gold until its plan gives it a colour of its own, plan 18 · W3) beside its SEEN and CAUGHT counts, which count that Pokédex's species only. A row has the number in that Pokédex, the name (dashes until it has been seen), the menu icon, and a small ball for a species that has been caught. Left and right jump ten. Any of the four keys held down runs on through the list, a species or ten at a time, and up and down held in an entry run through the species seen.
  - **The entry**: three pages chosen with tabs across the panel's top, INFO (Blue), AREA (Green) and SIZE (Gold). The A button moves into the entry: left and right then change page, up and down go to the previous or next species seen, and B goes back to the list, which shows INFO again.
  - **INFO**: a species that has been caught shows everything: its sprite at 3× on a disc, number, name, category, types, height, weight, habitat and its entry. One only seen shows its sprite, name and types, with question marks for the rest. One not yet seen is a question mark on the disc.
    - **Height, weight and habitat** (plan 08 · P13) are three captions over values in Black 34, 44 apart, under the types: HEIGHT, WEIGHT and HABITAT, the last one of Platinum's nine kinds of place in words (Grassland, Forest, Water's edge, Sea, Cave, Mountain, Rough terrain, Urban, Rare). Where the habitat's word wouldn't fit before the panel's inner edge it is set smaller (never under 22). A species the entries file lacks has no HABITAT, and HEIGHT and WEIGHT stand as they did, 64 apart.
    - **The written entry** (`Data/dex-entries.json`, plan 08 · P13) is Bold 30 on 42 leading under the rule, in Ink. A species has two, shown in turn: the first until its entry has been opened, then the next at each opening (A from the list, or a step up or down to it within an entry), round its two, for as long as the game runs. The rule ends early on the right to make room for a dot for each entry (8 in radius, 26 apart), the one shown in Blue and the other in the Rule colour. A species the file lacks shows the sentence generated from the data and no dots. The starters' briefcase shows the first entry of each.
  - **AREA**: the map of the Pokédex's region (the listing's own, and for the National listing the region the player stands in, as Platinum shows Sinnoh for everything; a region with no habitats file says "Area unknown." for every species) as the Town Map paints it (below: the half-size picture, smoothed, 20 units to a chunk, in its dark frame), with the cells the species lives in lit in the Selection colour under a white edge, breathing gently. Beside the map, its places in the order of the region, each with how it is met under its name: grass at which times of day, surfing, and with which rods. A species found nowhere says "Area unknown." Swarms, the Poké Radar and the species a second game in the console calls up are left out, as Platinum leaves them out.
  - **SIZE**: the species and the player as two dark silhouettes (black at 84 %) standing on one line at the heights the Pokédex gives (the player is 1.4 m), the taller of the two 520 tall. They are the one exception to whole-number scales: flat shapes scaled to whatever their heights ask for. Only for species caught, whose height the Pokédex knows.
  - **Search** (the Start button): a panel over the dimmed Pokédex with rows like the options' (a value on a pill between two arrows): which Pokédex (once the player has more than one: the regional Pokédex of each region entered, then the National one once it is open), the order (number, A to Z, heaviest, lightest, tallest, smallest), the first letter (A B C, D E F … Y Z; a language with its own groups offers those, plan 17 · N3: the kana rows for Japanese, the initial consonants for Korean, a name filed by the letter it sounds from), two types and a body shape (the Pokédex's fourteen), then SEARCH (Green) and RESET (Blue), and DIPLOMA (Gold) once one has been given. The results replace the list, with how many were found on a tag; B goes back to the whole list. As in Platinum it looks only among the species seen, and the orders by weight and height only among those caught; those orders show the weight or the height at each row's right.
  - **Diploma**: a cream certificate with a gold double rule over the dimmed screen: DIPLOMA, which Pokédex (SINNOH POKÉDEX, KANTO POKÉDEX, NATIONAL POKÉDEX), the player's name, a line on what was done (in our own words), the day, and a gold seal. It is shown the first time the Pokédex is opened once it is complete, and again from the search panel.
- **The party's menus** (plan 02 · S2): choosing a Pokémon on the party's cards opens its menu, a panel 400 wide standing on the prompt over the half of the screen its card isn't on, with a row of 70 for each entry (SUMMARY, its field moves in the order of its moves, SWITCH, CANCEL) like the bag's actions. The prompt then says "Do what with this Pokémon?", and any word the menu has (why a move can't be used here, what Soft-Boiled healed) takes the prompt's place until the next button. While Milk Drink or Soft-Boiled waits for a Pokémon to give HP to, the giver's card keeps the gold of a Pokémon being moved.
- **Choosing one of a species** (plan 14 · B14, `choosepokemon "Bidoof"`): the team's own screen in its choosing mode, as entering a team for the Battle Frontier is (`ModernUi.DrawPartyChoice`, the confirm hint CHOOSE). A Pokémon of the species named carries no tag and stays bright; every other card, an Egg among them, carries the grey NOT ABLE where a field move's tag would be and is laid over with a dim film (40 40 60 at 90 of 255, the card's radius), as a fainted card is greyed. A on a dimmed card plays the error sound and puts the refusal in the prompt at the foot ("Only Bidoof can be chosen here.") with nothing chosen. A choice with no species named is the screen as it was.
- **Pokérus** (plan 06 · R10): while a Pokémon carries it, its party card and its summary show a PKRS tag after its status, in a colour of its own that no status or type uses (a deep teal, 16 132 124; a plum read as the poison status beside it). A Pokémon over it shows nothing; the original's small face for a cured one is left out.
- **The Town Map** (`TownMapScreen`, `ModernUi.TownMap.cs`, plan 01 · M12): one screen for the Town Map out of the bag, for Fly's choice (it replaced `FlyScreen`) and for a map on a wall, titled `TOWN MAP`, `FLY` or `MAP`.
  - **The picture** (`RegionMapArt`, painted once and kept by `RegionMapPictures`): the region painted from the world's own tiles, a texel a tile (Sinnoh's is 960 by 768: columns 0 to 29 of the grid, rows 5 to 28), in a palette of its own, with nothing of the original's picture in it. A tile is the colour of what it is: lawn `134,192,106`, tall grass `102,166,88`, a path `228,208,152`, sand `240,226,174`, snow `232,240,248`, paving `200,204,214`, planks `178,134,86`; water in three depths by how far it is from the shore (`94,150,214`, `70,124,198`, `56,104,180`) with a pale line `150,194,234` along the shore itself; forest in pine `50,108,72` or leaf `70,128,62`, a darker and a lighter texel scattered through it, and frosted `104,148,136` in snow country; a mountain in bands of rock from `126,106,90` at its foot to `192,174,152`, capped with snow `230,236,246`, or in bands of ash with a dark crown and embers round a volcano; a building its roof over one row of wall `244,236,214` (a Pokémon Center's red `226,84,78`, a Mart's blue `70,128,220`, a Gym's gold `236,172,60`, a house's by its tiles). Where the world has only the forest, rock and sea that fill the space between its places, and where it has nothing at all, the land's outline is made up: a cell is sea where open water or the map's edge can be come to from it and land otherwise, and every border between two such cells is bent by noise, so no coast follows a chunk's edge. A hidden place is left off (open water, or the land round it) until the story reveals it, and the picture is painted again when it does.
  - **In its frame** (`ModernUi.RegionPicture`): the picture stands in a dark frame `36,44,68`, 8 units wide with round corners. It is drawn point-filtered where a texel is a unit or more, and from its half-size mean, smoothed, where the map is shown smaller. Everything that shows the region draws this: the Town Map, Fly, the Pokédex's area page.
  - **The screen**: the map's panel on the left (1024 by 832, the picture a unit to a tile), the place under the cursor on the right, a strip 56 tall under the map. The cursor is four corners round its cell, white under the Selection colour, which spread as it lands and settle within 0.14 s; it moves a cell at a time within the original's bounds (columns 1 to 28, rows 6 to 28) and a held key repeats. The player is a red pin, a ring widening from its foot once a second. Fly's places are discs of 9 units with a white rim, Gold where the town has been visited and Muted where not. The way come is a white dot with a dark rim on each of the last five cells passed through, one of them in turn larger and with an arrow the way the player faced there (26 thirtieths of a second each, the original's pace); a wall's map has none.
  - **The place**: a tag (ROUTE Green, TOWN or CITY Gold, LANDMARK Blue), beside it VISITED or NOT YET VISITED on one of Fly's places (FLY HERE in the Selection colour when choosing), the name in Black 52 (42 where 52 doesn't fit), a rule, and the place's words in Bold 30 on 42 leading, or "You haven't been here yet." in Muted where the original keeps them back. A landmark in the cell follows once it has been found: a blue disc, its name in Black 36, its words. At the panel's foot a window 256 tall shows the cursor's cell from near, four units to a tile, with white corners round the cell, and under a rule YOU ARE IN with the name of the player's place (indoors, the place the room was come into from).
  - **Zoomed in** (Confirm; the Menu key when choosing where to fly, where Confirm flies): a third of the map at three units to a tile, following the cursor and eased. The window at the foot then shows the whole map small, a white frame round the part on the left, under WHERE THIS IS.
  - **Under the map**: what the marks mean (You, Visited, Not yet visited, The way you came), or "Fly to where?". When choosing, the cells of an open place under the cursor are lit in the Selection colour, breathing; a place that isn't open answers Confirm with the error sound and the map stays.
- **Trainer Card** (`TrainerCardScreen`, plan 06 · R12): one wide card that rises into place, in the colour of what the player has done (`TrainerCardRules.CardColour`, `ModernUi.CardColor`; our own palette, never the games'): slate without a Pokédex, then teal, cobalt, bronze, silver, gold and black. The colour is the band across the card's top and the card's edge (a frame 6 wide in the colour darkened, with a thin line of it lightened inside); the paper stays the panels' white. The band carries the name, the five stars (each done a gold star with a dark edge, the rest in the band's colour lightened, opaque so their overlapping parts don't show) and the ID number.
  - **The front**: money, time played, the Pokédex's caught and seen counts and the score on the left (captions over values, two to a row), the player's own field sprite at 6× on a pale plate (touched with the card's colour) on the right, and along the bottom the badges of the region the player is in (plan 18 · W2: its own count, none to eight, from its file) at radius 44 with their names, at the eight places' spacing from the left, so a region of eight fills the row as Sinnoh's always has. The caption says how many of how many (BADGES 3 / 8). Once the player has been in a second region, the region's name in capitals stands in Muted at the caption's right end; with one region, nothing does, and the front is as it was.
  - **A page per region** (plan 18 · W2, decision 1; `CardPage.Region`): once the player has been in two regions or more (their Pokédex entered, a badge won there, or standing there now), left and right go through the card's pages: the front, the back, then a page for each region in the journey's order. A page turns like the back (0.3 s narrowing to the edge). Its top, under the band, is the region's name in Black 64 with the region's badges counted beside it in Muted (3 / 8) and CHAMPION in a gold pill once its Hall of Fame is entered; then the region's medallions in a row at radius 56, on the front's spacing scaled to fit, with their names in Black 24 under them (Muted until won); a region with no badges says so in a line of Muted. Under a rule, once the player has the region's own Pokédex (plan 18 · W3), how many of its species are seen and caught, captions over values as on the front (SEEN IN ITS POKÉDEX 24 / 151). The hints add "← → PAGE" only while there are such pages.
  - **The back** (the A button turns the card over: in 0.3 s it narrows to its edge and opens on the other side): a ledger of five rows 88 apart, each caption on the left and its value right-aligned in Black 40, with a rule between rows: the Hall of Fame debut (the day and the time, or a dash before it), link battles won and lost, link trades, the Super Contests won of those entered (plan 06 · R17b), and the day the adventure began. The stars again at its foot with how many of five. Platinum's signature is left out.
- **PC boxes** (`PCScreen`, plan 06 · R12): three columns. The party as six compact cards on the left; the box in the middle; on the right the Pokémon under the cursor, or the one being carried (sprite at 2× on a disc, its six marks in a row, name, level, types, HP, nature, moves, where and when it was met, what it holds).
  - **The box**: its panel is lined with the box's wallpaper (below), inside a white rim of 10. The box's own name sits on a pale plate between two arrows at its top, under the plate is what says which box this is (for Platinum's eighteen boxes a row of eighteen small dots 20 apart, the current one in the Selection colour; for a PC with more, the hundred that come with the National Pokédex (plan 03 · D13), a pale pill 560 wide holding a thin track 400 long with a small tick at every tenth box, a knob in the Selection colour where this box stands along it, and at its right end the box's number and how many there are, "35 / 100"), and its thirty places are 6 by 5 plates of translucent white (a white edge, the wallpaper showing through) with each Pokémon as its menu icon at 2×. Under the places, how many the box holds and the wallpaper's name, each on a Frame-coloured tag. The cursor moves from the party into the box and back, and up from the box's top row to its name, where left and right change box (a press goes round from the last box to the first; a key kept down runs on through the boxes and stops at either end); the place under the cursor has the Selection edge and glow.
  - **Carrying** (MOVE): the Pokémon picked up goes with the cursor, its icon at 2× lifted 46 above the place under the cursor and bobbing gently, a soft shadow on the place under it.
  - **Menus**: A on a Pokémon opens what can be done with it (MOVE, WITHDRAW or STORE, MARK, RELEASE, CANCEL), a panel 340 wide with a row of 70 to an entry like the party's menu, beside the place or the party card on the side with more room; A on the box's name opens NAME, WALLPAPER and CANCEL under the plate. WALLPAPER puts up the box's next wallpaper at once.
  - **Marks** (MARK): a panel beside the place with the six marks (circle, triangle, square, heart, star, diamond, drawn with `UiShapes`, `ModernUi.MarkShape`) on keys like the keyboard's, then OK. A mark that is set is Ink on a key tinted with the Selection colour, one that isn't is faint; the cursor is a Selection ring. Marks shown elsewhere (the detail panel) are Ink when set and the Rule colour when not.
  - **Release** (RELEASE): asked over the dimmed screen ("Release Starly?"), RELEASE in Red and KEEP IT in Blue, each naming its key, since the question has no cursor.
  - **Naming** (NAME): the introduction's keyboard, with eight slots, and on the left in place of a portrait the box's wallpaper on a plate with its name over it.
- **Box wallpapers** (`BoxWallpapers`): Platinum's twenty-four in its order (Forest, City, Desert, Savanna, Crag, Volcano, Snow, Cave, Beach, Seafloor, River, Sky, Poké Center, Machine, Checks, Simple; then Distortion, Contest, Nostalgic, Croagunk, Trio, PikaPika, Legend and Team Galactic, which a word game unlocks), each painted in code as a soft gradient with one simple motif of our own: fir trees, a skyline, dunes, a flat-crowned tree against the sun, angular rocks, a cone and its smoke, snowflakes and drifts, stalactites, sea and sand, bubbles and weed, a winding river, clouds, rounded crosses, circuit lines, a checkerboard, a plain inner line; rings that waver, rosettes, blocky four-tone squares, orange discs on blue, three overlapping discs in yellow, pink and blue, zigzags and red dots, a diamond, a pearl and a hexagon, orbits and stars. Never the games' pictures. They are painted at half the layout's size (`BoxWallpapers.Paint`) and drawn smoothed, and they are quiet so that the icons read on all of them: no pixel darker than 0.4 in lightness (its sRGB luma), and the darkest and the lightest of one wallpaper within 0.36 of each other (`MenuScreenTests`).
- **Hall of Fame** (`HallOfFameScreen`, plan 06 · R12): one panel. Its head is the team's number on a Gold tag, the day it entered, and on the right which of the teams kept this is. Under it the team as six cards in a row (sprite at 2× on a disc, nickname, level; an empty place a pale slot), the chosen one with a Gold edge and glow and hopping. Under the cards two tabs, PROFILE and MOVES (the A button turns between them): the nickname large with its gender mark and a gold star for a shiny one, then species, level, original trainer and ID No. as captions over values, and the species' types on pills; or its four moves on pills of their types' colours, two by two.
- **Journal** (`JournalScreen`, plan 06 · R12): a page of a notebook on the backdrop: cream paper (252 248 236 at the top to 244 237 218), ruled in pale blue every 70, a soft red margin line, and the spiral's rings down its left edge. Its day at the top (the weekday in small capitals over the date in Black 48), then the page's lines in Ink, each on its rule, a long one carried on to the next. The day's Pokémon is a photograph pinned at the top right: its sprite at 2× on a white card held by a strip of tape, its name under it (pixel art is never turned). At the foot, the page's number of how many there are between arrows that show only where there is another page (older to the left).
- **Starter choice** (`StarterSelectScreen`): the three partners as three cards side by side, each with its sprite at 3× on a disc, name, category and type; the chosen one hops. A panel under them has the chosen one's entry. Choosing asks first ("Choose Turtwig?"), with going back as the first answer.
- **Saving** (`SaveScreen`): over the dimmed field, the save as it will be (the same summary the title's CONTINUE card shows: place, player, time played, Pokédex, badges, party) with the question under it. SAVE is the first answer and the one highlighted, since nothing is lost by saving. Afterwards the panel says so for a moment and goes. The panel names the slot the game is saved in (plan 12 · Q2), which is the one it was continued from or the empty one a new game took: as a small tag after the heading, as on the title's card, and in the question ("Save the game in slot 2?").
- **Options** (`OptionsScreen`): one card per setting, 78 tall on a pitch of 86 (nine rows fill the screen's height), with its value on a pill between two arrows; a panel under them, filling what is left of the screen, explains the chosen one. The first row is the language (plan 17 · N1): its value pill shows the language's own name for itself (English, Français), and only the languages whose script the interface's faces can draw are offered (Latin until plan 17 · N5), never the pseudo-language that brackets every string for the tests. The second row is the text speed (slow, normal, fast: 24, 45 and 120 characters a second, whatever the frame rate). The last three are the sound: on or off (the M key too), then the music's volume and the sound effects', 0 to 100 in steps of 10, shown as a percentage, which stop at their ends rather than wrapping round. A setting the newest games made their own default (plan 12) is ON or OFF, ON unless chosen: since plan 06 · R19 every game is played by the modern rules, so the RULES it once had between the two is gone, and an older settings file's RULES reads as ON. The last row is HELP, whose value is OPEN: the A button opens the help pages. Just before it come the two rows of plan 16 · T3: **Pause in the background** (OFF or ON, off unless chosen: on, the game waits where it is and its sound drops to a quarter while another window is in front, and goes on when the player comes back) and **Frame limit** (30 FPS, 60 FPS, 120 FPS or NO LIMIT, 60 unless chosen: the most frames drawn a second and nothing more, beside V-Sync, which still holds the game to the display's refresh rate when it is on).
- **Help** (`HelpScreen`, plan 12 · Q10): three tabs, TYPES (Red), CONTROLS (Blue) and NOTES (Green). TYPES is the type chart as a grid on a panel at the left: the attacking types down its side as type pills 150 wide, the defending ones across its top as pills of their three-letter short names, and in each cell the matchup as a signed pill 30 tall, the sign as the battle's hint pills have it with ×2 (Green), ×½ (Gold) or ×0 (Muted), a plain matchup left as a faint dot. The cursor's cell is ringed in the Selection colour, its row and column lit faintly, and a panel at the right says it in words ("Fire against Grass: super effective, twice the damage") with the three pills explained under it. The chart is the one the game is played by (`TypeChart.GetEffectiveness` under the game's rules). CONTROLS lists every action as a list row with its keys and the pad's button on Frame-coloured key pills. NOTES is a few short paragraphs of our own on what helps a new player (running, the Pokétch, saving, the options), in Bold 30 on 42 leading. On the chart the arrows move the cursor, and up from its top row goes to the tabs; on the tabs (and on the other two pages) left and right change tab and down goes into the page. B goes back to the options.

### The new-game introduction (`IntroScreen`)

The games' opening talk, in our own words and with our own pictures: Professor Rowan in front of a soft backdrop, speaking in the dialogue panel.

1. Black, then the backdrop fades up: deep teal to night blue with slow drifting discs of light, and Rowan (his 3D model, as battles show a trainer, breathing and blinking) in the middle.
2. He welcomes the player and explains the world. Then he steps aside, a Poké Ball (the 3D one of the battles) comes up and opens in white light, and a Pokémon grows out of the light, hops and stands beside him: Buneary, as in Platinum (the 3D model, as in battle).
3. **Who are you?** Two cards, a boy and a girl (the two player characters as 3D models); the chosen one stands a little forward and waves. Choosing asks whether that is right ("So you're a girl?").
4. **Your name?** The name-entry screen: the character's field sprite at 6×, the name so far as large letters on a row of seven slots, and the keyboard. Seven letters at most, as in Platinum; an empty name takes the default (Lucas or Dawn).
5. He repeats the name and asks whether it is right. In both of his questions YES comes first and going back second: nothing is lost either way, and yes is what is nearly always meant.
6. The send-off: the picture closes to black round the player's own field sprite, which shrinks away in whole steps on a small pool of light (`UiShapes.Glow`), and the field fades in.

Any line can be hurried with the A button; nothing can be skipped, because each step sets something.

Whoever the player isn't is the professor's assistant, with the other default name. Written lines and place names never name the player outright: they say `{player}` and `{assistant}`, which `PlayerIdentity.Fill` replaces when they are shown.

### Pokémon in menus

- Always **2D pixel sprites** baked from the models (`PokemonSprites`): 48-px icons and 128-px front/back sprites with a one-texel outline, the eyes and markings baked in with them.
- Shown at whole-number scales only (party icons at 4×), point-filtered.
- Icons hop like the main games: the selected one 3 sprite-pixels every 0.16 s, the others 1 sprite-pixel every 0.4 s; fainted ones stay still.

### Eggs

An Egg (plan 06 · R15) is our own: a cream shell (246, 236, 210) shaded in a crescent along its lower right (222, 206, 172), whose edge follows the outline, five mint-green spots (104, 178, 136) and a white shine on its upper left, an egg's shape (an ellipse narrowed toward its top), standing on the floor of its canvas.

- In menus it is pixel art like the baked sprites (`PixelArtGenerator.EggArt`): a 48-px icon and a 128-px sprite with the same one-texel outline, at the same whole-number scales. It rocks once every 0.8 s by one sprite-pixel instead of hopping, and is never dimmed for being unable to fight.
- On the team's card it shows its name, Egg, with no gender, level, types or HP, and a muted "Waiting to hatch". In battle's lists it shows its name alone. Its summary has the left panel (the Egg on its disc, "A Pokémon Egg", who it was received from and on what day) and on the right THE EGG WATCH: one of four sentences by the cycles left (five or fewer, ten, forty, more). The PC's detail column shows the same sentence under its name.
- The Pokétch's apps show its icon in the LCD's tones, with no HP bar.

### Hatching

An Egg hatches on the evolution scene's stage (`EvolutionStage`): the dark hall, its pool of light and the slow rays, the glow rising as the Egg breaks.

- The Egg is drawn large and smooth (`ModernUi.HatchEgg`): the menus' shell, spots and shine as vector shapes, 400 units tall, standing on the pool of light with a soft shadow under it, outlined in ink.
- It rocks three times about its foot over 3.2 s, each swing quick and dying away, each stronger than the last (0.10, 0.16, 0.22 radians), with the ball's wobble sound as each comes back to rest.
- Then for 1.5 s it shakes fast while ink cracks run across its upper half from left to right, and a second crack branches up from the middle; it swells a little and turns white at the end.
- A white flash fills the screen as it breaks (0.55 s): the Pokémon is there, drawn as the evolution scene draws its new shape, in white, which drains from it over 1.2 s as it cries.
- "X hatched from the Egg!" in the dialogue panel, with the fanfare of a Pokémon received. No nickname is asked, as none is when one is caught.

### The Hall of Fame's ceremony (`HallOfFameCeremony`)

Plan 02 · S14. The team's entry into the Hall of Fame is a scene of its own, on the beats of the original's (each Pokémon in turn, then the Champion among them) and wholly our own to look at.

- **The stage** (`ModernUi.CeremonyStage`) is the evolution scene's dark hall in the Hall of Fame's colours: night blue `10,12,34` at the top falling to a deep wine `58,18,34` at the foot, a pool of warm light on the floor (gold `255,214,130`, never white), and two spotlights from the top corners that sweep slowly across the picture and cross over whoever stands on the pool. No rays turn behind the figure: this is a hall, not a change.
- **Each Pokémon in turn**: its model, 640 units across, slides in from the left edge onto the pool, left of the middle, over 0.6 s (ease out), and cries as it comes to rest. Its card slides in from the right at the same time (`Panel`, 760 by 470): a caption in gold capitals, "WELCOME TO THE HALL OF FAME", which is on the card as it comes; its name at 64 with its gender's mark and its level at the card's right; its species and its types' pills; under a rule, who first trained it with their ID number, and where and at what level it was met. The card's three parts appear 0.4 s apart. A small count ("2 / 6") sits in the card's lower right. After 3.4 s, or at the button once the card is whole, both slide out the way they came (0.45 s) and the next comes.
- **The Champion among them**: the player's own figure (dressed as they are, 600 units tall) steps onto the pool in the middle; the team comes in from both edges of the picture to stand either side, the first on the right, the second on the left and so outward, each 300 units across and 0.18 s after the one before. Above them "LEAGUE CHAMPION" in the title's display lettering, gold `255,236,170` to `226,160,60`, with "Congratulations!" under it in cream; below them a dark pill with the Champion's name, ID number and the time played. Then slips of paper fall for as long as the picture stays: ninety, in gold, red, blue, green and cream, six units by fourteen, each turning as it falls at 140 to 260 units a second with a slow sway. Nothing is read from chance: a slip's place is a function of its number and the clock.
- The picture waits for the button (the red arrow, after three seconds), then closes to black over a second.
- **Don't** show stats, moves or a level bar: the card says who the Pokémon is and where it came from, as the original's does.

### The credits (`CreditsScreen`)

Plan 02 · S14. This remake's own credits: its sources and its thanks, in its own words (`Data/credits.json`; nothing of the original's roll, its scenes or its list of names).

- **The backdrop** (`ModernUi.CreditsBackdrop`) is the same night hall without the wine: `8,10,30` to `22,30,78`, with slow motes of soft light drifting upward (forty, blue-white, 0.06 to 0.16 alpha). It is quiet on purpose: text is the subject.
- **The title card** opens the roll: the title screen's own lettering ("Pokémon" in cream over "PLATINUM" in the silver gradient, with its rule and "A FAN REMAKE"), centred, fading in over 1.2 s, held, and fading out over 0.8 s; five seconds in all.
- **The roll** is one column, 860 units wide, centred at 0.64 of the picture's width. A section's heading is gold `240,176,56` capitals at 28, Black, with a short rule under it; a name is white at 38, ExtraBold; a note under a name is pale blue `178,196,236` at 24, Bold, wrapped to the column in lines of about the same width (`ModernUi.WrapEvenly`: never one word under a full line). Lines stand 52 apart (a note's lines 34), sections 110. It climbs at 64 units a second, four times as fast while the button is held, and fades over the last 150 units at the top and at the foot.
- **The team walks the player through it**: on the left, at 0.25 of the width, the team's Pokémon stand in turn on a pool of light, each for an equal share of the roll, as 3D models 520 units across, one fading into the next over 0.8 s, with its name on a dark pill under it. With no team (a tool's run) the pool is left empty.
- **The last card**: "THE END" in the display lettering, silver, with "Thank you for playing." under it in cream, fading in over 1.2 s. It waits for the button (the red arrow after 1.5 s) and goes on by itself after thirty seconds; then black over a second.
- A character outside Latin-1 never appears in the file (`UiFonts`), and a long dash is never typed: a middle dot separates.

### The Underground

Plan 06 · R16a. Everything below ground is our own: nothing of the original's lower-screen art, its trap pictures or its goods' models is taken.
